AI Podcast › DHH › 本期

从怀疑到拥抱：DHH 谈 AI 革命

From Skeptic to Believer: DHH on the AI Revolution

DHH DHH · Lex Fridman 播客 · 2026-08-26 · 约 316 分钟 · 原视频 ↗

打开互动全文版（中英对照 + 朗读 + 问答）→

本期速览 · Overview

DHH 讲述他从 AI 怀疑者到热情拥抱者的转变，称代理工程领域的飞速进展是千载难逢的时代变革。

DHH discusses his transformation from AI skeptic to enthusiastic adopter, describing the rapid progress in agentic engineering as a once-in-a-lifetime epoch shift.

要点 · TL;DR

  • DHH 的 Linux 发行版中 100% 的代码由 AI 代理编写，标志着编程的根本性转变。
    DHH now has 100% of code in his Linux distro written by AI agents, marking a fundamental shift in programming.
  • 绕过管理层，直接与 AI 代理交互是提升生产力的关键。
    Direct human-agent interaction, bypassing management layers, is key to productivity gains.
  • AI 代理擅长创造性解决问题，并能产生新颖的想法，而不仅仅是复述已有的内容。
    Agents excel at creative problem-solving and can generate novel ideas, not just regurgitate existing ones.

核心观点 · Key points

  • AI 智能体已从根本上改变了编程，DHH 在他的 Linux 发行版项目中已实现 100% 的代码由智能体编写。
    AI agents have fundamentally changed programming, with DHH now having 100% of code written by agents in his Linux distribution project.
  • 生产力提升的关键在于人与智能体的直接交互，绕过层层人类管理和审批。
    The key to productivity gains is direct human-agent interaction, bypassing layers of human management and approval.
  • 智能体擅长创造性解决问题，能产生新颖的想法，而不仅仅是复述已有的内容。
    Agents excel at creative problem-solving and can generate novel ideas, not just regurgitate existing ones.
  • 软件开发的未来在于描述结果和问题，而不是规定实现细节。
    The future of software development lies in describing outcomes and problems, not prescribing implementation details.
  • 开源维护者受益于智能体生成的贡献，这些贡献通常质量更高且更容易拒绝。
    Open source maintainers benefit from agent-generated contributions, which are often higher quality and easier to reject.
  • 智能体时代需要一个可塑且可配置的新操作系统，而 Linux 独特地提供了这一点。
    The agentic era requires a new operating system that is malleable and configurable, which Linux uniquely provides.

反共识 · Contrarian takes

  • 在智能体时代，懂编程可能是一种劣势，因为它会导致过度规定解决方案。
    Knowing how to program can be a disadvantage in the agentic era, as it leads to over-prescribing solutions.
  • 智能体在产生创造性想法方面可能比人类更好，挑战了总是需要人类输入的观点。
    Agents can be better than humans at generating creative ideas, challenging the assumption that human input is always needed.
  • 对卓越的追求，即使在安装时间等看似琐碎的指标上，也能推动创新和发现。
    The pursuit of excellence, even in seemingly frivolous metrics like install time, drives innovation and discovery.
  • Linux 历史上的缺点（配置文件、命令行）现在是其在智能体时代的主要优势。
    Linux's historical drawbacks (config files, CLI) are now its major advantages in the agentic age.
  • 在某些情况下，没有编程知识的氛围编码可能比传统编程更有效。
    Vibe coding without programming knowledge can be more effective than traditional programming in certain contexts.
  • 手写代码的浪漫化已经在发生，但那是怀旧的过去，而不是未来。
    The romanticization of handwritten code is already happening, but it's a nostalgic past, not the future.

开场与介绍 Opening and Introduction

Host

有些年代里什么都没发生，有些星期里却发生了十年的变化。而我们在过去九个月里，见证了十年的进步。如果你没有意识到这一刻的严重性，那才是错觉，那才是疯狂。如果突然有个精灵从瓶子里蹦出来说：“你想要什么都可以。你在操作系统里梦想过的每一个功能，我都能给你，大部分五分钟内，少数二十分钟，如果我们真的放开手脚，那得花我两个小时。”谁会不欣喜若狂呢？我想要最快的车，突破速度极限。我想要能潜到马里亚纳海沟的潜水表。我想要能在 60 秒内装好的操作系统。我一直喜欢赛车的一点是，当我从车里跌跌撞撞出来，浑身散架，几乎抬不起头，躺在车库地板上，心里想：“天哪，我还活着。”奥弗顿之窗不会自己打开。它是一点一点被人撬开的，靠的是那些冒一点险的人。一点名声，一点反弹，一点批评，或者有时候是很多名声，很多批评，很多反弹。和你爱的人一起创造生命，绝对是活在这个星球上的巅峰体验。——以下是和大卫·海涅迈尔·汉森（又名 DHH）的对话，他是 Ruby on Rails 的创造者、37signals 的 CTO、新 Omarchy Linux 操作系统的创造者、畅销书作家、赛车手，也是世界上最大胆、最多产的程序员之一。过去 20 多年里，对他来说，编程意味着精心手工打磨优美的 Ruby 代码。但最近，从 2025 年底开始，DHH 拥抱了 AI 革命，公开转变成了——再一次——最大胆、最多产的智能体式工程师之一，尽管他讨厌这个术语。所以我们不妨称他为“无论编程正在变成什么形态的实践者”，在这种形态里，AI 做大部分实际编程，人类用高层设计、愿景和品味掌舵。是的，没错，DHH 有时充满争议，但他总是无畏、才华横溢，而且聊起来很有趣。这又是一次激烈、大开眼界、疯狂的过山车式对话。这里是 Lex Fridman 播客。为了支持我们，请查看描述里的赞助商，那里也有联系我、提问、反馈的链接。现在，亲爱的朋友们，有请 DHH。

There are decades where nothing happens and weeks where decades happen. And we have seen decades of progress happen in the last nine months. If you're not recognizing the gravity of the moment, that's the delusion. That's the psychosis. Who would not get delirious if suddenly a genie pops out of the bottle and says, "You can have whatever you want. Every feature you've ever dreamed of in an operating system, I can deliver them to you, most of them in five minutes, a few in 20, and if we really go hog wild, it's gonna take me two hours." I want the fastest car breaking the speed limits. I want the diver watch that can go down the Mariana Trench. I want the operating system that can install in less than 60 seconds. One of the things I always loved about race cars was when I would stumble out of the car absolutely smashed and barely able to hold my head up, and I'd lay down on the garage floor and just think, "Holy fuck, I'm alive." The Overton window does not open itself. It opens one nudge at a time by people risking a little. A little reputation, a little pushback, a little criticism, or maybe sometimes a lot of reputation or a lot of criticism or a lot of pushback. Creating life with another human that you love is literally the peak experience of being on the planet. - The following is a conversation with David Heinemeier Hansson, also known as DHH, creator of Ruby on Rails, CTO of 37signals, creator of the new Omarchy Linux operating system, best-selling author, race car driver, and one of the most outspoken and prolific programmers in the world. For the past 20-plus years, for him, programming meant meticulously handcrafting beautiful Ruby code. But recently, since late 2025, DHH has embraced the AI revolution and has openly transformed himself into, once again, one of the most outspoken and prolific agentic engineers, even though he hates that term. So let's say practitioners of whatever programming is becoming where AI is doing most of the actual programming and the human steers the ship with high-level design, vision, and taste. Yes, it's true. DHH is at times controversial, but he's always fearless, brilliant, and fun to talk to. This was, once again, an intense, eye-opening, and wild rollercoaster ride of a conversation. This is a Lex Fridman podcast. To support it, please check out our sponsors in the description where you can also find links to contact me, ask questions, give feedback, and so on. And now, dear friends, here's DHH.

AI在编程中角色的转变 Change in AI's Role in Programming

Host

13 个月前，我们坐在这里聊编程，然后一切都变了。那时候，你对 AI 在编程过程中的作用还有点怀疑，然后我们经历了智能体式工程的快速演进。所以，先问个简单的问题：你对 AI 在编程中作用的看法发生了怎样的变化？你兴奋吗？害怕吗？是不是在情绪上坐过山车？

13 months ago, we sat down right here to talk about programming, and then everything changed. At that time, you were a bit skeptical about the role of AI in the process of programming, and then we went through this rapid evolution of agentic engineering. So, simple question to start: How has your view on AI's role in programming changed? Are you excited? Are you terrified? Are you on an emotional rollercoaster ride?

DHH

我极其兴奋。

I am incredibly excited.

Host

你一直在说“好玩”。

You've been talking about fun a lot.

DHH

完全没有那种生存威胁。对我来说，那不是一个情绪成分。它只存在于智识层面，而我的情绪层面是 100% 纯粹、不掺假的喜悦、乐观和惊叹——我们竟然让计算机做到了这个。我觉得特别有意思的是，我们 13 个月前才聊过，但感觉就像在不同的宇宙、不同的时代里聊的。我喜欢这句话，我记得是列宁说的：有些年代里什么都没发生，有些星期里却发生了十年的变化。而我们在过去九个月里，见证了十年的进步。想象一下，你在莱特兄弟起飞的那一刻。昨天《纽约时报》还写“离飞行还要一万年”，第二天我们就上了天，几年之后就有了跨大西洋的飞机。整个世界彻底变了。能身处那一刻是多么幸运。如果你把视野拉远，看整个人类历史，有多少人活在了自己出生的那个时代里？他们从没见过世界和社会的彻底改变。而我有幸经历了两次这样的改变，真是莫大的荣幸。我见证了从互联网前到互联网后，然后又从 AI 前到 AI 后。多么精彩的旅程，多么幸运。

There is none of the existential threat. That doesn't exist for me as an emotional component. It is only there as an intellectual component, and the emotional component for me is 100% pure, unadulterated joy and optimism and amazement that we've made computers do this. And I find it so interesting that we talked just 13 months ago because it's like we talked in different universes, different eras. I love this quote. I think it's Lenin. There are decades where nothing happens and weeks where decades happen. And we have seen decades of progress happen in the last nine months. I mean, imagine you're there when the Wright brothers take flight. Yesterday, the New York Times would write, "It's gonna be 10,000 years before we fly," and then the day after, we're up in the skies, and just a few years after that, there were cross-Atlantic planes. The whole world has completely changed. What a blessing to be there in that moment. If you zoom out and look at all of human history, how many humans got to live within the same epoch that they were born in? They never saw that complete change of the world and of society. And to have been blessed with two of those feels just such a privilege. I got to see the internet from pre-internet to post-internet, and then now pre-AI, post-AI. What an amazing run. How fortunate.

Host

是啊，但问题是，这件事感觉比人类历史上任何事都快。

Yeah, but the thing is, this feels like a thing that happened faster than anything else in human history.

DHH

没错。

Correct.

Host

所以我会说大概在 12 月左右，也许是 11 月底——

So I would say somewhere around December, maybe late November-

DHH

11 月 24 日。

November 24.

Host

这就是……

This is...

DHH

就是那个确切的时刻。

That's the exact moment.

Host

你知道，当人们谈论世界大战中一国入侵另一国之类的事时，就是这样——我们就是这样谈论 AI 改变一切的。是的，它真的彻底转变了。对很多优秀开发者来说，它从 AI 做一点基本的自动补全，可能写 5%、10%、15%、20% 的代码，变成了写 80% 的代码。

You know, when they talk about when one nation invades another in a world war or something like this, it-- this is how we talk about AI changing everything. Yeah, the... And it really just sh- shifted for many great developers. It shifted to where AI is doing some, basic autocomplete, maybe writing 5, 10, 15, 20% of code to writing 80% of code.

DHH

或者 100%。

Or 100.

Host

或者 100%，对，尤其是如果不是面向公众的产品。

Or 100, yeah, especially if it's not a public-facing product.

DHH

对我来说，这就是为什么即使回头看一年前的对话，我其实并没有不同的观点。我还是同样的观点。一年前，我不喜欢当时提供给我们的 AI 模式。那是自动补全模式，或者 AI 聊天机器人模式。其实聊天机器人我很喜欢，就像我们聊过的。从一开始就是很好的导师，很好的上网查资料的方式，但它不会取代我雕琢代码。但后来我们有了智能体。智能体一开始大概五分钟内还只是新奇玩意儿，然后就变得惊人，然后就变成“天哪，这是 AGI 吗？”所有这一切都发生在去年以来，甚至就在过去九个月里。我们基本上经历了这几个阶段。我们有前智能体时代的 AI。我当时很兴奋，但它并没有从根本上改写游戏规则。它没有完全改变我的工作方式。我还在雕琢代码，只是多了个小帮手、小搭档，可以和我碰撞想法，我可以更高效地上网查资料等等。它只是让我做原来在做的事更高效了，并没有改变我和电脑之间的情感连接。然后我们到了 2025 年 11 月 24 日。对我来说，Opus 4.5 就是分水岭，突然之间……我甚至 24 号都没试。我记得是 26 号试的。我给了它几个任务，然后意识到输出的质量和我自己会写的东西惊人地接近。

To me, that's why even when I look back upon our conversation a year ago, I don't actually have different opinions. I have the same opinions. A year ago, I did not like the mode of AI we were offered. It was the autocomplete mode, or it was the AI chatbot mode. Now, the chatbot I actually liked, as we talked about. Great tutor right from the get-go, great way of looking things up on the internet, not what was gonna replace me chiseling code. But then we get the agents. And the agents start out being curiosities for about five minutes, and then they get amazing, and then they get, "Oh my God, is this AGI?" And all of that happened since last year, even just within the last nine months. We have basically these few phases here. We have AI in the pre-agentic era. I was excited about that, but it was not fundamentally rewriting the rules of the game for me. It was not completely changing how I worked. I was still chiseling code, I just had a little helper, a little sidekick who could bounce ideas off, and I could look up this information online and so forth in a more efficient way. It was just a more efficient way to do what I was already doing, and it didn't change the emotional connection I had to the computer. Then we get to November 24th, 2025. Opus 4.5, to me, was the dividing line, where suddenly... I didn't even try it on the 24th. I think I tried it on the 26th. I give it a couple of tasks, and I realize that the quality of the output is uncannily close to what I would've written.

代理时代：从Opus 4.5到子代理 The Agentic Age: From Opus 4.5 to Sub-Agents

DHH

我记得当时往后一靠，心想：“刚才发生了什么？” “我们是怎么从夏天我跟你聊的那个自动补全的烂摊子，短短几个月后就走到这一步的？我们是怎么同时获得智能的提升和可用性的提升的？” 这个智能体工具的问题在于，我不知道 Opus 4.5 是否比我们夏天用的 Opus 4 聪明多少，但它操控你的电脑、使用工具、检查自己的工作、以这样一种方式运用智能从而让你能从中获得真正有意义的工作成果的能力，是完全不同的。我认为这就是几乎所有在圣诞假期关注并开始摆弄它的人所经历的重大变化。我从 Shopify 和其他拥有大量员工的地方听到的就是这样，他们突然有了喘息之机，突然有了几周时间往后一靠，看看正在发生的事情，试了一下，然后就有了同样令人震撼的体验：这些智能体和我们之前所拥有的东西属于不同的类型。

And I remember just leaning back and thinking, "What just happened?" "How did we go from this autocomplete mess that I was talking to you about in the summer to this just a few short months later? How did we get both the increase in intelligence and then also the increase in usability?" This agent harness question where I don't know if Opus 4.5 was that much smarter than Opus 4, which is what we had in the summer, but its ability to instrument your computer, to use tools, to check its own work, to apply its intelligence in such a way that you could get real meaningful work out of it, was completely different. And I think this is then the big change that happens for almost anyone who paid attention and started playing with it over the Christmas break. This is what I heard from Shopify and other places with lots of employees who suddenly had a breather, suddenly had a couple of weeks to lean back and just look at what was going on, gave it a try, and had the same mind-blowing experience that these agents were of a different genre than what we had before.

DHH

然后我们到了这个点，我已经很兴奋了。比如，到十二月，我已经在重新审视我所有的先验，心想：“哇，如果它能做到这个，那它也能做到那个吗？” “哦，是的，它能。” 而且，Opus 4.5 现在看起来像个迟钝的模型。这就是这种进步的魔力，你以为你达到了某个高度……我记得当时心想：“如果这是我们得到的最后一个模型，我就满足了。我会很高兴。” 我可以在未来 20 年里用 Opus 4.5，你不会听到我任何抱怨，因为看到一个智能体以我想要的方式完成所有这些工作，实在是太令人惊叹了。因为这不仅仅是它能解决任务，还在于我可以看着它的路径说：“是的。是的。也许不是那里，但差不多。” 它会给你两个注释。你会以我想要的方式到达我想去的地方。你能生成我想要合并的代码。如果代码是用 Ruby 写的，它看起来真的很漂亮。Rust，那就是另一回事了。但这种让智能体真正成为我工作方式的延伸的能力，是非常新颖的。

And then what we get to is, I'm already excited at this point. Like, by December, I'm already revisiting sort of all my priors and going like, "Wow, if it can do this, can it also do that?" "Oh, yeah, it can." And again, Opus 4.5 now looks like a retarded model. And this is the magic of this progress, is you think you've reached something... I remember thinking at the moment, "If this is the last model we get, I'll be set. I'll be happy." I could live with Opus 4.5 for the next 20 years and you would hear no complaints from me because it was just so incredibly amazing to see an agent do all this work in the way that I wanted it done. Because it was not just about it being able to solve a task, it was also that I could look at its path there and go, "Yep. Yep. Maybe not there, but almost," and it would give you two notes. You'll get to where I wanted to go in the way I wanted to go there. You could produce code I wanted to merge. You could produce code that actually looked beautiful if it was written in Ruby. Rust, different question. But this ability for the agents to truly become an extension of how I wanted to work was very novel.

DHH

但随后我们只等到早春，突然我们有了子智能体。我们有了能够细分任务的工具，原本需要 Opus 花相当长时间的事情，突然只花了五分之一的时间、十分之一的时间，因为它可以被切碎，突然你有八个子智能体在为你工作。但在我看来，智能体时代的这两个初始阶段，仍然感觉我必须驾驶。我可以告诉它我想要什么、去哪里找，并在它偏离时稍微引导一下，然后我们就会到达那里，而且我会快得多。但我必须坐在驾驶座上。我必须告诉它我想要什么，而且我必须成为输出的审查者和审计者。

But then we wait just until early spring, and suddenly we get sub-agents. We get harnesses that can subdivide the task, and something that would take Opus quite a while suddenly took a fifth of the time, a tenth of the time, because it could get chopped up and suddenly you got eight sub-agents working for you. But both of those two first phases of the agentic age, to me, still felt like I had to drive. I could tell it what I wanted, where to look for it, and steer it a little bit when it went off, and then we'll get there, and I would go much faster. But I had to be in the driver's seat. I had to tell it what I wanted, and I had to be the reviewer, the auditor of what was coming out.

DHH

然后终于到了现在，这个夏天，随着 Opus 5、Fable 和 Sol、GPT Sol，以及在较小程度上一些开放权重模型的出现，我们到达了一个新时代，我不再告诉它我们要去哪里。我告诉它我遇到的问题。我告诉它我那个模糊、含糊的想法。它告诉我我们要去哪里。它告诉我该走哪条路，而我仍然会看它，因为我是一个好奇的人，我喜欢电脑，我喜欢它的结果，但我真的有点不必看了。在生成代码、选择路线的那部分，我已经变得可有可无了。

And then finally now, this summer, with Opus 5, Fable, and Sol, GPT Sol, and to a lesser extent, some of the open weight models, we've arrived at a new era where I'm not telling it where we're going. I'm telling it the problem I have. I'm telling it the fuzzy, vague idea I have. It tells me where we're going. It tells me which path to take, and I will still look at it because I'm a curious person and I like computers and I like the outcome of it, but I really kinda don't have to. I've become optional in the part that produces the code that picks the route.

DHH

还记得早期 GPS 系统刚出来的时候吗？与看地图相比，它们令人惊叹，但你仍然想保持警惕。它会不会把你开进港口？我记得那些报纸文章。“啊，GPS，它们太糟糕了，因为人们不注意，他们把车开进了港口。” GPS 最后一次把人开进港口是什么时候？就像，那种事不再发生了。事实上，现在汽车都是自动驾驶了，对吧？这就是我们现在到达的地方，我可以信任它。对于我现在工作的领域，我可以信任它，并且完全确信它会支持我。它不会做蠢事，如果它做了蠢事，它也能恢复过来。

Remember when early GPS systems came out? They were amazing compared to looking at a map, but you still wanted to pay attention. Is it gonna drive you in the harbor? I remember these newspaper articles. "Ah, GPS, they're terrible because people don't pay attention, they drive in the harbor." When was the last time GPS drove anyone in the harbor? Like, that just doesn't happen anymore. In fact, the cars now just drive themselves, right? And this is where we've arrived at now, that I can trust. For the domain I'm working in right now, I can trust it, and feel completely confident that it's gonna have my back. It's not gonna do something stupid, and if it does something stupid, it's gonna be able to recover.

Host

嗯哼。我们应该提到，程序员工作的领域多种多样。

Mm-hmm. We should mention that there's all kinds of domains that programmers operate in.

DHH

没错。

Correct.

Host

有……我其实不知道，但我认为开发中最常见的领域是像 Web 开发的 CRUD。你有一个数据库。你有一个面向用户的界面，它来回做一些事情，它可以是只面向一个人的内部系统，也可以是面向多个人、少数人，或者面向全世界。我认为对于那个领域，它——我的意思是，你真的可以让 AI 写出 100% 的代码，而且如果你是一个优秀的程序员，你对幕后发生的事情有很好的直觉，你真的可以不看代码，至少那是我的经验。我不知道你需要多少编程经验才能直觉地通过观察系统的连锁反应和症状来知道幕后的事情是否正常运作。但我认为对于那个领域，它接近 100%，然后还有一些领域，比如你也在做的，就是编写 Linux 发行版。在那里，也许需要更多关注，因为你痴迷于速度之类的东西。在那里，也许你需要多看一点代码。然后也许还有安全关键系统，一直到运营核电站或自动驾驶汽车。也许你需要更仔细地查看代码。

There's... I actually don't know, but I think the most common domain in development is, like, web dev CRUD. You have a database. You have a user-facing interface, and it does something back and forth, and it could be internal to just one person, to multiple people, to a small number of people, or to the world. And I think for that, it's-- I mean, you could really get to the 100% of code written by AI and really if you're a good programmer and you have a good intuition about what's happening behind the scenes, you can legitimately not look at the code, at least that's, that's been my experience. I don't know how much prior experience with programming you need to have to kinda know that things are working correctly behind the scenes, like intuitively by observing the ripple effects, the symptoms of the system. But I think for that domain, it's close to 100%, and then there's domains like you're also operating in, which is writing a Linux distribution. There, maybe there's more because you're obsessed with speed and all that kind of stuff. There, maybe you need to look at the code a little bit more. Then there's maybe safety critical systems all the way down to operating a nuclear power plant or a self-driving car. Maybe you need to look at the code more carefully.

DHH

是的，但话虽如此，AI 在发现和修复安全漏洞方面都极其强大。这就是关于 Fable 的整个风波。这个模型太擅长发现攻击者可以利用的漏洞，以至于发布它根本不安全。所以这里的讽刺之处在于，当你审视那个领域时，我们似乎已经达到了几乎没有人能匹敌的智能水平，因为许多这些安全漏洞都是关于将组合技串联起来的。你在这里发现一个小漏洞，它本身可能不是世界上最糟糕的事情，但当你把它和其他四个结合起来，突然你就有了 RCE，远程命令执行。能够做到这一点的人类非常罕见。他们通常在政府资助的组织或其他秘密行动中工作。他们不会到处闲逛寻找东西。所以它们在这方面变得非常擅长。

Yes, but that said, AI is insanely capable at both finding and fixing security vulnerabilities. This was the whole blowup about Fable. This model was so capable of finding holes that an attacker could exploit that it was simply not safe to release. So the irony here is that when you look at that field, it seems like we've reached levels of intelligence that virtually no human can match because many of these security holes are about stringing combo moves together. You find one little vulnerability here that by itself might not be the worst thing in the world, but then you combine it with four others, and suddenly you have RCE, remote command execution. Humans who are able to do that are very rare. They usually work inside state-sponsored organizations or other clandestine operations. They're not just out and about finding things. So they've gotten so good at that.

DHH

Linux 发行版就是为什么我 100% 相信 AI。因为过去三个月我一直在做 Omarchy，这个几天前刚发布的版本叫 Quatro。几乎从一开始我就在做那个版本，智能体的加速接近 100%，而在过去两个月里它一直是 100%。Quatro 中发布的任何代码都不是我手写的。我审查了所有代码的结构。

The Linux distribution is why I've gotten 100% AI pilled. Because I've been working on Omarchy for the last three months, this version that just dropped a few days ago called Quatro. And almost right from the beginning I'm working on that version, the agent acceleration neared 100%, and in the last two months it has been 100%. I have not written any of the code that's shipped in Quatro by hand. I've reviewed the shape of all of it.

Host

真的吗？

Really?

DHH

……Quatro 中发布的任何代码都不是我手写的。我审查了所有代码的结构。

... any of the code that's shipped in Quatro by hand. I've reviewed the shape of all of it.

代理加速与Basecamp 5 Agent Acceleration and Basecamp 5

DHH

我审查了系统模型层中任何关键部分的每一行代码，但我没有看大量的 UI 代码。我也没有看大量的辅助代码，而且我没有完全手工编写任何新功能。但是，在 Web 部分，实际上，演进 Basecamp 和 Hey，这些拥有大量用户且代码库相对较大的专业产品，被证明要完全用智能体加速是出奇地棘手。我们不久前刚发布了 Basecamp 5。那是 37signals 第一款真正由智能体加速的产品。因为我们从大约二月份就进入了最后的冲刺阶段。那时，智能体已经很好了。我们有过一阵早期的热潮，觉得“问题解决了。我们可以让设计师来做编程。他们知道自己想要什么功能。他们知道想要什么形态。就让他们——让他们凭感觉来。”我们让他们凭感觉来。结果我们得到了大量的 PR，单独看每一个也许在某个瞬间是合理的，但合在一起，却摧毁了系统的架构。我们实际上不得不手动清理，用人工的手来收拾残局，才能回到一个感觉连贯一致的架构。所以我们还有一点那种——顺便说一句，那是二月份的事。现在情况已经大不相同了。

I've reviewed the individual lines of anything that's critical in the model layer of the system, and I've not looked at a bunch of the UI code. I have not looked at a bunch of the auxiliary code, and I've not written any of the new functionality entirely by hand. But then the web part, actually evolving Basecamp and Hey, our professional products that have lots of users and are relatively large code bases, have proven surprisingly tricky to fully accelerate with agents. We just released Basecamp 5 not too long ago. That was the first product at 37signals that was really agent accelerated. Because we were in this final sprint phase from around February. By then, agents were already good. And we had this early surge of, "It's solved. We can just have the designers do the programming. They know what features they want. They know what shape they want it to take. Let's just-- Let them vibe." And we let them vibe. And we ended up with a lot of PRs that individually perhaps could have been justified for a hot moment, taken all together, destroyed the architecture of the system. And we actually had to clean up manually, mop it up by hand, by human hand, to get back to an architecture that felt cohesive and coherent. So we still have a bit of that-- that was February, by the way. Things are quite different now.

Host

嗯，等一下。从中得到的教训是什么？其中一个教训是不是，在这个阶段，你必须是一名程序员才能凭感觉写代码？

Well, hold on a second. What's the lesson from that? Is one of the lessons from that that you have to be a programmer at this stage to be able to vibe code?

DHH

要在现有的大型代码库上凭感觉写代码，即使这些代码库很粗糙，如果你想保留让系统走到今天的那种架构元素，你就得是程序员。

To be able to vibe code on existing substantial code bases, even if they're crud, if you wanna retain the element of architecture that got that system to where it was.

Host

我明白了。

I see.

DHH

这也是我多次强调的一点：当人们指责凭感觉写代码的人是垃圾生成器时，我会直接反驳他们说：“你看过普通程序员的产出吗？”那也是另一种垃圾。如果你看过许多伟大公司的幕后，看看他们的代码库在经历了 3000 个人之后是什么样子，那真是糟糕透顶。绝对糟糕。

Now, that's also a point where I've stressed many times that when people accuse vibe coders of being slob generators, I go right back at them and say, "Have you looked at the average programmer's output?" That is some other slob too. If you've looked at behind the scenes of many great companies and what their code bases look like after there's been 3,000 humans through them, they're awful. Absolutely awful.

Host

你能告诉我你的直觉吗？我似乎只是退后一步，观察我们都依赖的各种应用，比如 Adobe Photoshop 这类东西。那里的开发进展似乎并没有加速。那么，为什么？你能从 Basecamp 这样成熟、拥有庞大用户基础的产品中得出什么教训？为什么我们没有看到这些成熟应用的新更新、新功能之类的东西出现超快速的增长？

Can you tell me the intuition you have? I seem to just, stepping back and observing the different apps that we all rely on, I don't know, Adobe Photoshop, all this kind of stuff. There seems to be the progress on development there has not accelerated. So why is it, what lessons can you draw from Basecamp, like well-established, huge user base? Why aren't we seeing, like, super rapid increase in, like, new updates, features, all this kind of stuff in these well-established apps?

DHH

原因有很多。我先说最关键的第一个。一旦你让人类团队一起做某件事，瓶颈很少是实现本身，而是人力的带宽和沟通。当你有一个产品经理、几个设计师、他们上面一个副总裁、再上面一个 CTO，每个人都想参与塑造过程，因为我们都在证明自己为什么在这里，那就是生产力消亡的地方。过去三个月在 Omarchy 工作的启示是，要获得那种神奇的 10 倍、100 倍，少数情况下 1000 倍的生产力提升，你必须直接与智能体互动，你不能用另一个人来中转这种带宽，因为那实在太慢了。一方面，这有点令人沮丧。我的意思是，我喜欢人类，一起工作很棒。但这也意味着，如果由人类驱动，并且你有三层审批和大公司的所有其他机制，我们需要调整对这些智能体能力的期望。实现部分只是其中一小部分。我要说的另一件事是，大多数组织不知道自己想要什么。他们不知道如何做得更好。他们的瓶颈不是实现，而是想法。他们的瓶颈是愿景，是品味。如果你的这些元素没有超过你的实现能力，那也没用。所以你可以让很多糟糕的想法成真，然后呢？你要发布那个吗？那听起来像是，我不知道，微软出来的东西。那不是我们想要复制的。那个过程实际上并不是……因为我们已经有了这个。如果你退一步想想那些庞大的组织，他们拥有数万名程序员。我的意思是，我这里是在挑微软的毛病。我有时也喜欢微软，但在这个案例中我要挑它的毛病，因为他们几十年来拥有无尽的资源、无尽的编程能力，对吧？这向我们展示了，仅仅能写大量代码并不能产生出色的、引人注目的软件。另外，我们拥有这种能力大约六个月了。在人类内化正在发生的事情的生命周期中，这并不长。而且我觉得有趣的是，对 AI 的批评是，为什么它没有更快？你在说什么？我们没有任何其他形式的进步能像 AI 一样快，而你却因为过去三个月我们没有重写整个世界，让它变成软件美好的乌托邦而不耐烦？

Multiple reasons. I'll start with the first one that's the most critical. As soon as you're having human teams work together on something, the bottleneck is rarely implementation. It's human bandwidth and communication. When you have a product manager and a couple of designers and a VP above them and a CTO above them, and everyone wants to be part of the shaping process because we're all justifying why we're here, that's where all the productivity goes to die. The revelation I've had working on Omarchy the last three months is that to get that magical 10X, 100X, in a few rare cases, 1000X productivity boost, you have to interact with the agents directly, and you cannot intermediate that bandwidth with another human because it's simply too slow. And on the one hand, that's a bit of a bummer. I mean, I like humans, and it's great to work together. But it also means we need to temper our expectations with what these agents can do if it's humans driving it, and you have three layers of approval and all the other machinery of a large corporation. The implementation part is only a small segment of it. The other thing I'd say is that most organizations don't know what they want. They don't know how to make it better. They're not bottlenecked on implementation. They're bottlenecked on ideas. They're bottlenecked on vision. They're bottlenecked on taste. And if you don't have those element in excess of your implementational capacity, it doesn't help. So you can make a lot of shitty ideas come true, then what? Are you gonna ship that? That sounds like, I don't know, something coming out of Microsoft. That's not what we're trying to replicate here. That process is not actually... Because we already had this. If you step back for a moment and think of the towering organizations we have who have had tens of thousands of programmers at their disposal. I mean, I'm picking on Microsoft here. I love Microsoft some of the time, but I'll pick on them in this case because they have had endless resources, endless programming capacity for decades, right? This is what's showing us that just being able to write a lot of code does not produce great compelling software. Now, the other thing is we've had this capacity for about six months. That's not very long in human life cycle capacity of internalizing what's going on. And I think it's actually funny that the critique of AI is, why isn't it going faster? What are you talking about? We've not had any other form of progress that has moved as fast as AI, and you're impatient because in the last three months we haven't rewritten the whole world and made it a utopia of software goodness?

DHH

这是经典的创新者困境。这些公司在旧方式上已经变得如此出色、如此成熟，因此它们的整个结构、管理层级、流程都是为一个不再存在的时代而调整的。

This is the classic innovator's dilemma. These companies have gotten so good, so established at the old way, and therefore their entire structure, management layers, processes are tuned for a time that no longer exists.

平台转变与代理机遇 Platform Shift and the Opening for Agents

DHH

但你没法掉头。这些是超级油轮，根本转不了弯。这就是为什么科技行业终于开始闹腾了。有一阵子，我对苹果和谷歌在移动端的双头垄断非常不满，因为移动端感觉是我们最重要的计算平台，而我看不到任何一条路能撼动谷歌或苹果坐在顶端、控制一切、收过路费。但游戏规则变了。这不再是那个最重要的平台了。手机很重要，但还有很多其他形态正在涌现，比如眼镜、耳机，或者其他什么。现在一切都还在博弈中，但计算平台本身，可能是 40 年来第一次真正处于博弈之中。看看桌面端，Linux 从 91 年就有了，在桌面上并没有起飞或接管。它接管了其他一切。你桌上的所有设备，你的冰箱、你的烤面包机，全都跑 Linux，除了你的电脑。现在，有趣的是，你的安卓其实也是 Linux，只是被包装得你根本认不出来。但现在出现了一个缺口，而这个缺口正如你所说。如果你依赖某个软件，它把你绑定在 Windows 上，那么你个人完全有能力开始重写它。也许你做不到 100% 覆盖，但这就是关于 Microsoft Office 的老笑话：“我只用 5%。”是啊，但我们每个人用的 5% 都不一样。那如果我们每个人都构建自己的 5% 呢？如果我只需要我需要的功能，然后就去实现它呢？这是一个完全不同的挑战，而智能体现在、今天就能极其出色地完成它，我已经反复做过很多次了。

But you can't pivot that. These are super tankers. It just doesn't happen. This is why we're finally getting this upset in the technology industry. For a while, I was very upset about the duopoly between Apple and Google on mobile, because it felt like mobile was the most important computing platform that we had, and I could not see a path to unseat either Google or Apple sitting on top of that, controlling everything, taking their toll booth money. But the game has changed. This is no longer the most important platform. Mobile phones are important, but there's also a lot of other form factors coming, whether it's glasses or earpieces or whatever else have you. It's all in play now, but the computing platforms themselves are in play for the first time in probably 40 years, if you look at the desktop. Linux has been around since '91. It's not taken off or taken over on the desktop. It's taken over everything else. All the devices you have on your desk, your fridge, your toaster, everything runs Linux, except for your computer. Now, funnily enough, your Android is actually Linux, but wrapped so sufficiently that you can't really recognize it. But now there's an opening, and the opening is exactly as you say it. If there's a piece of software you depended on that bound you to Windows, it is completely within reach for you personally to start rewriting it. And maybe you won't get to 100% coverage, but this is the old joke about Microsoft Office. "I only use 5%." Yeah, well, we all use a different 5%. Well, what if we all just build our own 5%? What if I just took the functionality that I need and just did that? That is a completely different challenge and one agents are incredibly capable of doing right now, today, and I've done it a lot of times over.

用代理成为多语言程序员 Becoming a Polyglot Programmer with Agents

DHH

所以，我在最新的智能体参与中发现的一件神奇的事情是，我变成了一个多语言程序员，这在以前绝对不可能。我首先是一个 Ruby 程序员，然后偶尔在需要时碰一点 Bash。而在过去两个月里，我写了 C++。我写了三个已经随 Omarchy Quattro 发布的应用。我写了一个写作应用。我之前在用一款叫 Typora 的应用，那是一款非常好的应用。

So one of the amazing things I've found with the latest agent engaged is I have become a polyglot programmer, something I was absolutely not before. I was a Ruby programmer first and foremost, and then I dabbled a little bit in Bash when I had to. And in the last two months, I've written C++. I've written three applications that have shipped in Omarchy Quattro. I wrote a writing app. I was using this app called Typora, which is a very nice app.

Host

很棒，很棒的应用。

Great, great app.

DHH

它本身又是基于另一款叫 iA Writer 的应用，那是我在 Mac 上真正钟爱的应用，用于干净、简单的 Markdown 写作环境。我所有的文章都是在那里写的。后来我转到 Linux，没法用 iA Writer，于是换到了 Typora 这个工具。它是一款共享软件，有很多我根本不需要的功能。大约六七周前，我想：“你知道吗？我只需要 Typora 的 5%。”而它已经是一款基础应用了。我直接告诉智能体开始干，我希望用 C++ 和 Qt 写，因为那会和我用 Quattro 构建的美学很契合。我想，大约 20 分钟后，它就有了第一个版本。我开始使用，发现不太对。两天之内，我就放弃了 Typora，从那一刻起，我所有的文章都是在 Omawrite 里写的。

Which in itself is based on another app called iA Writer, which was my real love on the Mac for a clean, simple Markdown writing environment. That's where I write all my essays. And then I moved to Linux, and I couldn't get iA Writer, so I moved over to this other tool called Typora. It was a piece of shareware, and it had a lot of features I just didn't need. And about six weeks, seven weeks ago, I thought, "Do you know what? I only need 5% of Typora," which is already a basic app. I literally told the agent to get going, that I wanted it written in C++ and Qt, because that would fit well with the aesthetic of what I was building with Quattro. And in, I think, about 20 minutes, it had the first version. I started using it, and it wasn't quite right. Within two days, I'd given up Typora, and I wrote and have written all of my essays since that moment in Omawrite.

从个人工具到产品：扩展建议 From Personal Tool to Product: Advice on Scaling

Host

我能请教一下你的建议和愿景吗？我发现，用智能体，你确实可以写出像 Typora 替代品这样的东西，完美地定制给你，满足你的需求。

Can I ask for your advice and your vision about something? So what I find with agents, you can actually write something like a replacement for Typora that is perfectly customized to you, to your needs.

DHH

没错。

Correct.

Host

只考虑一个用户。然后有一个版本，从一个用户开始，但扩展到更大的受众。所以我实际上写了很多只为我自己的软件，我发现这样做更容易，也更能解决我的问题。比如，我写了一个视频编辑器，只为我自己的话非常容易。我从来没有，不像你，构建过被大量人使用的东西，我觉得这有点可怕和令人生畏。所以，在这个智能体时代，你如何迈出一步，真正构建一个解决你问题的东西——我认为这是一种美丽的构建方式——同时扩展到对其他人也有用？比如，你有什么建议吗？你也看到这个问题了吗，就是为自己构建一个工具太容易了？

With just one user in mind. And then there's a version of that that starts with one user in mind but expands to a larger audience. So I've actually written a large number of software just for me, and I find it easier to do and better to get my situation done. For example, I have a video editor I've written, which is very easy to do just for me. I've never, unlike you, built something that's used by a large number of people, and I find that a bit scary and intimidating. So, like, at this, with this agentic age, how do you take a step to actually build something that solves your problem, which I think is a beautiful way to build, but also expand it to actually useful for other people? Like, is there advice you can give, and do you also see the problem of how easy it is to just build a tool for yourself?

分享的简单性与开源改革 The Simplicity of Sharing and the Open Source Reformation

DHH

是的，但比你想象的要简单。当你为自己构建完工具时，你只需告诉智能体把它放到 GitHub 上。你甚至不需要做任何其他事情。它会自己想办法把仓库放到 GitHub 上。它会写一个漂亮的 README。它会开始使用 GitHub Releases 来跟踪事情。它实际上会成为一个比你更好的软件维护者，因为它比你更有耐心，在管理开源软件的琐碎工作上比你更勤奋。而你就可以继续享受使用自己构建的软件的乐趣，并按你认为合适的方式去演进它。现在，这就进入了我们在开源界争论已久的大问题：AI 的贡献对维护者是好是坏？现在有很多维护者非常不满，因为他们突然收到大量拉取请求，来自可能不是最好程序员、甚至根本不是程序员的人的贡献。我看到这个争论，心想：“你在开玩笑吗？”这里有一条免费贡献的矿脉，你可以接受也可以不接受，但你却在抱怨它们存在？这听起来就像“我的牛排太嫩了，我的龙虾太黄油了”。你在抱怨什么？这是最神奇的事情。这是我们长期以来宣称的开源的魔力，我们都能为它做贡献，但当然，这从来都不是真的。只有一小部分人贡献开源，那就是那些技术高超的巫师。所以这是一个宗教改革的时刻。我们曾经有我们和计算机之间的去中介化，那是一群被称为程序员的牧师和神职人员，突然他们被去中介化了——是 1500 年代路德的《九十五条论纲》钉在门上的吗？其中一条非常明确地关于中介化，即你应该直接接触更高的智能。

Yes, but it is simpler than you think. By the time you're done building the tool for yourself, you simply tell your agent to put it on GitHub. You don't even have to do anything else. It will figure out how to put that repo on GitHub. It'll write a nice README. It'll start using GitHub releases so that you can track things. It'll actually be a better software maintainer than you could ever be, because it is far more patient, it is far more diligent in doing the drudgery of managing open source software than you are. And you can simply get on with the joy of using the software that you built and evolving it as you see fit. Now, this gets into the big argument we've been having in open source for a while. Is AI contributions good or bad for the maintainer? There's a lot of maintainers right now who are quite upset about the fact that they're suddenly getting a huge influx of pull requests, of contributions from people who may not be the best programmers or programmers at all towards their software. I look at that argument and go, "Are you kidding me?" Here is a vein of free contributions that you can take or don't take, but you're complaining about the fact that they're there? It sounds straight out of "My steak is too juicy and my lobster too buttery." What are you complaining about? This is the most amazing thing. This is what we proclamated for so long was going to be the magic of open source, that we could all contribute to it, but it was, of course, never true. There was only a small subset of people who contribute to open source, and that was the very highly skilled wizards. So this is a bit of a reformation moment. We had this disintermediation between us and the computer, and it was this class of clerics and priests called programmers, and suddenly they're being disintermediated here by was it the 95 theses by Luther in 1500s, nailing them to the door? And one of those was very clearly about the intermediation, that you should have direct access to the higher intelligence.

巫师角色与程序员现实 The Role of Wizards and the Reality of Most Programmers

Host

嗯。但你难道不需要巫师来保持代码库的高水准吗？

Mm-hmm. But don't you need the wizards to keep a high bar of excellence in the code base?

DHH

100% 需要，这也是开源界一直以来的事实，这是第二个真正让我恼火的论点。我运营开源项目已经 25 年了。我真的看过成千上万，甚至数万名程序员的产出。我觉得我有统计基础来断言：大多数程序员，他们很烂。我不是说听起来那个意思。这就是为什么我在这里停顿了一下。我的意思是，他们烂在不会写我想写的代码。他们不会准备包含所有相关信息的 bug 报告。他们不会在拉取请求中详细说明为什么。

100% you do, which is also something that's always been true about open source, which is the second argument that really grinds me. I've been running open source projects for 25 years. I have literally looked at the output of thousands, if not tens of thousands of programmers. I feel like I have the statistical basis to assert that most programmers, they suck. And I don't mean that in the way it sounds. That was why I paused for a hot second here. I mean, they suck in the sense that they don't write the code I want to have written. They don't prepare their bug reports with all the relevant information. They don't detail their pull requests with the why.

代理编写的PR与人类贡献 Agent-written pull requests vs human contributions

DHH

他们懒得补上必要的代码注释，不复查自己的工作，不写单元测试，也不做那些打造好软件所需的一切。但你知道谁会做这些事吗？智能体，只要你告诉它们。它们非常勤勉地遵循你的指示，大多数时候如此，有时甚至过于死板。但这实际上意味着，如果你拿普通程序员和他们对一个普通开源项目提交的拉取请求来看，他们已经被智能体超越了。我宁愿收到一个由智能体写的拉取请求，也不愿收到人类写的，这不仅是因为质量更好，还因为如果我直接拒绝它，我不会感到那么内疚。你甚至都没写它，所以我只需看看你的智能体替你写了什么，然后说：“嗯，不想要。”这在开源界一直如此，事实上，我认为这正是开源维护者的问题之一。他们背负了太多神经质和焦虑，以至于把任何贡献都视为自己的义务，必须竭尽全力让它进入代码库。这完全不对。你可以直接说“不”，或者更好的是，说“不，谢谢”。一旦你意识到你的项目可以按照你的愿景、你的路线图来存在和发展，在智能体时代拒绝你不想要的贡献就变得容易得多，因为你甚至不会给人类带来不便或伤害感情。它只是个“铁疙瘩”，而“铁疙瘩”不会介意。事实上，“铁疙瘩”的生产者，也就是那些实验室，很喜欢你白白消耗 token。他们照样收钱。所以，在我看来，这是成为开源软件维护者的绝对最佳时机。我们不仅获得了大量由智能体写就、所有选项都打勾的精彩拉取请求，还能挖掘到那些以前无法为项目做贡献的人的创造力。我运营 Omarchy 项目已经一年多了，而在过去三个月里，在开发 Quattro 的过程中，我合并了超过 1000 个拉取请求。其中相当一部分是由非传统程序员写的，或者是在其他领域工作的程序员，不是搞 Linux 操作系统或发行版开发的。他们因为智能体而能够贡献自己的好点子，而我得以从中挑选出最好的……因为那些点子就在那里。否则，这些想法只会留在他们脑子里。再说一遍，开源的目的不就是挖掘整个地球上所有人的集体智慧和创造力，并将其导向一个我们都能从所有人的共同努力中受益的公共资源吗？目前，Omarchy 上大约有 400 个未合并的拉取请求，比一周前翻了一番。所以加速是真实的，工具也是。我不再审查每一个拉取请求了，我已经有一段时间没这么做了。我让智能体替我审查，然后它们会给我一个摘要，告诉我某件事是否已经准备好做决定，人类做决定。该合并还是不该合并？我不需要看那些错误的、重复的、糟糕的拉取请求。智能体可以替我把那些糟粕筛掉。我只需要看那些珍珠……那些准备好上马的好点子，那些智能体已经替我在虚拟机里验证过的 bug 修复。复制和管理开源项目的所有苦差事正以闪电般的速度消失，我们只剩下那些金色多汁的部分，软件开发中的骨髓……决定这个东西应该做什么，以及它应该往哪里去。

They don't bother to fill in needed code comments. They don't double-check their work. They don't write unit tests. They don't do all of these things it takes to create good software. But do you know who'll do all that stuff? Agents, if you tell them to. They're very diligent at following your instructions, most of the time, sometimes to a fault. But it actually means that if you take the median programmer and their pull requests towards an average open source project, they're already getting outclassed by agents. I would rather get an agent-written pull request to one of my projects than I'd get one written by a human, and it's not just because the quality's better. It's also because I feel a lot less bad if I just reject it. You didn't even write it, so I can simply look at what your agent wrote on your behalf and go, "Eh, don't want it." That's always been true in open source, and in fact, I think this is one of the problems with open source maintainers. They have way too much ball of neuroticism and anxiety that requires them to look upon any contribution as an obligation on their part to do everything to get that to land in the code base. That's not true at all. You can simply say, "No," or even better, you can say, "No, thank you." And if you get into the mode of realizing that your project is allowed to exist and evolve according to your vision, according to your roadmap, it becomes so much easier in the agentic age to decline the contributions you don't want because you don't even inconvenience or hurt the feelings of a human. It's just a clanker, and the clanker won't mind. In fact, the producers of clankers, the labs, love when you spend tokens in vain. They get paid just the same. So, in my opinion, this is the absolute best time to ever have been an open source software maintainer. Not only do we get this wealth of glorious pull requests made by agents with all the boxes ticked, we also get to tap into a creativity of people who did not have access to contribute to a project before. I've been running the Omarchy project now for a little over a year, and in the last three months working on Quattro, I have merged over 1,000 pull requests. Quite a lot of those pull requests were written by people who were not classical programmers, or were programmers in other domains, not Linux operating system or distribution development. They were able to contribute their good ideas because of agents, and I got to cherry-pick the very best of that... because it was there. Otherwise, those ideas would just have lived inside their heads. Again, that is not the purpose of open source, that we tap into the collective intelligence and creativity of the whole goddamn planet, and we channel that towards a commons where we all benefit from the combined efforts of everyone. Now, there's currently, I think, about 400 unmerged pull requests on Omarchy, about double what it was a week ago. So the acceleration is real, but so are the tools. I'm not reviewing every pull request anymore. I haven't been reviewing them for quite some time now. I have the agents review them for me, and then they will give me a summary about whether something is ready for the decision, the human decision. Should we merge or should we not merge? I don't need to look at all the pull requests that are either wrong, duplicated, bad. An agent can sort that chaff away from me. I just get to look at the pearls... the good ideas that are ready to go, the bug fixes that the agent has validated in a VM on my behalf. All the drudgery of replicating and managing open source projects is evaporating at lightning speed, and we are left with the golden juicy parts, the bone marrow of software development... deciding what should this thing do and where should it go.

好主意源自人类吗？ Do good ideas originate from humans?

Host

你觉得那些好点子，最终好点子的核心是源于人脑吗，也就是那些个体贡献者？比如，这能由智能体完成吗，还是说拉取请求最终需要人类的点子？比如如何改进 Omarchy，那些涌进来的……还是说这一切都可以只是一个智能体池？

Do you think the good ideas, ultimately the kernel of the good ideas originated in the human mind, so the individual contributors? Like, can it be done by agents, or is the PR ultimately requires a human idea? Like how to improve Omarchy, all those incoming... Or can it all just be a pool of agents?

DHH

那是我在第一个智能体时代（从 11 月 24 日到 2 月 28 日）对智能体的思考方式和模式。我以为所有点子都会源于人类。他们会告诉自己的智能体要构建什么，然后它们就出发了。我现在完全不这么认为了。我见过你们这些人无法相信的东西，从模型里涌现出的点子如此伟大，让我这个自诩有好点子的人都感到谦卑。智能体在创造性思维方面能力惊人，而那些仍然固守“智能体是鹦鹉”的分析、认为它们只是在复述已有想法的人，对过去六到九个月取得的进展是妄想。

That was the mode and the way I was thinking about agents in the first agentic age from November 24 to February 28. I thought all the ideas would originate with the humans. They would tell their agents what to build, and off they went. I don't think that's true anymore at all. I have seen things you people wouldn't believe, ideas coming out of models so great that it makes me humble... as a person who otherwise prides himself on having good ideas. The agents are incredibly capable of creative thought, and folks who are still stuck in the analysis that agents are parrots... just regurgitating the ideas that are already there are delusional about the progress that's been made in the last six to nine months.

为AI精神病论点辩护 Steel-manning the AI psychosis argument

Host

所以现在听你说话的一些人会说，DHH 患上了老式的 AI 精神病。你能为“你实际上正处于一种妄想状态，就像《飞越疯人院》里那样”这个论点做最强辩护吗，然后你能反驳它吗？

So some people listening to you right now will say DHH is suffering from the old case of AI psychosis. Can you steel man the case that you are in fact experiencing in a state of delusion, like One Flew Over the Cuckoo's Nest, and can you argue against it?

DHH

我正处于一种谵妄状态。这就是我现在的状态。因为我和电脑打交道 40 年了，我从未见过在过去两个月里看到的那些东西。整个职业生涯，整个一生都致力于对电脑的热爱，把它作为我工作时间甚至更多时间的主要焦点，突然之间被彻底颠覆，彻底重写。我当然谵妄了。看到这个现实的人谁不是呢？如果你不谵妄，或者至少不非常兴奋……实际上，我觉得这甚至都不对。如果你没有认识到这一刻的严重性，那才是妄想。那才是精神病。精神病是相信世界几乎没变，我们只是有一些电子鹦鹉在给我们背诵东西。在我看来，这个论点之所以被提出来，是因为人们看不到进步的成果。这正好呼应了你之前的观点。那些惊人的软件在哪里？为什么美国 GDP 没有以每年 12% 的速度增长？首先，给它一点时间。这还没到一年呢，天哪。但其次，我觉得就我自己而言，我确实端出了布丁。Omarchy Quattro 周五发布了，已经被数万人下载。他们喜欢它，非常喜欢。

I'm in a state of delirium. That's what the state I'm in. Because I have been working with computers for 40 years, and I've never seen the things that I've seen in just the last two months. A whole career, a whole life dedicating to the love of computers as my main focus for the working hours and then some, suddenly completely upended, suddenly completely rewritten. Of course I'm delirious. Who looking at this reality is not? If you are not delirious or at the very least very excited... Actually, I guess that's not even true. If you're not recognizing the gravity of the moment, that's the delusion. That's the psychosis. The psychosis is believing that the world is barely different. We just have some electronic parrots reciting things to us. Now, in my opinion, the reason this argument is actually brought up is because people are not able to see the fruits of the progress. That goes to your point earlier. Where is the amazing software? Why isn't American GDP running at 12% year over year? First of all, give it a minute. It hasn't even been a year, goddammit. But second of all, I feel on my own account, I have actually brought the pudding. Omarchy Quattro launched on Friday. It's been downloaded by tens of thousands of people. And they like it. They like it a lot.

介绍Omarchy与Quattro Introducing Omarchy and Quattro

Host

我们应该提一下，这里查一下 Perplexity，Omarchy 是一个高度有主见的基于 Arch Linux 的桌面发行版，以 Hyprland Wayland 平铺合成器为核心。它由 David Heinemeier Hansson（DHH）创建，作为一个精致的开发者工作站，具有统一的视觉风格，并且已经配置了许多日常工具。

We should mention that going to Perplexity here, Omarchy is a highly opinionated Arch Linux-based desktop distribution centered on the Hyprland Wayland tiling compositor. It was created by David Heinemeier Hansson, DHH, as a polished developer workstation set up with a cohesive visual style and many everyday tools already configured.

DHH

Omarchy的起源与演变 Omarchy's Origin and Evolution

DHH

那是我们刚发布的最新版本，也挺有意思的。Omarchy 项目其实才刚满一年多一点。我是去年夏天在勒芒 24 小时耐力赛的间隙开始做的，当时我看了太多关于 Linux 崛起的 YouTube 视频，被那个 bug 给咬了。我开始着手这个第二次迭代，试图打造一个更好的 Linux 操作系统。上次我们聊过的第一个迭代叫 Omakub，是建立在现有系统 Ubuntu 之上的，当时觉得还行。但我能倾注到新版本 Omarchy 里的雄心，因为我从技术栈往下深挖了七层，完全不一样了，但它仍然是在一个前智能体时代完成的。一开始我手写了所有那些批处理脚本，然后我看到了中途的转折点。我做了几个版本的 Omarchy，有部分智能体加速，然后三个月前，它全速运转，100%，所有东西都由智能体编写，但由我来掌控。那最终变成了一种非常不同的体验，也是一个非常不同的操作系统，因为我突然被赋予了我野心的无限上限。我可以看着 Windows、Mac、其他 Linux 系统上的任何功能说，“我想要那个”，然后智能体就会交付。所以我想要的一切突然触手可及，这意味着我有点得意忘形了。谁不会得意忘形呢，如果突然有个精灵从瓶子里冒出来说：“你可以拥有任何你想要的东西。你在操作系统里梦想过的每一个功能——我都能给你，大多数五分钟内，少数二十分钟，如果我们真的放开手脚，那得花我两个小时。”

That's the latest version that we just dropped, which is also funny. The Omarchy project is only a year old and a little bit. I started it last summer in between sessions at the 24 Hours of Le Mans, where I had watched one too many YouTube videos on Linux Rising and got bitten by that bug. Started working on this second iteration of my attempt at creating a better Linux operating system. The first iteration we talked about last time was called Omakub, was built on top of an existing system called Ubuntu, and it was fine. But the ambition I was able to pour into the new version, Omarchy, because I started seven layers deeper down the stack, was completely different, but it was still done in a pre-agentic world. I wrote all those batch scripts by hand in the beginning, and then I got to see the midway point where things changed over. I did a couple of versions of Omarchy that had partial agent acceleration, and then three months ago, it was full throttle, 100%, everything is written by agents but steered by me. And that just ended up being a very different experience and a very different operating system because I was suddenly granted a limitless ceiling on my ambition. I could look at any feature in Windows, on Mac, on other Linux systems and say, "I want that," and the agents would deliver. So everything I wanted was suddenly within reach, which meant that I got a little delirious. Who would not get delirious if suddenly a genie pops out of the bottle and says, "You can have whatever you want. Every feature you've ever dreamed of in an operating system—I can deliver them to you, most of them in five minutes, a few in 20, and if we really go hog wild, it's gonna take me two hours."

Host

嗯哼。你看过《梦之安魂曲》这部电影吗？

Mm-hmm. Have you seen Requiem for a Dream, the movie?

DHH

所以这里有一种类似药物的效应。我个人觉得——我们会从很多层面讨论这个，但突然之间被赋予如此巨大的力量，真的让人不知所措，而且很难知道，就像《霍比特人》里那样，该怎么处理它。我发现自己真的被多任务处理压得喘不过气来，几乎快要精疲力竭了。对很多事情超级兴奋，但最终回头看，你想要专注于一件事并真正交付——我没有那种感觉。

So there's a drug-like effect here. I find it personally—We'll talk about many levels of this, but it is overwhelming how much power is placed in our hands so suddenly, and it's very hard to know, it's like with "The Hobbit" or something, what to do with it, and I find myself truly overwhelmed with the multitasking, on the verge of almost burnt out. Super excited about so many things, but ultimately looking back, you want to focus on one thing and really ship—I don't have that sense.

Host

我没有那种不知所措的感觉。我没有那种恐惧感。我没有那种扭曲感，因为我有使命。我有方向，因此，我可以把所有这些新力量引导到——

DHH

我想在你的播客里，Elon 谈到了 Neuralink 和其他项目试图加速的实际人类带宽。比如，我们现在交流的带宽相当低，因为我们受到认知的限制。我们受到说话速度的限制。当你和一群智能体一起工作时，你不会受到这些限制。

呃，所以我理解。我完全理解。如果你没见过当前智能质量能产生什么，你当然会看着某人说：“他们听起来有点疯，”因为你到目前为止的所有经验都会告诉你他们疯了。因为如果你看看计算机编程的历史，过去四五十年，人们一直在承诺普通人只要说话就能写代码。我们会有第四代语言。Lisp 曾经被提出，Smalltalk 被呈现为这些环境，让普通人编写自己的应用程序，但除了像 Microsoft Access 数据库或 Excel 电子表格之外，这些都没有实现。那些是最终用户编程环境，但和我们现在能做的完全不一样。

Uh, so I get it. I totally get it. Because if you look at the history of computer programming, for the last 40, 50 years, people have been promising that regular people could write code just by talking. Omarchy插件市场增长 Omarchy plugin marketplace growth

DHH

举个例子，假设你想要一个不同的日历。Omarchy 自带一个日历。你点击那个小时钟，就会弹出一个日历，但它不支持 iCal，也不读取你的日程。这类实现已经有大约 17 个了，而三天之内，Omarchy 插件市场上就有了 330 个插件。在我参与过的任何项目里，我都没见过这样的增长速度，没见过这么广泛的参与，也没见过这么多人能在这么短的时间内创造出对自己有意义、同时也能被别人使用的软件。这一切都源于 Omarchy 自带的一套技能，它告诉任何你带来的智能体如何为操作系统创建扩展，从而让你看到真正可塑的愿景……我差点要说智能体式了。我恨那个该死的词。我恨它的部分原因是，首先，它已经变成了营销废话。就像，什么东西都往上贴。我希望有个不同的词，就只是表示 AI 在做事。

For example, say you want a different calendar. Omarchy ships with a calendar. You click the little clock. It pops up a calendar, and it doesn't have iCal support, for example. It doesn't consume your appointments. There's about 17 implementations of that already, and in three days, we had 330 plugins on the Omarchy plugin marketplace. I have never seen growth like that with any project I've ever been involved in. I've never seen participation that broadly. I've never seen that many people be able to create software that's meaningful for them—also be usable for others so quickly. All of it is driven by the fact that Omarchy ships a set of skills that tells any agent you bring to it how to create extensions to the operating system, and therefore affording you the vision of the true malleable... I was about to say agentic. I hate that fucking word. And the reason in part I hate that word is, first of all, it's become marketing slob speak at this point. Like, it's just slapped onto everything. I wish we had a different word that just meant AI doing stuff.

I've written things in Rust. Rust. I hate Rust with a passion. Rust to me is like pouring acid in my eyes when I have to look at the code.

The memory safety, the fact that it's a system language, the fact that it's highly efficient and so forth is incredible. So Rust, to me, can both be the most repugnant programming language ever devised for human consumption and a wonderful platform for agentic engineering. Host

你觉得有没有可能有一天，我们干脆就把智能体式工程叫做编程？我们能不能直接改变编程的含义？因为几个月后，谁还会用老式方式编程呢？

Do you think that there can be a point at which we can just call agentic engineering programming? Can we just change what programming means? 'Cause who's actually going to be programming the old-school way in a few months?

DHH

我不认为我们应该重新使用这个词，因为对我来说，编程意味着对某些原语、循环、条件、变量的理解。编程语言的所有构造才是编程本身，而不是创建程序。因为在智能体时代之前，你可以说，你的 CEO 在编程。他雇了一堆程序员，告诉他们做什么，然后软件就出来了，他可以卖。呃，我不认为大多数人会称那个 CEO 为程序员，我也不认为我们应该称 vibe coder 为程序员。而 vibe coding，如果我们在这里定义它，就是你告诉一个智能体为你构建软件。你不看实现。对我来说，这就是 vibe coding 和编程，或者说，智能体加速开发之间的区别。

I don't think we should reuse the term because for me, programming implies an understanding of certain primitives, loops, conditions, variables. All the constructs of programming languages are what constitutes programming itself, not the creation of programs. Because you could say in the pre-agentic era, well, your CEO is programming. He's hiring a bunch of programmers, he's telling them what to do, and out comes software that he can sell. Eh, I don't think most people would call that CEO a programmer, and I don't think we should call the vibe coder a programmer either. And vibe coding, if we define it here, is you tell an agent to build software for you. You do not look at the implementation. That, to me, is what separates vibe coding from programming or, let's say, agent-accelerated development.

Host

但你不觉得，稍微反驳一下，你不觉得一个程序员，老式程序员，你，做智能体式工程，和一个非程序员做智能体式工程是不同的吗？我的意思是，如果你知道什么是 for 循环，如果你知道什么是函数式编程，如果你知道一些软件工程的基本良好原则，你做基于自然语言的智能体式工程的方式会不同，而且更系统化，而且你能实际构建的东西的规模和种类会比 vibe coder 大得多。

But don't you think, so to push back a little bit, don't you think a programmer, old-school programmer, you, doing agentic engineering is a different kind of agentic engineering than a non-programmer doing agentic engineering? What I mean is, if you know what a for loop is, if you know what function programming is, if you know some of the basic good principles of software engineering, the way you do natural language-based agentic engineering will be different and more systematic, and this, the scale and the variety of things you can actually build is much bigger than a vibe coder.

DHH

为了论证，我稍微扩展一下支持的观点。

I'm just gonna extend the pro case here a little bit just for the sake of the argument.

Host

对。

Right.

DHH

我实际上认为，有一段时间，我对编程的了解反而成了我的劣势，因为我指示智能体按照我规定的方式做事，它们非常擅长。在第一个智能体时刻，我们别叫它时代，它持续了三个月。时刻。在第一个智能体时刻，那感觉非常高效。我可以通过告诉智能体按照我会做的方式做事来获得更高的生产力，我利用了我作为程序员的所有经验来做到这一点。然后我有点晚了进入下一个时刻，下一个时刻允许我，允许任何人，描述结果，向智能体描述问题，并获得比程序员规定路径更好的解决方案。

I actually think for a while it was to my deficit to know as much as I know about programming because I was instructing the agents to do things as I prescribed them to do, and they were very good at that. And in the first agentic moment, let's not call it an era, it was something that lasted three months. Moment. In the first agentic moment, that felt very productive. I could get more productivity out of telling the agents what to do in the way I would've done it, and I used all of my experience as a programmer to do just that. Then I was a little late on the next moment, and the next moment allowed me, allowed anyone, to describe outcomes, to describe problems to the agents and get better solutions than if you had a programmer prescribe the path.

Host

看，好吧，我们在这里争论得很开心。那么你是在暗示，有些问题在智能体式工程上，程序员比非程序员更差吗？

See, okay, so we're having fun arguing here. Well so are you suggesting that there's problems for which programmers are worse than non-programmers at agentic engineering?

DHH

百分之百。我这么说，让我们在这里定义一下。我这么说的原因是，有很多程序员并不是很好的产品经理。软件就是产品管理。它应该做什么？为谁做？怎么做？看起来怎么样？我们的优先事项是什么？我们先从什么开始？第一版包含什么？所有这些技能并不是轻易或均匀地分布在所有程序员身上的。在智能体时代，你让智能体，AI，去做实现，这些才是你需要的技能。而我恰好同时属于两个阵营，这就是为什么我喜欢这个时刻。我仍然可以生活在旧世界里，看完整的实现，因为有些领域我觉得那仍然有价值，然后我也生活在未来。正如我所说，当我创建 Omawrite 时，我没有看过那 C++ 的一行代码。我实际上对自己强调，我要把它当作一个实验。这 100% 是一个黑盒。我会像任何其他用户一样对待它，他们对他们的数字打字机应该如何工作有意见，因此觉得这是一个公平的实验，就像任何其他类型的作家对软件应该如何做和如何工作有意见一样。正如你提到的，视频编辑者确切知道他们不喜欢 Adobe Premiere 的哪些地方，以及如何改进，但没有能力改进它，那会怎样。

100%. And the reason I say that, let's define it here. The reason I say that is there's a lot of programmers who are not very good product managers. Software is product management. What should it do? Who should it do it for? How should it do it? How should it look? What are our priorities? What do we start with first? What does version one include? All of those skills are not easily or equally distributed across all programmers. And in the agentic era where you are letting an agent, an AI, do the implementation, these are the skills you need. And I happen to sit in both camps, and this is why I love this moment. I still get to live a little bit in the old world where I look at the full implementation because there are domains and areas where I feel that still brings value, and then I also live in the future. And as I said, when I created Omawrite, I've not looked at a single line of that C++. I actually made it a point to myself that I was gonna treat it as an experiment. This is 100% a black box. I will treat it as though I was any other user who had opinions about how their digital typewriter should work, and therefore felt like it was a fair experiment as if a writer of any other sort who had opinions about how software should be done and how it should work. As you mentioned, video editors who know exactly where they don't like what Adobe Premiere's doing and how it could be better, but don't have the capacities to improve it, how that could be.

Host

不过，反驳一下，我认为程序员做得好的，优秀程序员做得好的，是系统化设计，比如从系统的角度思考——

The pushback, though, I think what programmers do well, good programmers do well, is systematic design, like think in terms of systems—

DHH

正确。

Correct.

Host

——以及严谨。

—and rigor.

DHH

是的。

Yes.

Host

而且我认为，可能在未来很长一段时间内，即使是基于自然语言控制智能体系统，也需要某种严谨性。不是过度结构化的严谨，而是真正非常具体地解释系统的目标，如何进行验证，什么样的安全测试。你想做所有这些事情。

And I think still, and probably for a long time to come, even natural language-based control of agentic systems will require a certain kind of rigor. Not overly structured rigor, but really explain very specifically the goals of the system, how to do the verification, what kind of security testing. You wanna do all those kinds of things.

代理最懂 Agents Know Best

DHH

虽然话又说回来，系统长期来看应该会更聪明。

Although then to push back on that, the system will... should be smart longer term.

Host

六个月前我也会这么说。

I would've said the same thing six months ago.

DHH

是啊，所以这已经变了。

Yeah, so this has changed.

Host

而且我觉得六个月前情况确实如此。但自从那以后，尤其是在过去几周用智能体加速开发 Mach-E 的过程中，我发现大多数时候，我不得不谦卑地承认，智能体最懂行。

And I think the same thing would've been true six months ago. What I have found since, especially even just the last few weeks of the agentically accelerated development of Mach-E, is that more often than not, I have the humility to recognize that the agent knows best.

DHH

你可以理直气壮地说：“确保它是安全的。”

You can legitimately say, "Make sure it's secure."

Host

没错。而且它更清楚这需要做什么。现在，你在笑——

Correct. And it would know more about what that entails. Now, you're laughing-

DHH

What a crazy world.

Host

……但这是真的。而且事实上——

...but it's true. And in fact-

DHH

天哪。

Host

……一个很好的类比就是 AGENTS.md 和 CLAUDE.md 文件。有一阵子，大家疯狂追求微调这些文件。哦，你告诉你的智能体这个，告诉它那个，最后你搞出一个巨大的指令文件。Boris 在 Claude Code 团队工作，他最近接受采访时提到 Opus 5 的一件事：他们为 Opus 5 发布的系统提示词（可能 Fable 也一样）缩减了 80%，因为智能体不仅需要的人类指令少得多，而且过度指令式的人类反而会损害它。任何经历过那种“尖头发老板”的程序员都深有体会。当老板走进房间，什么都不懂，却开始教你怎么写程序、怎么编码，你会怎么做？你会闷闷不乐。如果你被迫做违背自己判断的事情，你会写出更烂的代码。为什么智能体就不会这样呢？

...a good parallel to this is the AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md files. There was a hot moment where it was all the rage about micro-optimizing that. Oh, you tell your agent this, you tell your agent that, and you ended up with this huge file of instructions for your agent. One of the things that Boris, working on Claude Code, shared in an interview recently about Opus 5 was that the system prompt that they ship for Opus 5, and presumably also Fable, shrunk by 80% because the agent not only needed far less human instruction, it was actually being damaged by overly prescriptive humans. And any programmer who's had a pointy-haired boss knows exactly what that is like. When the boss walks into the room, doesn't know anything, starts telling you how to program, how to code. What do you do? You sulk. You write shittier code if you're mandated to do things that are against your better judgment. Why would an agent not be the same?

DHH

其实，我要反驳一下自己。我确实发现我目前正在培养的一项实际技能——在这方面，可能每个人都在努力培养的——就是不要过度指定细节，要给系统让路。

Actually, to push back against myself, I do find the actual skill I'm currently developing, which I along those lines is probably what everybody's trying to develop, is not to overspecify things, is to get out of the system's way.

Host

当然。

Of course.

DHH

就是指定足够的内容，提供一种高层次愿景和指导。

Is to specify enough to provide a kinda high-level vision and guidance.

Host

其实更好，因为你甚至不需要那样。现代软件开发的根本洞见来自敏捷软件开发运动。在 90 年代末、2000 年代初，一群聪明、诚实、勇敢的人聚在一起说：“我们过去 40 到 50 年尝试的软件开发方式没有奏效，也不会奏效。我们一直试图预先指定软件应该是什么样子。我们以为可以问人类想要什么，把它写下来，用系统思维和严谨性应用到规格说明书上，然后把规格说明书交给一群程序员，他们只实现那些内容，每个人都会满意。”结果呢？没人满意。因为没有人知道自己想要什么，直到他们得到它。你不知道程序应该做什么，直到你玩过它。所以在智能体时代，你应该抵制一开始就过度具体的诱惑。尽可能模糊地实现一些东西，然后与那个东西互动。你得到好软件的方式是，先写一点软件，然后尝试使用它。正是在使用软件的过程中，你发现自己真正想要什么。你发现什么重要，什么不重要。而智能体非常擅长让你在摸索中体验这种“意识到自己想要什么”的过程，因为你不知道自己想要什么。

It's even better because you don't even need that. The fundamental insight of modern software development came from the agile software development movement. In the late '90s, early 2000s, a group of smart, honest, and brave people came together and said, "The way we've been trying to do software development for the last 40 to 50 years has not worked, will not work. We've been trying to specify upfront what software should look like. We thought that we could ask humans what they wanted, write it all down, apply our systems thinking and our rigor to a spec sheet, hand that spec sheet over to a group of programmers who would then implement just that, and everyone would be happy." Well, guess what? No one was happy. Because no one knows what they want until they receive it. You don't know what a program should do until you play with it. So in the agentic age, you should resist the temptation to be overly specific upfront. Be as vague as you can to manifest something, then interact with the something. The way you arrive at good software is you write a little bit of software, and then you try to use it. It is in the process of using software that you discover what you really want. It's where you discover what's important and what's not important. And agents are incredible at allowing you to bumble into this experience of realizing what you want because you don't know what you want.

DHH

嗯。我确实做了类似你建议的事情，就是实现不同的设计和完全不同的实现，然后我为自己创建 HTML 页面、PHP 页面，在上面投票我更喜欢哪个，等等。它做这种迭代的事情，非常棒、非常有趣。与智能体系统的互动，如果你为自己创建一个好的界面，可以超级有趣，而且你专注于设计部分，以及设计中最有趣的部分。

Mm-hmm. And I've actually done something similar to what you suggested, which is implement different designs and totally different implementations, and then I create HTML pages for myself, PHP pages, where I vote on what I like more and so on. And it does this iterative thing, and it's very nice and fun. The interaction with the agentic system, if you create a nice interface for yourself, it can be super fun, and you're focusing on the design part and the most fun parts of the design part.

Host

没错。这正是人类真正擅长的。

Correct. And this is what humans are really good at.

DHH

是的，品味。

Yeah, taste.

Host

差异化评估。你给我三个选项，我选一个。但如果你给人类 22 个选项，他们就会崩溃。这就是选择的悖论。但如果你给他们三个选项，他们会在瞬间告诉你他们喜欢什么。有很多方法可以利用人类会做出这些快速判断的事实，这些判断实际上是前智力的，对吧？它们来自直觉，然后到达大脑，大脑试图理性化为什么直觉这么说。如果你愿意放弃一些智力上的预处理和理性化，只是让直觉驱动，智能体时代就是一个启示。

Differential evaluation. You give me three options, I pick one of them. Now, humans actually fall off a cliff if you give them 22 options. That's the paradox of choice. But you give them three options, they very quickly within a split second will tell you what they like. There are all these ways that exploit the fact that humans will make these snap judgments that are actually pre-intellectual, right? Like, they're coming from the gut, and then they arrive up in the, in the brain, and the brain tries to rationalize why the gut said what it said. And if you're willing to let go of some of that intellectual pre-processing rationalization and simply let your gut drive, the agentic age is a revelation.

Host

所以问你这个问题很有意思，因为你长期以来以细致雕琢漂亮的 Rails Ruby 代码而闻名，而现在你在几个月内就转变了，不再那样做了。那么，你目前的设置是什么样的？因为你，我猜，仍然在寻找美、雕琢、工艺，但你只是在用不同的媒介进行创作。

So this is a fascinating thing to ask you because you have sort of famously for a long time talked about, like, detailed chiseling of beautiful Rails Ruby code, and now you have switched in a matter of months to not doing that. So, like, what is your current setup look like? 'Cause you're, I assume, are still looking for the beauty, for the chiseling, for the crafting, but you're just crafting in a different medium.

DHH

而且在不同的抽象层次上。当我在写 Ruby 代码时——我们有很多 Ruby 代码，因为它遍布整个业务——我让智能体修改那些代码，我仍然会纠结细节。我逐渐意识到，这种纠结的经济回报正在迅速减少。我 25 年来如此谨慎地纠结每一行代码，是因为我知道保持架构连贯和可塑的回报是：软件能够以小团队快速变化和演进，成本不高，而且当你改动一个地方时不会引入一堆 bug。那是为什么你应该写漂亮代码的经济论据，因为漂亮的代码更容易理解、更简单、更可塑。那是基于人类进行修改的前提。我认为这在多大程度上仍然重要，是一个开放的问题。现在，它确实重要，我这么说是因为，至少目前，token 仍然稀缺。在当下，我们都受到 token 限制。好吧，不是所有人，但任何没有无限预算的人都受到 token 限制。因此，编写那些智能体更容易处理、更容易演进而不必重新学习整个上下文的系统，有巨大的回报。就像人类一样，如果他们能够在不破坏架构的情况下对代码库进行迭代和修改，他们就能以与上次相同的成本进行下一次修改。这就是经典的“泥球”问题，你最终得到一个泥球系统，因为它只是以一种没有连接的方式拼凑在一起，真是一团糟，对吧？我在智能体身上看到过这种情况，也在我们自己的代码库中看到过，比如第一个 PR 质量平庸，然后你在上面加另一个 PR，再加上五个，结果就不太好了，对吧？所以仍然有回报，但那个回报是以我们当前时刻为前提的。

And at different levels of abstraction. When I'm working in Ruby code, and we have a lot of Ruby code because it's in our entire business, and I'm asking agents to make changes to that code, I still sweat the details. What I'm coming to realize is that the economic payoff of that sweat is diminishing rapidly. The reason why I, for 25 years, was sweating every line of code so judiciously was because I knew the payoff of keeping an architecture coherent and malleable was software that could change and evolve quickly with a small team, and not exorbitant cost, and not introducing a bunch of bugs when you change one thing over the other. That was the driving economic argument for why you should write beautiful code, 'cause beautiful code is easier to understand, it is simpler, it is more malleable. That was premised on humans doing the modifications. I think it is an open question to which degree this still matters. Now, it does matter, and the reason I say that, at least for the moment, is that tokens are still scarce. At this moment in time, we are all token limited. Well, not all, but anyone who doesn't have endless budgets are token limited. So therefore, there is great payoff to writing systems that agents have an easier time dealing with and evolving without having to relearn the entire context. Just like humans, if they can make iterations and changes to the code base without wrecking the architecture, they can make the next change just as cheaply as the last one. And this is the classic ball of mud where you end up with a system that's a ball of mud because it's just put together in a way where nothing is connected and it's just a real mess, right? I've seen that with agents, and I've seen it in our own code bases where, again, the first PR is, like, mediocre of quality, and then if you add another PR on top of that, and then five more down the line, it's not very good, right? So there's still a payoff to that, but that payoff is premised on our current moment.
