... continues.

DHH

我们孕育了这一刻。多么荣幸，我个人深有感触，因为我……我写过的几乎所有代码都是公开代码。我职业生涯的绝大部分时间都致力于编写开源代码，因此，其中一些代码，那些美丽的行，都在训练集中。事实上，我听说人们在写 Ruby 代码时会这样做。他们让智能体“像 DHH 那样写”。他们对结果很满意，这确实让我心里暖暖的，我以我微小的部分帮助孕育了这一刻。任何贡献过开源代码或任何智能体在过去（好吧，编程作为一门学科存在的整个时期）能够访问的代码的人，现在都是这些新智能体霸主的一部分。

We gave birth to this moment. What a privilege, and I feel that very personally because I... Almost all of the code I've ever written is public code. The vast majority of my career has been dedicated to writing open source code, and therefore, some of that code, some of those beautiful lines are in the training set. And in fact, I've heard people do this when they write Ruby code. They ask the agent, "Write it like DHH would." And they're pleased with the result, and that does warm my heart a little bit, that I helped give birth to this moment in my tiny little small part. And anyone else who contributed open source code or any code at all that the agents have had access to over the last, well, entire time programming has been around as a discipline, is now part of these new agent overlords.

Host

那么，你难道没有一点点悲伤，手写美丽代码现在越来越没用了？

So you don't have a little bit of sadness that writing beautiful code by hand is now less and less useful?

DHH

我是否悲伤，如果我明天必须找一份工作，我不太可能以我的技能被雇佣去写这些手工代码？不。我做了 25 年。我想在这里看到一些新的东西，尤其是如果我们都会永生，我希望我们不会。也许我们会谈到那个。但是，我已经做过了。足够了。没关系。我对那件事的怀旧，并不比对我不用在田里用锄头或在装配线上度过时光的事实更多。

Am I sad that if I had to get a job tomorrow, it's unlikely that I could apply my skills in such a way that I would be paid to write these manual lines of code? No. I did it for 25 years. Like, I wanna see something new here, especially if we're all gonna live forever, which I hope we don't. Maybe we'll get to that. But, like, I've done that. That's enough. That's fine. I don't feel any more nostalgic about that than I do about the fact that I don't have to spend my time in the field with a hoe or at the assembly line.

Host

嗯，等一下。你是世界上最擅长写手工美丽代码的程序员之一，是品味高雅的美感欣赏者。

Well, hold on a second. You are one of the best programmers in the world at writing handcrafted beautiful code, appreciators of beauty with great taste.

DHH

正确。

Correct.

Host

那已经被取代了。

That has been replaced.

DHH

我谦虚地说。

I say humbly.

Host

是的。那已经被取代了。就像，你现在……你只是快速进化，你能够……你一个真正伟大的品质是你能够快速改变主意并进化。但是，你不得不杀死那个另一个人。

Yeah. That has been replaced. Like, you had now... You're just fast evolving, and you're able to... One of your really great qualities is you're able to change your mind and evolve very quickly. But, like, you had to kill that other person.

DHH

我对每一个把我带到现在的时刻都心存感激，因此，我也尝试过，也许这确实需要一些练习。我承认这一点。也许我并不总是这样，但斯多葛哲学，amor fati，热爱你的命运，是一种令人难以置信的解放生活方式。你可以攻击这一点，说：“嗯，那是一种特权。”没错，但在我没有所有这些所谓的“特权”时，我也有同样的方向。所以我认为有一种生活和与世界互动的方式，你接受你能改变的事情和你不能改变的事情，并爱上所有的一切。

I am grateful for every moment that brought me to where I am right now, and therefore, I have also tried, and maybe this did take some practice. I will concede that. Maybe I wasn't always like this, but stoic philosophy, amor fati, loving your fate, is an incredibly liberating way to live. And you could attack that and say, "Well, that's a point of privilege." True, but also I had the same directional point when I didn't have all this, quote unquote, "privilege." So I think there's a way to live and interact with the world where you accept the things you can change and the things you cannot change and fall in love with all of it.

程序员焦虑与建议 Anxiety and Advice for Programmers

Host

那我们能谈谈程序员在经历同样变革时感受到的普遍焦虑吗？他们可能上了大学，主修计算机科学，梦想成为程序员，构建东西，拿高薪，而现在一切都在改变，他们深感焦虑：“我该怎么办……我的人生怎么办？”你能理解这种感受吗？他们到底该怎么做？

So can we talk about the general anxiety that programmers feel, going through the same transformation? They've maybe gone to university majored in computer science, dreamed of being programmers and building stuff, high salary, and now everything's changing, and there's deep anxiety about, "What do I do when... with my life?" Can you empathize with that, and what the hell are they supposed to do?

DHH

非常能理解。因为尽管我有这种性格，我清楚地知道这种乐观并非人人都有，不是每个人都能站在这个位置，对未来抱有这种乐观。但我确实想把事情稍微分开来看。我认为，如果你对编程的热爱仅仅在于那些机械的部分——把正确的逻辑结构拼凑起来，产出别人让你产出的东西——那你确实很难适应新的现实。因为那个机械过程正受到威胁。但如果你像你刚才提到的，对构建东西本身感到兴奋，我认为你根本不受威胁。事实上，我认为有充分的理由说明我们比现在需要更多的构建者。如果你看就业数据，其实很模糊，完全不清楚会发生什么。有些数据实际上显示职位空缺在增加，因为 AI 的出现极大地降低了程序的价格，人们想要更多的程序。这就是经典的杰文斯悖论：当某样东西的价格下降时，需求会增加。ATM 的例子也是如此。当初 ATM 刚出现时，很多银行柜员非常担心自己的工作，因为突然有了一种机器可以从人们的账户里取钱。但 ATM 所做的是降低了开设分支机构的成本，银行突然能开更多的分支机构，最终我们拥有的银行柜员比之前还多。当然，这些都不是必然的。也有事情确实改变的时刻。直到 19 世纪末，绝大多数人都在田里劳作，然后突然我们有了机械化收割工具，不再需要人拿着锄头牵着牛在田里干活了。我们有机械工具来替代。在那个时刻，有没有人感到自己的工作、生计受到威胁？有。那种威胁感合理吗？合理。英国砸毁织布机的卢德分子也经历了同样的事。我认为这可能比田间劳作的例子更贴切，因为那些人是高技能的专业人士，在相当优越的条件下做着自己喜欢的工作，而不是整天在烈日下辛苦劳作。但看看我们现在的生活，我们还希望服装成为如此稀缺的资源吗？比如，如果这件 T 恤要 400 美元呢？好吧，那我可能只有两件。我的衣柜看起来大概也就两件。但我认为人类的整体进步依赖于生产力的提高。什么是生产力提高？这非常重要，因为抽象地说，每个人都会说：“耶，生产力，那很好。”你以为这只是空谈吗？不。生产力意味着用更少的人完成同样数量或同样的工作。当然，你想要完成的工作量可能会增加，因此需要更多人，但也可能不会。可能会有一些公司只需要完成一组固定的任务，突然他们能用十分之一的人手完成。顺便说一句，这对被裁员的个人来说，当下是悲惨和困难的。但对整个经济来说，这也很惊人。突然你释放了这些资源，他们可以去从事更有生产力的事情。这就是整个经济演进和改善的方式，也是我们获得增长的方式，而增长是好事。我认为这是另一件需要大力辩护的事情：增长总体上是好事。我们不想把时钟拨回 1920 年或 1950 年。我们应该感到兴奋的是，我们发现了新技术，让我们能做更多的事情。虽然有些被 AI 取代的任务是人类喜欢做的，只是不再有经济价值，但很多任务只是苦差事。我的编程经历肯定如此。有些编程时刻我觉得很棒，那些时刻产生了我们上次谈到的“心流”状态，令人欣喜若狂。但一年中这样的时刻有多少次？如果你拿一个普通程序员来说，在 2000 小时的工作时间里，有多少时间处于心流状态？100 小时？200 小时？25 小时？我觉得这些答案对某些人来说都有可能。把那些苦差事交给机器，这就是文明的历史。

Hugely. Because just because I have this disposition, I'm keenly aware that that's not evenly distributed, that not everyone sits with this position and this optimism for the future. But I do think I wanna separate things a little bit. I think you're gonna have a hard time coping with the new reality if the only thing you loved about programming was the mechanical bits of putting the right logical constructs together to produce something other people told you to produce. Because that mechanical process is under threat. If you are, as you just mentioned, excited about building things, I don't think you're under threat at all. In fact, I think there's a great argument for us needing far more builders than what we have now, and if you look at the employment stats, it's fuzzy. It's not clear what's gonna happen at all. Some stats actually show an increase in openings because the advent of AI is so dramatically lowering the price of programs that people want a lot more programs. This is the classic, the Jevons paradox, that says when the price of something goes down, there's gonna be more demand for it. And this is the example of the ATMs too. When the ATM originally came, a lot of bank tellers were very afraid for their job because suddenly there was a machine that could dispense money from people's account. Well, what the ATMs did was lower the price of a branch, and suddenly banks could afford to open a lot more branches, and we ended up with more bank tellers than we did before. Now, none of this is guaranteed. There are also moments where things do change. The vast majority of people worked in the fields up until the late 1800s, and then suddenly we got mechanized harvesting tools, and we did not need human labor with a hoe and an ox out there. We had mechanical tools to do that for us. In that moment, are there people who were threatened that their job and their livelihood was at stake? Yes. Was that a reasonable thing to feel threatened about? Yes. The Luddites smashing the weaving machines in England had the same thing. And I think maybe that's the better parallel than people working the fields because these were actually highly skilled professionals doing a job that they liked on rather favorable conditions, not slaving outside in the sun all day. But where we live now, would we still like for clothing to be this heavily constrained resource? Like, what if this T-shirt was $400? I mean, okay, then I'd have two. Well, my wardrobe probably looks like I have two. But I think the general progress of mankind depends on productivity improvements. And what are productivity improvements? This is very important because in the abstract, I think everyone says, "Yay, productivity. That's good." What did you think, this was all just vibes? No. Productivity means fewer people to do the same number or the same job. Now, the amount of job you want done may increase, and therefore you get more people, but it also may not. There may be pockets where a company just needs a certain set of fixed tasks done, and suddenly they can do them with a 10th the number of people. This, by the way, could... is tragic and difficult in the moment for the individual being laid off. It's also amazing for the economy at large. Suddenly you've freed up these resources who can now go do more productive things. This is how the whole economy evolves and improves and how we get growth, and growth is good. I think this is the other thing to make a strong defense for, that growth in general is a good thing. We do not want to wind the clock back to 1920 or 1950. We should be excited about the fact that we've discovered new technology that allows us to do vastly more. And while some of the tasks that may be taken over by AI were tasks that humans were excited to do, and they're just no longer economically viable, a lot of the tasks were just drudgery. This is certainly true of my programming. There were moments of programming I thought were amazing. These were the moments that produced that state of flow we talked about last time, and they were ecstatic. How often did they happen out of the course of a year? If you took your average programmer, how much time out of 2,000 hours at the job did that person spend in a flow state? 100? 200? 25? I think all those answers could be plausible for someone. And taking that drudgery, handing it over to machines, that is the history of civilization.

Host

是啊，那些花在调试上的时间。编程里有太多痛苦的时刻。现在它们消失了。大部分时候，编程就只是有趣了。

Yeah, I mean, the hours spent debugging. So programming has so many painful, painful hours in it. And now they're gone. For the... I mean, for the most part, it's just fun.

DHH

在很多领域，大部分痛苦已经消失了，剩下的可能很快也会消失。所以浪漫主义也由此而来……因为当下的痛苦是艰难的、困难的、烦人的。而后视镜里的痛苦则是成就、骄傲、学习、进步，所有这些，对吧？所以我们把这一切都视为人类经验的一部分：我们讨厌当下的痛苦，但当痛苦过去五分钟，我们不记得艰辛，只记得进步时，我们又珍视它。

A lot of it is gone in a lot of domains, and the rest of it may be gone very soon. So that's where some of the romanticism also comes in ... because pain in the moment is difficult. It's hard. It's annoying. Pain in the rear view mirror is accomplishment, proud, learning, steps forward, all the things, right? So we encompass all of this as part of the human experience, that we hate pain in the moment, but we cherish it when it's five minutes past and we don't remember the hardship. We just remember the progress.

Host

所以确实，在社会层面、宏观经济层面，增长是令人兴奋的。但在个人层面，可能会有很多焦虑，甚至很多痛苦。那么作为建议，如果你是一个年轻的 DHH，或者现在年轻的开发者、程序员，你会建议他们怎么做？

So certainly at the societal level, at the macroeconomic level, growth is exciting. But the individual level, there's going to be a lot of anxiety potentially a lot of suffering. So by way of advice, if you're like a young DHH or young developer programmer now, what would you advise they do?

DHH

不要试图预测任何事。你真的会疯掉。因为即使是业内最聪明的头脑也无法预测从现在起两次模型迭代之后会是什么样子。这完全是浪费时间，而且你会因为试图推断两年后的样子而患上 AI 精神病。专注于当下，而当下是从事计算机行业最不可思议的时期。如果你投入其中，你可以让计算机做出最惊人的事情。如果你哪怕短暂地强迫自己去了解最前沿的技术，我敢打赌你不可能不为可能的事情感到兴奋。

Don't try to anticipate anything. You will literally go crazy. Because even the smartest brains in the business cannot anticipate what two model hops from here is going to look like. It's an absolute waste of time, and you will develop an AI psychosis trying to deduce what two years from now is gonna look like. Focus on right now, and right now is the most incredible time to be into computers. You can make them do the most amazing things if you lean in. If you maybe for a hot moment force yourself to learn where the state of the art is, I double dog dare you not to get excited about what's possible.

Host

那你会推荐公开构建吗？

And would you recommend building publicly?

DHH

我认为公开构建其实是可选的。这样做很好，因为你能成为社区的一部分，开源中有一些同志情谊。不只是“一些”，而是大量的同志情谊。开源是社区和同志情谊的绝佳来源，也是缓解个人存在主义恐惧的好方法。

I think it's optional actually to build publicly. I think it's nice to do because then you get to be part of a community, and there's some camaraderie in open source. Not some camaraderie. There's an enormous amount of camaraderie. Open source is an incredible source of community and camaraderie, and it's also a great way to stem some of that personal existential dread.

思维病毒与雄心 Mind Viruses and Ambition

DHH

如果你置身于其他人类之中，你可能会感染上好的思维病毒、令人兴奋的思维病毒——那些告诉你一切都会好起来的、未来一片光明的病毒，它们说如果我们齐心协力建造一堆东西，我们就能做到我们谁都不敢梦想的事。我刚才还在和 Ryan Hughes 聊天，他几乎从第一天起就是我 Omarchy 的主要搭档，我们昨天还在聊，当初一起踏上这段旅程时，我们的野心非常小。我们当时就想：“能不能让这个发行版不要在凌晨 3 点因为一个不受我们控制的 Arch 包推送而崩溃？那就太棒了，那就太好了。”现在我们往后一靠，开始想：“我们怎么才能统治世界？怎么才能让 Omarchy 登上火星？怎么才能实现我们所有的梦想？”这种野心的膨胀，一部分是因为智能体变得更好了，但很大程度上也是因为我们身边围绕着一群对同一段旅程充满热情的人。如果你只是坐在自己那个与世隔绝的小洞穴里担心未来，那你确实会有点发疯。我觉得如果说我们从新冠中学到了什么，那就是人们独自坐在自己的小洞穴里担心会发生什么，会发疯的。那和抑郁没什么区别，没完没了地反刍那些你控制不了的事。抱歉我要借用一下黄仁勋的话，但那纯粹是失败者言论。你不必当失败者。你可以选择投身其中，然后赢。而“赢”的定义非常宽泛：学到更多、赚到更多、贡献更多、参与更多。归根结底，你还有什么选择？你没得选，伙计。未来不管你喜不喜欢都会来，所以你不如选择对它感到兴奋。

If you're amongst other humans, you can get infected with good forms of mind viruses, exciting forms of mind viruses, the ones that tell you that it's all gonna be okay, and the future looks bright, and if we build a bunch of things together, we can do things none of us ever dreamed we could do. I was just talking to Ryan Hughes, who's been my main partner on Omarchy almost since day one, and we were literally talking about yesterday how when we started on this journey together, our ambitions were very modest. We were just like, "Can we get this distro to not crash at 3:00 AM because of an Arch package that just got pushed out outside of our control? That would be amazing. Then it'd be great." Now we're leaning back and thinking, "How can we take over the world? How can we make Omarchy go to Mars? How can we do everything we've ever dreamed of?" And that level of ambition inflation is partly because things have gotten better with agents, but hugely also because we've surrounded ourselves with others excited about the same journey. And if you're just sitting in your little isolated cave worrying about the future, yeah, you're gonna go a little nuts. I think if we learned anything during COVID is that people will go nuts sitting in their little cave inside by themselves worrying about what's gonna happen. That is indistinguishable from depression, ruminating endlessly about things you can't control. I'm sorry to channel some Jensen Huang here, but that's just loser talk. You don't have to be a loser. You can choose to lean in and win. And win is very broadly defined as learning more, making more, contributing more, being part of more. And at the end of the day, what are your choices? You don't have a choice, mate. The future's coming whether you like it or not, so you might as well choose to be excited about it.

规划不可能 Planning is Impossible

Host

但我的意思是，我们应该强调一个事实：有了 AI，规划几乎是不可能的。我们大多数人都会稍微规划一下自己的人生。年轻的时候，你有希望和梦想。你上大学是有原因的，背后有一个计划。

But I mean, we should emphasize the fact that planning is nearly impossible with AI. I mean, most of us plan our life a little bit. When we're younger, you have, like, hopes and dreams. There's a reason you go to college. There's a plan underlying that.

DHH

我同意。

I agree with that.

Host

但是——

But there-

DHH

这点我承认。

I'll concede that.

Host

……真的没法好好规划，这有点疯狂。因为，你根本不知道六个月后还会不会有 Claude Code、Codex 和 Cursor。也许你会对着 WhatsApp 说话。也许一切都变成开放式调用。也许一切都变成语音。也许 Omarchy 会统治世界，然后就是语音。也许操作系统都不复存在了。你就……会有一个发光球体，你直接对它说话，因为如果自然语言足以完成生活中的所有任务，那生活还有什么意义？生活中需要完成的主要任务是什么？如果你光靠语音就能做到，它帮你写好所有程序，一切都系统化了，那还需要什么工作？也许更多是服务业。也许根本就不会有程序员，比如智能体工程师。所以，是的，你专注于当下的建议是明智的，但这并不意味着这只是一趟疯狂的过山车，因为我们不知道今年 12 月或明年 1 月会是什么样子。

...it is kinda nuts that it's impossible to really plan. Because, like, you really don't know if there will even be a Claude Code, a Codex, and Cursor six months from now. Maybe it'll be you'll be talking to WhatsApp. Maybe it'll be all open call type. Maybe it'll just all be voice. Maybe Omarchy will take over the world and it'll be voice. Maybe there won't be operating systems anymore. You'll just... There'll be this glowing orb that you just talk to because if natural language is enough to accomplish all tasks in life, like, what is life about? Like, what are the major tasks you need to accomplish in life? If you can just do that with voice, and it writes all the programs for you, and all is systematized, and what, what are the jobs needed then? Maybe it's more about the service industry. Maybe there won't be programmers, like agentic engineers at all. And so yes, your advice is wise to focus on the moment, but that doesn't mean that it's just a crazy rollercoaster ride, 'cause we don't know what this December or what this January will look like.

选择信念而非焦虑 Choosing Faith over Anxiety

DHH

我们不知道，因此，我们应该选择相信它会成功。这是我对抗焦虑最好的建议：在很大程度上，你可以选择如何倾斜。你想倾向 PDoom 吗？不管你倾不倾向，PDoom 都会发生。你控制不了。

We don't know, and therefore, we should choose to have faith that it's gonna pan out. That is my best advice towards fighting anxiety, is that you have a choice, to a large degree, of how to lean. Do you wanna lean into PDoom? PDoom is gonna happen whether you lean into it or not. You don't have control.

寻找永恒 Finding Timelessness

Host

但还有实际的时间表：你做了多少学习，做了多少构建，做了多少多元化。比如，我最近穿越了中国农村，我这么做是相当刻意的，因为你在和中国农村的人交谈，那里时间流逝得慢得多，没有人讨论智能体工程。我一开始去了中国的长城。那是永恒的，跨越了几个世纪。你知道，当时有一种 FOMO 的紧张感，好像我错过了什么。但后来我非常刻意地想要找到人类存在的永恒性，真正去体会这样一个事实：好吧，在整个人类历史上，一切似乎总是在变化，但你知道，那些关于生活中什么重要的普遍真理仍然成立。我只是想保持与那种永恒的联系。

But there's also actual daily schedule of how much learning are you doing, how much building are you doing, how much diversification you're doing. And for example, I traveled across rural China recently, and I did it quite deliberately because you're talking to people in China, in rural anywhere, where time moves much slower, where there's no discussions about agentic engineering. And I started by going on the Great Wall of China. That's timeless. That spans centuries. And, you know, there was, like, this kind of tension of FOMO, like maybe I'm missing out on something. But then I very deliberately wanted to find the timelessness of human existence, really plug into the fact that, okay, it always feels like everything's changing throughout human history but, you know, the universals are still true, of what matters in life. And I just wanted to keep connected to that.

两周即可赶上 You Can Catch Up in Two Weeks

DHH

你不会错过任何东西。这是转型早期真正让我恼火的部分。每个人都那么激动，好像如果你不……现在是循环了。哦，不，不，循环已经过时了。现在是图了。哦，不，不，那个也过时了。它利用这个，对吧？他们不断在前沿翻腾，从某种意义上说这其实非常令人兴奋。这就是一个新领域开启时会发生的事。就像一个新传送门，所有东西都涌出来。这很令人兴奋。但这也意味着，如果我过去一年一直在背包旅行，没碰过电脑，没见证这个智能体时刻，然后我昨天才出现，你知道吗？我两周就能赶上。没有任何积累，这在某种程度上是一种巨大的解脱。如果你错过了过去一年，你可以在两周内赶上前沿。如果你是一个程序员，去喜马拉雅山徒步了一年，回来之后，你可以在两周内赶上。这实际上是对我们所经历的进步的巨大赞誉。我们现在同时运行着这么多实验，它们不断无情地筛选什么有效、什么无效。你不必记住甚至不必参与整个旅程。你只需要来看结果就行。

You're not gonna miss anything. This is the part that actually grinded my gears in the early phases of this transition. Everyone was so up in arms that if you didn't... It's loops now. Oh, no, no, we're done with loops. It's graphs now. Oh, no, no, we're done with that. It's, it harnesses this, right? They're constantly churning through the frontier, which in one way is actually very exciting. This is what happens when a new field opens up. It's like a new portal, and it's all spilling out. That's exciting. But it also means that if I had just been backpacking for the last year, hadn't touched a computer, hadn't witnessed this agentic moment, and I just showed up yesterday, do you know what? I would've been caught up in two weeks. There's not any accumulation, which is in some ways a great relief. If you missed the past year, you can catch up to the frontier in two weeks. If you're a programmer who was out hiking the Himalayas for a year and you come back, you can catch up in two weeks. And this is actually the great credit to the progress we're experiencing. We have so many experiments running simultaneously right now that are constantly and ruthlessly sorting what works, what doesn't work. You don't have to remember or even be part of that entire journey. You can just show up for the results.

成为新人类 Becoming a New Human

Host

但关键的一步是，当你从背包旅行回来时，要愿意成为一个全新的、不同的人，因为事情变化太快了。

But the key step there, when you come back from the backpacking journey, is to be willing to become a totally new, different human because things are changing so fast.

DHH

没错。

That's right.

Host

我的意思是，真的，如果你去年 10 月出发去背包旅行，4 月或 5 月回来……

I mean, literally, if you left for the backpacking journey in October last year and came back in April or May...

DHH

那你简直就像在冷冻舱里待了 100 年。

You might as well have been in the cryo chamber for 100 years.

Host

……就像，“什么，你什么意思？我们不再编程了？”这是一次巨大的转变。

...it's like, "What, what do you mean? We're not programming anymore?" This, it's a huge transformation.

哀悼旧世界 Grieving the Old World

DHH

这很惊人，而且我实际上……我是说，我稍微有点夸张。我也承认，短暂地悲伤一下是可以的。那不是我的性格，我不会那样做，但我接受这是一种经过时间考验的应对失落感的机制。你对曾经熟悉、也许非常喜爱的世界不复从前感到失落，这也是可以的。

It's startling, and I actually... I mean, I'm putting some of this on slightly for effect. I also recognize that it's okay to grieve for a hot moment. That's not my disposition. It's not how I do it, but I accept that that's a time-tested mechanism for coping with a sense of loss. And it's okay to have a sense of loss that the world that you knew and maybe you were very fond of is no longer the same.

Host

也许我应该大声明确地说，我确实有那种感觉，就像那个流着泪的印第安人……有一种悲伤。这是一场告别。是对旧编程世界的告别。

I maybe I should say out loud and explicitly that I do have that, the Native American with a tear rolling down my... Like, there's a sadness. It's a goodbye. It's a goodbye to the old world of programming.

DHH

这对我来说太迷人了。

That's, it's so fascinating to me.

AI之旅 The Journey to AI

DHH

我花了这么多年——但这一切都汇聚到了这一刻。对我来说，这就是让整个旅程如此有意义的原因。AI 并非从天而降，也不是某种穿越宇宙、突然出现在我们海岸上的外星科技。AI 和计算机科学一样历史悠久。在 50 年代，当时最聪明的人认为这个问题大约十年内就能解决。结果花了更长时间，而部分原因在于我们在通往 AI 和神经网络的道路上走了一些弯路，以为其他符号表示方法会胜出，结果没有。那大概让我们倒退了 15 年。所以部分原因也是我们必须经历游戏革命、3D 游戏。如果没有《雷神之锤》，没有《毁灭公爵》，没有《虚幻竞技场》，我们永远不会有 AI，因为我们永远不会有 GPU，因此也永远无法以现在的形态获得 AI。所以直到这一刻我们所做的一切，都是到达那里所必需的。

I've spent so many years—but it all amounted to this moment. That is, to me, what makes the entire journey so meaningful. AI did not arrive from the sky. It was not alien technology that blasted through the universe and suddenly showed up on our shores. AI has been around for as long as computer science has been around. In the '50s, they thought the problem was gonna be cracked in about a decade, the smartest people at the time. It took a little longer, but the reason it took a little longer was in part because we took some blind turns on the road to AI and neural networks and thought that there were other symbolic representations that were gonna carry the day, and they didn't. And that probably set us back about 15 years. So some of it was also we had to go through the gaming revolution, 3D games. If we had not had Quake, if we had not had Duke Nukem, if we had not had Unreal Tournament, we'd never have gotten AI because we would never have gotten the GPUs, and therefore we would never have been able to get AI in the shape it is now. So everything we did up until this moment was required to get there.

Host

你是其中一部分。

You were part of that.

DHH

天哪……多么幸运。能贡献自己的一份力量，无论是通过训练还是玩电子游戏，都是多么幸运。真的，你可以选择这样想。对我来说，90 年代那些花在玩电子游戏上的时间，它们——

Holy... What a blessing. What a blessing to have contributed in your small part, either by training or by playing video games. Literally, you could just choose to think of it that way. All those times you spent in, for my case, in the '90s just playing video games, they were—

Host

你在做贡献。

You were contributing.

DHH

是的，我当时就在为 AI 革命做贡献。

Yeah, I was contributing to the AI revolution right there and then moment.

Host

为人类文明的进步。

To the progress of human civilization.

DHH

没错，当然。文明随着旧事物的消亡和新事物的诞生而进步，它就这样继续着，但我觉得，悲伤的过程仍然是其中的一部分。

No, for sure. Civilization progresses with the death of the old and the birth of the new, and it continues in this way, but it's still the grieving process is a part of it, I think.

Host

花点时间。没关系。花点……

Take a moment. It's okay. Take a...

DHH

而且，你甚至不必接受一切都已消亡。比如，如果你仍然非常留恋手工雕刻，如果你一年前问我，我会预测我会更加留恋。我很惊讶——

And also, you don't even have to accept that it's all dead. Like, if you are still very attached to the hand chiseling, which if you'd asked me a year ago, I would have predicted that I would've been more attached. I'm surprised—

Host

我有点惊讶——

I'm kinda surprised—

DHH

……有点惊讶我没有更加留恋。但我觉得，我没有更加留恋的原因是我找到了更有趣的东西。如果不是这样，如果 AI 只是取代了我热爱的东西，却给了我讨厌的东西，我可能会有点痛苦。

... a little that I've not been more attached. But the reason I've not been more attached, I think, is that I found something more fun. And if it hadn't been like that, if AI had just replaced the thing I loved and gave me something I hated, I'd probably be a little bitter.

Host

我的意思是，这真的感觉……看着既好笑又精彩，也很鼓舞人心。就像毕加索突然获得了图像生成能力，变得超级兴奋，几个月内就转型了。这确实发生了。所以，就像一个欣赏优美代码的艺术家，突然你开始谈论乐趣——

I mean, this really feels... It's hilarious and awesome to watch and inspiring to watch. It's like Picasso all of a sudden like, getting access to image generation and getting super excited and switching in a matter of months. That's literally what happened. So, like, it's an artist that appreciated beautiful code, and all of a sudden you're talking about having fun—

DHH

我觉得毕加索其实是个很好的类比，因为如果你看他早期的作品，他学习技艺的时候，画的是写实画，对吧？他训练自己成为描绘现实的大师，用我们当时知道的最佳方式。然后出现了各种其他表达形式，立体主义出现了。所有这些抽象表达形式，他并没有哀叹自己不再画文艺复兴风格的画，不再只是画一个完美的苹果。他重新想象苹果，把它变成一个该死的方块，并为此兴奋不已。我感受到了那种转变，我们从一种表达模式，突然打开了大门，色彩光谱变得如此宽广。我看到了更多。这也许是因为我成为程序员的方式。我成为程序员并不是因为对 if 语句的深爱，而是因为我想要程序。我想要存在不存在的东西。起初，我必须学习编程来实现这一点。然后我碰巧爱上了编程这门手艺，接着我花了二十年深入钻研。但现在我回来了。我回到了有一个想法，就迫不及待地想看到它出现在世界上的状态，而 AI 把这段时间缩短到了几乎为零。所以这是重新发现的一种加速。

I think Picasso is actually a good parallel because if you look at his early work where he learned his craft, it was painting realistic paintings, right? Like, he was training to be a master of the old ways of depicting reality the best way we knew how, and then along comes all these other forms of expression. Cubism comes along. And all these forms of abstract expression, he's not decrying that he's not doing Renaissance paintings anymore, that he's not just painting the perfect depiction of an apple. He's reimagining the apple to be a freaking square, and he's getting excited about that. And I feel that transition, that we've gone from this one mode of expression, and we've suddenly opened the gates where the, like, the color spectrum is so much wider. I'm seeing more. And it's perhaps because the way I became a programmer. I did not become a programmer because a deep love of if statements. I became a programmer because I wanted programs. I wanted things to exist that did not exist. And at first, I had to learn how to program to make that happen. Then I happened to fall in love with programming as a craft, and then I spent two decades really diving deep on that. But now I'm back. I'm back to having an idea and being impatient beyond belief to see it exist in the world, and AI has just shrunk that down to almost nothing. So it's an acceleration of a rediscovery.

Host

我明白，这对每个人来说并非如此。有很多程序员从一开始就热爱编程，他们被逻辑结构等吸引。我在那里待了二十年，所以我理解。但我也认为，你能够发现自己的新一面——

Now, I understand that that's not true for everyone. There are plenty of programmers who just loved programming right from the beginning, and they were attracted to the logical constructs and so forth. I mean, I've been there for two decades, so I get it. But I also think that you're able to discover new sides of yourself—

DHH

……如果你让它进来。我一直在回到这个近乎“遗憾最小化”的框架，借用杰夫·贝索斯的话。两年后你会看着自己，心想“我把时间花在对现状、对行业改变这件事上发脾气，真是值得”吗？还是两年后回顾这一刻，为自己积极投入、或许有过片刻悲伤而感到自豪？再说一次，这一切都有空间。然后继续，“我要学习世界现在如何运转，我不仅要做到最好，我还要创造更多。”

... if you let it in. Again, I keep coming back to this almost regret minimization framework, to borrow Jeff Bezos's terms here. Are you gonna look at yourself two years from now and think, "Oh, I spent my time well being pissy about the present, about the fact that my industry changed"? Or would I two years from now look back upon this moment and be proud of myself for leaning in, for maybe having a moment of grief? Again, there's room for all of it. And then going, "I'm gonna learn how the world spins now, and I'm gonna make not just the best of it, I'm gonna make more of it."

Host

我的意思是，确实，很多程序员，我的很多朋友，我，显然还有你，都在享受乐趣。

I mean, it is true, a lot of programmers, a lot of friends of mine, me, obviously you, are having fun.

DHH

过去三个月里，我在电脑上获得的乐趣比以往任何时候都多。

I have had more fun with computers in the last three months than at any time previously.

Host

快速上个厕所，然后我得问问你，你现在实际上是如何与这些系统互动的。

Quick bathroom break, and then I gotta ask you about how you actually interact with the systems now.

DHH

好的。

Yes.

编程设置变化 Programming Setup Changes

Host

我得问问你，你的编程设置发生了怎样的变化？比如键盘、语音，用什么 IDE？

I gotta ask you about how has your programming setup changed? So keyboard, voice, what's the IDE?

DHH

现在想想真疯狂，但没错，我用 TextMate 差不多 20 年。我从 2005 年开始用 TextMate，我想。我帮忙推出了第一个版本，然后我就不再感兴趣了。我没有寻找替代品的需求。直到切换到 Linux，我才被迫离开我的舒适区。现在随着切换到，我们怎么称呼它？智能体式工程？

It's crazy to think about now, but yeah, I used TextMate for almost 20 years. I used TextMate starting in 2005, I think. I helped get the first version out, and then I just wasn't interested. I wasn't in the market for an alternative. And it wasn't until the switch to Linux that I was forced out of my habitat. And now with the switch to, what are we calling it? Agentic engineering?

Host

哦，我他妈讨厌这个词。我们得想个听起来像编程一样朴素，但又能体现它是与智能体一起工作的词。但是——

Oh, I fucking hate that term. We gotta come up with something that sounds as plain as programming, but encapsulates the fact that it's with agents. But—

DHH

我仍然认为，在这一点上，它应该被称为编程。

I still think it should be called programming at this point.

Host

……好吧，我们就叫它编程。

... All right, let's just call it programming.

DHH

与智能体一起编程需要不同的工具集。确实如此。主要变化在于，你从头脑中的单线程编程转向了并行处理。当我写代码时，在 TextMate 甚至 Neovim 里手工雕刻，就在不久前，我一次只专注于一个问题，有条不紊地解决它，那实际上是进入心流状态的入口。进入心流的入口是深度沉浸于单个问题，直到完成。但和智能体一起工作不是这样，部分原因是智能体既太快又太慢。对于你要求它们做的事情，它们不会像键盘输入那样立即回复。所以你必须让智能体“烹饪”一会儿——

Programming with agents requires a different tool set. It really does. And the main change here is that you're going from single thread programming in your head to parallel processing. When I was writing code, chiseling it by hand in TextMate or even Neovim, not that long ago, I would just focus on one problem at the time, and I would methodically work my way through it, and that was actually the portal to flow. The portal to flow was deep, immersion into a single problem, see it through to the end. That's not how it works with agents, in part because the agents are at once both too fast and too slow. They don't give you an immediate reply on something that you asked them to do that's the same as typing on a keyboard. So you have to let the agent cook—

Host

……一会儿，然后你意识到，好吧，如果我只是坐在那里等它们，首先，这感觉没有效率。

... for a bit, and therefore you realize, well, if I just sit around waiting for them, first of all, that doesn't feel productive.

代理工作流与扩展 Agentic Workflow and Scaling

DHH

要做到这一点，你需要不同的设置。我最初是在 tmux 里做的，就是有单独的窗格和单独的分屏。基本上，把终端想象成带标签页的窗口是个好办法。你打开一堆标签页。我想大多数人都很清楚这是怎么回事。他们不会只在一个标签页里工作。

And to do that, you need a different setup. I started first doing it in tmux and just having separate panes and having separate splits. Basically, a terminal with tabs is a good way to think about it. You open a bunch of tabs. I think most humans know exactly how that works. They don't work in just one tab.

Host

所以还是坚持用终端、命令行界面？

So still sticking to the terminal, CLI.

DHH

绝对如此。

Absolutely.

Host

所以你不用 Claude Code 应用，或者……

So you're not using Claude Code app or the...

DHH

不用。

No.

Host

……或者 Codex 应用，或者……

... Codex app or the...

DHH

我很喜欢这个事实：智能体革命是在终端里发起的，因为我本来就是 TUI（终端用户界面）和终端的超级粉丝。那感觉是一个非常好的地方。那是一个美丽的地方。现代终端就是一个看起来不错的工作场所。所以我喜欢这样。然后，拥有多个智能体这个事实，尤其是当它不只是在你自己的机器上运行多个智能体，而是你开始运行多台机器时，现在仅靠 tmux 就不足以跟踪了，这就是为什么最近我切换到了一个叫 Herdr 的东西。Herdr 基本上就是 tmux 加上智能体通知。所以每当你的智能体完成工作并且需要你时，它会“叮”一声，一个小铃铛告诉你它准备好迎接它的人类了，呃……是主人还是仆人？我不太确定。但它准备好做决定了，它还会跟踪它是否处于工作模式。所以我已经设置好了 Herdr。实际上我在多台机器上运行着多个 Herdr 设置。

I love the fact that this agent revolution was kicked off in the terminal because I was already a huge fan of TUIs, terminal user interfaces, and the terminal in general. That feels like a, a really nice place to be. It's a beautiful place to be. The modern terminal is just a good-looking place to work. So I like that. And then this fact of having multiple agents, especially once it's not just multiple agents running on your own machine, but you start running multiple machines. Now tmux alone is not enough to keep track of it, and that's why as of late I've switched to this thing called Herdr. And Herdr is essentially tmux plus agent notifications. So whenever your agent is done and needs something for you, it goes ding, a little bell telling you it's ready for its human, uh... Is it master or servant? I'm not quite sure always. But it is ready for a decision, and it also keeps track of these is it in working mode or not. So I have this Herdr set up. I have multiple Herdr setups actually running on individual machines.

DHH

就在大约一个月前，我进入了一个疯狂阶段，意识到在单台机器上做这项工作不够快。就像我发现了多核编程，但我只有两个核心。我想：“如果我有 16 个核心呢？如果我有 32 个核心呢？如果我有 64 个核心呢？”所以我立刻出去买了这些很棒的 KVM，叫做 GL.iNet Comets。它们的作用就是这个小盒子。你插入 HDMI，插入 USB，然后连接到电脑。它就像一个 KVM。所以 KVM 是一种远程控制电脑的方式，但它的特别之处在于它太容易了。你把这个东西接进去，去一个网页，登录一次，设置一个密码。现在这个东西可以跳上你的 tailnet。这对我来说是过去一年的另一场革命，就是发现这些 WireGuard 网络。呃，Tailscale 基本上把你所有的电脑变成了一个本地网络，无论你在哪里。就像现在在我的手机上，我可以直接访问我在马里布办公室的所有电脑。我也可以访问我在哥本哈根办公室的所有电脑。我可以把它们当作我就坐在它们旁边一样对待，而无需在防火墙上打洞或设置复杂的 VPN。这样做的作用就是减少了让新算力上线所需的摩擦。所以我一发现这个，就看了看我的壁橱，我意识到我有一堆之前实验用的迷你电脑，我就说：“如果我把它们都连接起来会怎么样？”然后我就把四台电脑……在壁橱里连接起来了。它们都有各自的小 Comet，突然我就可以同时在更多电脑上运行智能体了，而且我可以用 Herdr 控制它们全部。

I went on this crazy phase just about a month ago realizing that doing this work on a single machine is not fast enough. It's like I've discovered multi-core programming, but I only have two cores. I'm like, "What if I had 16 cores? What if I had 32 cores? What if I had 64 cores?" So I instantly went out and I bought these amazing KVMs called GL.iNet Comets. And what they do is it's this little box. You plug in HDMI, you plug in USB and connect it to the computer. It's like a KVM. So a KVM is a remote way of controlling a computer, but what's special about this is just how easy it was. You connect this thing in, you go to a webpage, log in once, set one password. Now this thing can hop on your tailnet. This has been the other revolution of the last year for me, is discovering these WireGuard networks. Uh, Tailscale is essentially turning all the computers you have into a local network wherever you are. Like right now on my phone, I have direct access to all the computers in my Malibu office. I also have access to all my computers in my Copenhagen office. And I can treat them as though I sat right next to them without having to punch holes in a firewall or set up complicated VPNs. And what this does is it just decreases the friction it takes to get new compute online. So as soon as I discovered this, I looked at my closet, and I realized I had a bunch of mini PCs from prior experiments, and I just said, "What if I just connected all of them?" And I just connected four of the computers... in a closet. They all had their little Comet, and suddenly I could run agents on more computers at the same time, and I could control them all with Herdr.

DHH

Linux作为代理操作系统 Linux as the Agentic OS

DHH

它喜欢那些可以通过命令行调用的独立工具，而地球上主流的操作系统——我数一下，Mac、Windows、Linux 三个——没有哪个能像 Linux 那样与这种机制配合得这么好。在 Linux 里，一切都是配置文件或命令行工具。五分钟前，这曾是它最大的缺点，也是人们不喜欢 Linux 的原因——全是配置文件和命令行工具。宇宙跟我们开了个多大的玩笑：五分钟前 Linux 的缺点，如今成了它最大的卖点。

It loves individual tools that it can invoke through the command line, and there is no operating system on Earth of the majors, I'm counting three here, Mac, Windows, Linux, that works as well with that mechanism as Linux. Everything in Linux is either a config file or a CLI tool. Now, that was its main drawback five minutes ago. This was the reason people didn't like Linux. It's like it's all config files and CLI tools. What great irony that the universe has played it upon us that now the drawbacks of Linux five minutes ago are now its major selling points.

DHH

This was why I was so excited that all these agent harnesses arrived as TUI's, arrived as CLIs... has arrived as a terminal because suddenly, a whole new generation of both programmers and regular users discovered the glory of the terminal. The terminal was actually one of the most impressive and productive user interfaces we can have. Now, we've built user interfaces since then that were easier to learn. The mouse is a wonderful invention for letting someone who doesn't know a lot about computers just click around and find things out, but the terminal is for people who want to learn their systems, who want to learn the keyboard commands.

Host

那你觉得 TUI 会一直存在吗？

DHH

100%. Now, it's not gonna be the only thing. Of course it's not, because these labs now have trillion-dollar valuations, so they need to be able to reach billions of people. And I don't think we're gonna teach billions of people the intricacies of the TUI. So we're gonna have other tools, and we already have other tools, which is, by the way, one of the sweet things about Linux, the ChatGPT Codex tool.

安装速度痴迷 Installation Speed Obsession

Host

现在，你痴迷于让安装在一分钟内完成，网上很多人问你为什么。所以，为什么？你为什么这么执着于让安装少于 60 秒？

Now, you're obsessed about getting the installation to be under one minute, and a bunch of people asked you online why. So why? Why were you so obsessed to getting the installation to be less than 60 seconds?

DHH

首先，让我引用 Mitchell Hashimoto 的话，他创造了很棒的 Ghostty 终端，在 Omakub 上也有，他还是 HashiCorp 的创始人，他不久前在 X 上发了一篇很棒的文章，里面有句杀手级的话：「追求卓越无需解释。」想把东西做得尽可能好、尽可能快、尽可能美，不需要理由。我们应该想要漂亮、快速、用起来愉悦的系统。有趣的是，安装时间这件事，其实一开始并不是一分钟的目标。刚开始时我还挺谦虚的。我想如果我能用 15 分钟设置好整个系统，那已经是巨大的进步了，我会非常满意。这源于基准测试被扭曲得太离谱了。你之前说过你的第一台电脑是 Commodore 64，对吧？当你拆开那台 Commodore 64，按下开机键，我想不到一秒，BASIC 解释器就准备好接受你的命令了。几乎是瞬间的。几乎没有启动时间。你知道我设置一台新 Mac 花了多久吗？我买它其实是为了一个我还没能在 Linux 上 vibe coding 出来的应用，就是 Adobe Lightroom。全新电脑，开箱。我设置它，有软件更新。在我准备好安装 Adobe Lightroom 并让它完全更新到最新之前，花了 42 分钟。这太离谱了。太荒谬了。这是对所有喜欢电脑的人的侮辱，你拿到一台新电脑，在能用之前，你得花 42 分钟更新那些出厂时就已经装好的东西。这简直荒谬，而且疯狂的是这还不是最糟的。我还买了一台 PC，嗯，因为所有这些 Linux 机器，它们都是 PC 形式，对吧？

First, let me quote Mitchell Hashimoto, who created Ghostty terminal that's awesome and also available on Omakub and was also the founder of HashiCorp, who had this great post on X not too long ago that had the killer line, "The pursuit of excellence deserves no explanation." Wanting to make something as good and as fast and as beautiful as possible has no need for justification. We should want beautiful, fast systems that are a delight to use. Now, the funny thing with the install time is it didn't actually start with a one-minute mission. I was being modest when it got going. I thought if I could set up my entire system in 15 minutes, it would already be such a dramatic improvement over what came before it that I would be perfectly happy. And it came off the fact that the benchmarking had been skewed so out of whack. You talked about the Commodore 64 being your first computer, right? When you unwrap that Commodore 64 and you hit the on button, in I think about less than one second, the basic interpreter was ready to accept your command. It was literally almost instant. There was virtually no boot time. Do you know how long it took me to set up a new Mac that I had bought for actually the one app that I still haven't vibe coded my way into on Linux, which is Adobe Lightroom? Brand-new computer, out of the box. I set it up. There's a software update. Before I was ready to install Adobe Lightroom that I had it fully up to date, it took 42 minutes. That's grotesque. That's absurd. That's an insult to everyone who likes computers, that you get a new computer and before you can use it, you have to spend 42 minutes doing updates on something that was already installed on it out of the box from the factory. That is just preposterous, and it's crazy that that's not even the worst thing. I also got a PC, um, because all these Linux machines, they come as PCs, right?

Host

对。

Right.

DHH

而且它们预装了 Windows。所以我买了一台。就在三周前。全新机器，Intel Panther Lake，全套配置。从拆箱到能使用，花了一个小时 35 分钟。你觉得你进入 Omakub 花了多久？

And they have Windows pre-installed. So I got one. This was just three weeks ago. Brand-new machine, Intel Panther Lake, the whole shebang. An hour and 35 minutes from unwrapping the thing until it's ready to use. How long did you take before you think you were in Omakub?

Host

我的意思是，肯定不到一分钟。

I mean, it's less than a minute for sure.

DHH

一分钟。我们能造出这样的电脑。我们有技术。从 1981 年 Commodore 64 首次问世时，我们就有了这项技术。现在我们有一台快一万亿倍的电脑，你却要花 42 分钟或一个半小时去设置才能用，这让我非常愤怒，以至于它成了我的热情所在。而且有趣的是，就在我们刚才休息的时候，我和我的同谋 Ryan Hughes 聊了聊。我们现在有了一条思路，可以创建针对特定电脑预制的特殊 turbo 镜像，因为它依赖于一些硬件知识，比如 Dell XPS。我们要做一个 Dell XPS turbo 镜像，大约 12 秒就能安装完成。一个完整的 Linux 系统，大约 12 秒安装完。

A minute. We can build computers like this. We have the technology. We've had the technology since like 1981 when the Commodore 64 first came out. The fact that we now have a computer that's a trillion times faster and you have to spend 42 minutes or an hour and a half setting it up before it's ready to use is so infuriating to me that it became a passion. And it's funny. Just while we were having a little break here, I was talking to Ryan Hughes, my co-conspirator here. We now have, we think, a line where we can create these special turbo images that are pre-made for specific computers because it relies on some knowledge of the hardware, like the Dell XPS. We're gonna make a Dell XPS turbo image that can install in about 12 seconds. A full Linux system installed in about 12 seconds.

Host

太不可思议了。

That's incredible.

代理加速现实与第一性原理 Agent-accelerated reality and first principles

DHH

正是这种执念，让我在使用电脑时获得极大的满足感——我们不接受事物本来的样子。这也是为什么我现在所处的这个由智能体加速的现实如此令人振奋。因为我可以看着这些限制，然后想：你知道吗？我们做到五分钟的时候，我本可以停下来。那已经够快了。这就是 X 上的争论，对吧？你为什么还要继续？五分钟已经够快了。好吧，也许对你来说够了，但对我来说，12 秒有趣得多。而且我总是试着用第一性原理去思考。

That obsession is what gives me this great satisfaction with using computers, that we do not accept things the way they are. And this is why this agent-accelerated reality that I now inhabit is so invigorating. Because I can look at these kinds of constraints and go, do you know what? I could have stopped when we were at five minutes. That would already have been fast enough. This was the argument on X, right? Why do you keep going? Five minutes is fast enough. Okay, maybe for you, but for me, 12 seconds is a lot more fun. And I always try to think these first principles.

DHH

好，在那台机器上，再按一下 Omarchy 键，然后选 speed。你会看到一个列表，其中有一项是磁盘速度。

所以是每秒 7 GB。那块硬盘就是这么快。为什么我们不能在一秒内安装一个 Linux 发行版？Omarchy 发行版是 5.8 GB。如果我们能以 7 GB 的速度运行……当然，实际上你做不到，因为 USB 密钥比那慢得多，但你应该能最大化我们正在处理的底层物理和协议，这就是你的目标。我们必须一路回到那个水平。

So seven gigabytes a second. That's how fast that drive is. Why can we not install a Linux distribution in one second? The Omarchy distribution is 5.8 gigabytes. If we can run at seven gig... Now, of course, you can't actually do that because the USB key is a lot slower than that, but you should be able to maximize the underlying physics and protocols that we're dealing with, and that's your goal. We gotta get all the way back down to that.

Yeah. I love that you're doing a Linux distribution. 'Cause I mean, you could be doing anything. But the fact that you're building a Linux distribution means you're rethinking about what a computer is, 'cause an operating system is the fundamental foundation of how you interact with a computer.

DHH

是的。而且它已经太陈旧了。

Yes. And it's been so stale.

Host

确实如此。

Yeah, it has.

DHH

是的。而且它已经太陈旧了。我们今天拥有的 macOS 与 10 年前的 macOS 几乎没有区别。事实上，它在很多方面更糟了，因为苹果继续牢牢控制着用户。他们不希望用户安装他们尚未批准运行的软件。他们不希望用户在不手动操作的情况下重新配置热键。他们不想改变切换工作区时 500 毫秒的动画。这是一台令人恼火的锁定电脑。平心而论，苹果的电脑作为一台锁定电脑还算不错，但我不想要一台锁定的电脑。我想拥有我的电脑。更好的是，我想改造我的电脑，而这正是智能体时代需要新操作系统的地方。当你可以用 vibe coding 生成你想到的任何应用时，你应该也能用 vibe coding 生成你的操作系统。你应该能够改变任何东西：它的外观、它的工作方式、有哪些面板、没有哪些面板，而这需要 Linux。就这么简单。没有……另外两个操作系统都无法做到这一点。

The macOS we have today is scarcely different from the macOS we had 10 years ago. In fact, it's worse in many ways because Apple continues to chokehold the control on users. They don't want them to install software that they haven't already said good to go for. They don't want users to reconfigure their hotkeys without doing it manually. They didn't want to change the 500-millisecond animation switching workspaces. It is an infuriatingly locked-down computer. Now, to Apple's credit, it's a pretty good computer for being locked down, but I don't want a locked-down computer. I wanna own my computer. Better yet, I wanna mutate my computer, and this is where the agentic age needs a new operating system. When you can vibe code whatever app comes to your mind, you should be able to vibe code your operating system. You should be able to change anything, how it looks, how it works, what panels are there, what are not there, and that requires Linux. As simple as that. There are no... None of the other two operating systems can deliver this.

Host

但你能谈谈这个吗？我想有人问过你，比如：“你算老几，居然觉得自己能比 Ubuntu 做得更好？”或者，“Ubuntu 已经够好了，你为什么还要做？”而你的回答是：“我觉得我能做得更好。”所以你能谈谈吗？这是在 X 上的，你能谈谈这种疯狂从何而来吗？你作为一个人，或者也许和 Ryan 一起，就开始构建一个会比 Ubuntu 或 Arch 或任何这些更好的东西？

But can you speak to the, I think somebody asked you, like, "Who the hell are you to think you could do better than Ubuntu?" For example. Or, or like, "Why are you doing it when Ubuntu is already pretty good?" And your answer was, "I thought I could do better." So can you speak to, this was on X, can you speak to, like, where the craziness comes from, thinking that you as one person or maybe with Ryan, you could start building something that's going to be better than Ubuntu or Arch or any of this?

DHH

嗯，我要说的是，一开始并没有那么宏大。当我开始我的 Linux 之旅时，我实际上只是从 Ubuntu 开始的，我只是在它之上构建。这是一个已经做了很多我想做的事情的系统，而且至少它是 Linux。所以我能只是在它之上构建，让它更现代，让它看起来真的很好，并且有我想要的程序吗？我得到了某种版本，然后我发现你可以深入七层，而越接近硬件，越接近内核，越接近各个软件包等等，你发现的自由就越多。所以随着我发现越来越多的自由，我变得越来越雄心勃勃，我意识到，哦，惊喜，惊喜，我对计算机应该如何工作、应该看起来如何、应该安装哪些程序、以及安装应该花 42 分钟还是 45 秒，都有强烈的意见。顺便说一句，这是目前的世界纪录。所以如果有人在听完这个之后安装 Omarchy Quattro 并且能打破 45 秒，截图发给我，你就是新的世界纪录保持者。

Well, I'll say that it didn't start quite as grandiose. When I got going with my Linux journey, I actually just started with Ubuntu, and I just built on top of it. Here's a system that already does a lot of the things I wanted to do, and at least it's Linux. So can I just build on top of it and make it more modern, make it actually look good, and have the programs I wanna use? I got, like, some version of that, and then I discovered that there's seven layers deeper you could go, and as the closer you get to the metal, the closer you get to the kernel, the closer you get to the individual packages and so forth, the more freedom you discover. So as I discovered more and more freedom, I got more and more ambitious, and I realized, oh, surprise, surprise, I have strong opinions about how computers should work, how they should look, what programs should be installed, and whether they should take 42 minutes to install or 45 seconds. That's the current world record, by the way. So if anyone installs Omarchy Quattro after this and can beat 45 seconds, take a screenshot, send it to me, and you'll be the new holder of the world record.

Host

什么时候，你多久前突破了 60 秒大关？

When, how long ago did you cross the 60-second mark?

DHH

这很有趣，因为就像旧纪录，是什么来着？就像 1952 年的四分钟一英里之类的，人们认为人类不可能打破。然后一个人打破了，在接下来的几个月里，又有三个人做到了，对吧？所以我们有这个目标。我最初的目标是两分钟。然后我们打破了两分钟，我就想：“好吧，如果我们能打破两分钟，为什么不能打破一分钟？”我记得我当时想：“那太疯狂了。你不可能在一分钟内安装一个现代操作系统，当它们都……”如果其他的要花 42 分钟，对吧？比如苹果，有很多聪明人，花了 42 分钟，所以那一定是标杆。不，不，那不是标杆，因为没有速度限制。就像，你理所当然认为的几乎所有事情，在几乎所有情况下都不是固定的现实支柱。直到你达到那块硬盘能够传输的每秒 7 GB，你还没有达到极限，所以你应该继续推进。当然，然后它本身就变成了一场游戏，本身就是一种满足感。我能削减的每一秒都让我兴奋。当我们打破一分钟的障碍时，我认为那真的只是三周前左右，也许甚至两周前，我就疯狂了。然后我让一群智能体运行所有这些自动研究循环，尝试所有这些不同的理论，比如，哦，如果你改变这个，如果你改变那个，如果你删掉这个会怎样？如果你并行处理会怎样？如果你在输入用户名的时候，我们开始把软件包预加载到内存系统中，我们可以更快地加载它们。这变成了一场有趣的游戏。我认为这就是你应该思考产品开发的方式。它应该是有趣的。你应该，它应该是轻浮的。它应该试图超越目标，让它比所谓的“需要”好得多。

It's funny because it's like that the old limit on, what was it? It was a four-minute mile or something like that in 1952... that people thought was impossible for humans to beat. Then one guy beats it, and literally within the next few months, three more guys do, right? So we had this goal. I originally had the goal it was two minutes. And then we beat two minutes, and I'm like, "Well, if we beat two minutes, why can't we beat one minute?" And I remember thinking like, "That'd be crazy. You can't install a modern operating system in one minute when they're all..." If the other ones are taking 42 minutes, right? Like Apple, full of a lot of smart people, like, it took 42 minutes, so that's gotta be the bar. No, no, it's not the bar because there's no speed limit. Like, none of the things you take for granted are fixed pillars of reality in almost any case. Until you get down to the seven gigabytes per second that drive is able to transfer, you've not reached the limit yet, so you should just keep pushing. And of course, then it becomes a game in itself and a satisfaction itself. Every second I could shave off was an excitement. And when we broke the one-minute barrier, which was really only broken, I think, three weeks ago or something like that, maybe even two weeks ago, I just got crazy. And then I got a swarm of agents to run all these auto research loops of trying all these different theories on like, oh, if you change this thing, if you change that thing, what if you cut this out? What if you do things in parallel? What if while you were typing in your username, we start preloading packages into memory system, we can fire them in faster. And it becomes a fun game. And I think that's how you should think about product development. It should be fun. You should, it should be frivolous. It should be trying to overshoot the target and making it so much better than what it quote unquote needs to be.

Host

尤其是现在，因为你可以行动得这么快，而且因为智能体，你可以尝试这么多不同的方法。

Especially now because you can move so quickly and you can try so many different approaches because of the agents.

DHH

是的，这只会让它更令人满意。但我认为这一直是事实。如果你看看有史以来最伟大的梅赛德斯-奔驰，我想它叫 W126。我看过一部关于它的精彩纪录片。它是由一个在梅赛德斯安全部门工作多年的人主导的。突然他负责整个 S 级项目，他就把一切都投入进去了。

Yes, that just makes it more satisfying. But I think this was always true. If you look at the greatest Mercedes-Benz of all time, I think it was called the W126. I saw this wonderful documentary on it. It was steered by this guy who for years had worked in Mercedes' department for safety. And suddenly he gets put in charge for the whole S-Class project, and he just puts in all of it.

过度设计的汽车与卓越赞助 Overbuilt Cars and Patronage of Excellence

DHH

这就是自动回缩安全带、大灯清洗器这些东西的由来。这辆车对市场来说简直过度设计了，难以置信。但你看它就会想：‘我就想要那个。’就像你看一块机械表，会想：‘它能下潜到 100 米？’或者我记得劳力士有一款能下潜到 4000 米，深海、深海潜水……随便它叫什么。全世界除了三个人，没人会真去测试那块表。但你难道不想成为那种人，成为那些想把人类往前再推一把的人的赞助者？我想要最快的车，突破限速；我想要能下潜到马里亚纳海沟的潜水表；我想要 60 秒内就能装好的操作系统。

This is where you got the auto-retracting seat belts and washers on the headlights and everything. This car was so overbuilt for the market. It was unbelievable. But you look at that and go like, 'Well, I want that.' In just the same way that you look at a mechanical watch and go, 'It could go down to 100 meters?' Or I think Rolex has one that can go down to 4,000 meters, the deep ocean, the deep diver ocean... or whatever it's called. No one outside of three people in the world, whoever buys that watch, will ever put it to the test. But don't you wanna be the kind of person who is a patron of people who wanna push the human race that much forward? I want the fastest car breaking the speed limits. I want the diver watch that can go down the Mariana Trench. I want the operating system that can install in less than 60 seconds.

DHH

所以从个人层面，我敬佩那些人。我觉得每个人都应该为此努力。但我们也应该提到它的后果，比如追求卓越，即使指标看起来很无聊，它可能带来的意外后果是其他种类的卓越、发现、创新，以及所有那些与速度无关的东西。

So from the individual level, I admire those people. I think everybody should strive for that. But also the consequences of that we should mention, like the pursuit of excellence, even when the metrics seem to be frivolous, the maybe unexpected consequences of that is other kinds of excellences, discoveries, innovations, and all that kind of stuff that has nothing to do with the speed.

Host

100%。

100%.

DHH

这就是发现。你追逐的指标成为创新的催化剂和引擎，这很好，因为你会不断发现。这和登月、去火星是同一个道理。你会问：为什么？谁在乎？其实真正重要的是，你会发现很多关于化学推进的东西，会发现很多关于人类如何在太空中长期生存的东西。也许你会发现全新的太空太阳能利用方式，或者太空数据中心，或者其他什么。

It's discovery. It's nice to have the metric that you're chasing be the catalyst, the engine that drives innovation, because you'll just discover. It's the same reason you go to the moon, you go to Mars. It's like, why? Who cares? Well, actually, the real thing is you'll discover a lot about chemical propulsion. You'll discover a lot about how humans can live for a long time in space. Maybe you'll discover whole new ways of doing solar energy in space or data centers in space or whatever the hell.

Host

没错。

That's right.

DHH

各种各样的……也许你会遇到——

All kinds of... Maybe you'll meet--

Host

没错。

That's right.

DHH

……外星人，但你永远不知道。但设定一个目标并去追逐它，尤其是数字化的目标，真的很棒。

...aliens, but you never know. But setting a goal and chasing it, especially when it's numerical, is really nice.

Host

设定一个宏大的目标甚至更好。当你拒绝只做实际的事情，而是努力追求略超乎常理的目标时，奇迹就会发生。我们应该全力投入其中。这也是原因之一……事实上，在某种程度上，这个具体目标的动机就是看到别人这样做。我的意思是，埃隆是这次对话中独一无二的人。他的目标太超前了，乍一看似乎荒谬至极。我当然在很多次审视中都认为它们荒谬。然后你意识到它们有时确实会成真。也许不总是按时，但那又怎样？那又怎样？所以我们没有按时得到自动驾驶汽车。我在 2017 年为此骂了埃隆很多。但现在它来了，除了那个在 2017 年就认为这可能的人，还有谁能带我们走到这一步？他对现实如此不耐烦，以至于纯粹的力量将推动现实到达他想要的地方。我认为这非常鼓舞人心，我们需要这样的目标被移动。我们需要有人跑出四分钟一英里，这样我们其他人就会意识到：‘好吧，但我猜我能跑得更快。’这就是我努力追求的，我在 Omarchy 上真正追求这一点，因为这样生活更有趣，拥有崇高的抱负和野心。

And setting a grand goal is even better. When you refuse to just do what's practical and you strive for what's slightly beyond plausible, that's when the magic happens. And we should be going all in on that. This is one of the reasons... And in fact, to some degree, this specific goal was motivated by seeing others do this. I mean, Elon is the singular individual in this conversation. The guy is so out there in his goals. They seem so preposterous at first glance. I've certainly considered them preposterous on many glances. And then you realize that they come true sometimes. Maybe not always on schedule, and so what? So what? So we didn't get the self-driving car on schedule. I gave Elon a lot of shit for that back in '17. But now it's here, and who would have gotten us here except for the person who thought it was possible in '17, who is so impatient with reality that the sheer force is just gonna propel that reality to the place he wants it to be. I think that's deeply inspiring, and we need these kinds of goalposts to be moved. We need someone to run the four-minute mile, so the rest of us realize, 'Well, okay, but I guess I can run it quicker.' That's what I try to strive for, and I really strive for with Omarchy because it's just more fun to live that way, to have high aspirations and ambitions.

DHH

我要说的是，我实际上在这点上改变了一点想法。我花了很多时间在另一本书里说，有很多人实际上并不需要那些野心。而且我还——

And I will say I've actually changed my mind on that a little bit. I spent quite a lot of time speaking the other book that there are a lot of people who don't actually need those ambitions. And I also--

Host

这也是事实。我们不希望世界上有 80 亿个埃隆到处跑。那会相当疯狂，我想。

That's also true. We don't want 8 billion Elons running around the world. Would be rather crazy, I think.

DHH

But I've come to realize that for entrepreneurs, it's healthy to have suitable chunky goals. The idea of just running the Italian restaurant can't just be that. It should be maybe I just run the one Italian restaurant, but I make the best damn pizza possible. I make the most delicious Parmesan. We have to strive for excellence to muster the motivation to keep going.

DHH

其中一件事是把人类输入的延迟视为预加载的机会。所以——

One of the things was to treat the lag of human input as an opportunity to preload. So--

当你设置 Omarchy 机器时，你要回答五个问题——

There are five questions you answer when--

DHH

你设置 Omarchy 机器时，它会在后台做事情。我们也在后台做事情。这是我甚至没有考虑过的事情，我的意思是，这是最古老的把戏。长期以来，各种视频游戏都这样做，你让玩家与某些东西互动，然后在后台进行预加载等等。所以这是一种古老的技术，但我只是没有想过把它用在安装程序上。我以为，首先你问用户一些关于密码、用户名、时区等问题，然后你才做工作，而实际上你可以预加载这些。

You set up an Omarchy machine. So it's doing stuff in the background. We're doing stuff in the background. And that was one of the things I didn't even consider, which is, I mean, the oldest trick in the book. All sorts of video games for a long time have done this, where you let the player interact with something, and then you do preloading and so forth in the background. So it's an age-old technique, but I just hadn't thought about it for an installer. I thought, first you ask the user some questions about their password and their username and their time zone and so forth, and then you do the work, when, in fact, you could preload that.

DHH

现在，其他事情只是一堆对安装程序本身做事顺序的调整。其中一些只是缩小它。Omarchy 的最后一个版本是 7.5 GB，我们现在减少的时间很多实际上是在解压压缩的包文件。这是 ISO 上的主要内容，也是安装时间的主要部分。所以通过缩小到，我想我们现在是 5.85 GB，一个相当大的减少，它几乎直接一对一地转化了，我在那里做了一些非常有趣的事情。

Now, the other things were just a bunch of other tweaks to how the installer itself was doing things in a certain order. Some of them was also just shrinking it. The last version of Omarchy was 7.5 gigabytes, and a lot of the time we're down to now is literally decompressing compressed package files. That's the bulk of what is on the ISO, and that's the bulk of the install time. So by shrinking it down to, I think we're at 5.85 gigabytes now, a pretty chunky reduction, it almost literally translated one-to-one, and I did some really interesting things there.

DHH

Omarchy 使用 JetBrains 字体，这是一个很棒的字体，漂亮的字体，实际上是一个我第一眼不喜欢的字体，但后来它是我能在 Mitchell Hashimoto 的 Ghostty 中看起来完美的唯一字体。Ghostty，不管什么原因，不想以我习惯的方式渲染我心爱的 Bitstream Vera Sans。

So Omarchy uses the JetBrains font, which is an awesome font, beautiful font, and actually a font I didn't like at first glance, but then it was the only font I could get to look perfect in Mitchell Hashimoto's Ghostty. Ghostty, for whatever reason, did not wanna render my beloved Bitstream Vera Sans just the way I was used to it.

但它完美地渲染了这种 JetBrains 字体，所以我切换了。但它……Arch 上 JetBrains 字体的标准包是 200 MB，因为它包含所有这些变体和子类型等等。我意识到，我们并没有使用那些。我们使用的是这个 Nerd——

But it rendered this JetBrains font perfectly, so I switched over. But it... The standard package for JetBrains font on Arch is 200 megabytes because it includes all these variations and all these subtypes and whatever. And I realized, like, we're not using any of that. We're using the monospace version in this one Nerd--

font patched edition. Do you know how big that is? I think it's 16 megabytes.

JetBrains 包。

of the JetBrains package.

就在那里，我节省了 180 MB。我反复这样做。我们对 NVIDIA 驱动也做了同样的事情。所以 NVIDIA 驱动，当它们直接从 Arch 仓库出来时，我认为没有使用最极端的压缩形式，我认为叫 ZSTD，这是一种非常慢的压缩形式。创建归档很慢，但如果你在构建包——

And there, right there, I saved 180 megabytes. I just went through and did that a bunch of times. We did that with the NVIDIA drivers too. So the NVIDIA drivers, when they came straight off the, I think the Arch repo, were not compressed with the most extreme form of compression, which I think called ZSTD, which is a really slow form of compression. It's slow to create the archive, but if you're building a package--

Host

嗯……

Hmm...

DHH

你有的是时间，如果这意味着你可以缩小 ISO。所以仅仅在两个 NVIDIA 包上，我想我们节省了 200 MB。

you have all the time in the world if that means you can then shrink the ISO. So on just the two NVIDIA packages, I think we saved 200 megabytes.

Host

Mm-hmm.

迈凯伦对重量的痴迷 McLaren obsession with weight

DHH

所以我喜欢的是，我把自己想象成迈凯伦的汽车设计师。迈凯伦现在制造世界上最轻的超跑之一，他们绝对痴迷于把车上的每一克重量都减掉。这就是为什么即使是新的 750S 迈凯伦也比竞争对手轻得多。他们使用碳纤维单体壳，这让他们比法拉利（比如用铝的）更有优势。但他们也有这种对重量的痴迷，我看到迈凯伦的人接受记者采访时，只是说：“嗯，我们在这辆车上省了 370 克。”我就想：“等等，什么？一辆车可能重 1040 公斤或 1380 公斤，他们却在担心 370 克？”

So what I loved about this was I imagined myself being a car designer at McLaren. So McLaren makes some of the lightest super sports cars in the world right now, and they are absolutely obsessed with shaving every damn last gram off their cars. That's why even a new 750S McLaren is just way lighter than the competition. They use a carbon fiber monocoque, and that helps them over like Ferrari, for example, that's uses aluminum. But they also just have this obsession with weight, and I would see this interview with the McLaren folks talking to some journalist just going over, "Yeah, we saved 370 grams off that one." And I just went, "Wait, what? There's someone on a car that weighs maybe 1,040 kilos or 1,380 or something. They're worrying about 370 grams?"

Host

是啊。真是痴迷的疯子。

Yeah. What obsessed maniacs.

DHH

嗯。我爱他们。是的。我想要那样的生活。我想要一辆迈凯伦，就因为这个原因。所以那一刻，当我从软件包里一个个兆字节地削减时，我想，老兄，我有点像这里的迈凯伦汽车开发者，尽管我们甚至还没完成那项工作。这个软件包还能再缩小。

Hmm. I love them. Yeah. I want that in my life. I want a McLaren just for that reason. So I thought in that moment as I was shaving individual megabytes off the packages, man, I'm kinda like a McLaren car developer here, even though we haven't even completed that work. The package could shrink even more.

Omarchy与臃肿 Omarchy and bloat

DHH

但这实际上是我们与 Omarchy 和传统 Linux 社区之间的另一个冲突：臃肿。

But this is actually this other conflict that we've had with Omarchy and the traditional Linux community is bloat.

Host

嗯哼。

Mm-hmm.

DHH

这种想法是，在某些圈子里，预装软件是不合规矩的，因为你是谁，凭什么决定人们应该用什么程序？我就说：“老兄，这名字里就写着呢。它叫 Omarchy，因为 Oma 部分是 omakase 的缩写，字面意思是‘主厨的选择’。我是主厨，我来做选择，这是我觉得很棒的应用集合。”顺便说一句，它有一个视频编辑器。它预装了 OBS 用来录制。它就在里面。

This idea that it's actually not kosher in certain circles to ship with pre-installed software because who are you to make decisions about what programs people should use? And I'm like, "Dude, it's in the fucking name. It's called Omarchy because the Oma part is short for omakase, which literally means chef's choice. I'm the chef. I'm making the choices, and this is the collection of applications I think is awesome," which by the way has a video editor. It ships with OBS to do your recording. It's just in there.

Host

是啊。OBS 预装了。不错。

Yeah. OBS is pre-installed. Nice.

DHH

Kdenlive 是一个视频编辑器，时间线编辑器，很棒。我用它编辑我所有的视频。然后我做了我自己的剪辑工具。

Kdenlive is a video editor, timeline editor, which is great. I edit all my videos with that. And then I made my own clip editor.

Host

你提到了同步的问题。是的。

You talked about the thing with syncing up. Yeah.

DHH

我有这个剪辑的问题，比如我做播客什么的，我需要为 X 做一个片段。用 Kdenlive 或其他时间线编辑器有点麻烦，所以我做了 Omacut。

I had this thing with clips, so I would do a podcast or whatever. I need a clip for X. And it's kind of a hassle to use Kdenlive or other timeline editors, so I made Omacut.

Host

嗯哼。Omacut 也包含在内。

DHH

Omacut，酷。它只是用来做剪辑的，你可以用键盘驱动整个东西。所以你可以去到正确的时间点，然后按 Control + Space，它会把剪辑线移到那个位置。然后你到剪辑的结尾，按 Alt + Space，它会把结束线移过去。然后按 Control + S，它就保存了。我现在用 Omacut 做剪辑快得惊人。太棒了。

Omacut, cool. It's just for making clips, and you can drive the whole thing with keyboard. So you can go to just the right time mark and then you can hit Control + Space. It moves the clip line to that line. Then you go to the end of the clip, and you hit Alt + Space, and it moves the end line. And then you hit Control + S, and it saves. I can make clips so damn fast now with Omacut. It's amazing.

Host

不错。

Nice.

DHH

但它上面有所有软件。它有 Neovim，有 Herder，有 Tmux，有一个终端，有一堆主题，有一堆背景图片，我挑的最好的。那会占用一些空间。应该的，因为这不应该是那种贫瘠的风景，你必须重建一切。这应该是一个你打开就能用的高效系统。

But it has all the software on it. It has Neovim on it. It has Herder on it. It has Tmux. It has a terminal. It has a bunch of themes. It has a bunch of background images, the best ones I could pick out. That takes up some space. It should because this is not supposed to be this just barren landscape where you have to reconstruct everything. This is supposed to be a productive system the minute you unpack it.

用Bash和代理构建 Built in Bash and agents

Host

它是用什么构建的？

What is it built in?

DHH

所以实际上，对于 Linux 配置部分，它主要是用 Bash 构建的。

So it's actually mostly built in Bash for the Linux configuration part.

Host

对。Bash 是很好的语言。它真的是为系统管理而生的。但它也有局限性。你不应该用 Bash 构建所有东西。

Right. Bash is a great language for that. It is made for that really, for system administration. It also has its limitations. You should not build everything in Bash.

Host

智能体生成 Bash 的能力怎么样？相当好吗？

How are agents with generating Bash? Pretty good?

DHH

哦，太棒了。除了一件事，每次我发现的时候，我都得敲打智能体的后脑勺，提醒它去看 agent 的 MD 文件，因为我在那里有一条指令，不要提前退出。智能体喜欢用前置条件，而不是完全展开的条件语句。我喜欢那种“如果这样，那就那样”的写法。智能体喜欢前置条件或退出，另一个前置条件或退出，然后才落到它真正想做的最后一件事情上。我讨厌那种风格。所以除了那一点，它已经变得惊人地、戏剧性地、棒极了地好，好到上次我们谈话时，我们谈到我会让智能体写个 Bash 的东西，然后我自己再打一遍，我有点记不住。然后我努力确保那些能力没有从我的手指间流失，所以我真的自己学了怎么写 Bash，现在我自己不写任何 Bash 了。我大概几个月没自己写过 Bash 了，因为智能体变得太好了，如果你给它们一些风格上的指点，偶尔对复杂性提出反对……

Oh, amazing. Except for one thing, and I have to smack the agents over the back of the head every single time I catch it, and remind it to look at the agent's MD file, because I will have an instruction in there not to make early exits. The agents love to have preconditions instead of fully expanded conditionals. I like something that says, "If this, then do that." Agents like to have precondition or exit, another precondition or exit. And then it falls down to the final thing it actually wants to do. I hate that style. So except for that, it's gotten shockingly, dramatically, awesomely good, to the point where when we talked last time, we talked about how I would ask the agent for a Bash thing, and then I would type it in myself, and I kinda wouldn't remember. And then I took the effort to make sure that all that competence wasn't draining out of my fingers, so I actually learned how to write Bash myself, and now I'm not writing any Bash myself. I have not written any Bash myself for probably a couple months, because the agents have just gotten so good, that if you give them the few pointers on style, and occasionally put pushback on complexity...

Host

这是……我几天前发过推。让我震惊的是，智能体经常能做一些事情，说完成了，让另一个智能体审查……它们说完成了，然后我就像：“嗯，对我来说看起来有点太复杂了。”然后它就会说：“哦，是的，你说得对。你说得对。我完全把它复杂化了，”然后它会把代码砍掉一半。我就想：“难道它不能从一开始就这么做吗？”然后有人提醒我，人类也是一样的。如果你在做一个东西，别人审查它，他们只是说：“嗯，看起来有点复杂，”你通常也能说：“啊，你说得对。”

This is... I tweeted about this a couple of days ago. It blows my mind how often an agent can do something, say it's done, have the work reviewed by another agent... they say it's done, and then I go like, "Eh, looks a little too complicated for me." And then it'll go like, "Oh, yeah, you're right. You're right. I totally overcomplicated this," and it'll cut it in half. I'm like, "Couldn't it just do this, like, from the outset?" And then someone else reminded me, humans are the same way. If you work on something and someone else reviews it and they just are like, "Eh, it looks a little complicated," quite often you're able to go like, "Ah, you're right."

Host

看，那，那只是……那些小迹象向我表明，程序员仍然是需要的，那种直觉，因为我想，什么，你说了什么？我对代码的景观有一种感觉。

See, that, that just... Those little indications to me show that the programmer is still needed, that intuition, 'cause I think, what, what did you say? I have a sense of the landscape of the code.

DHH

当我构建像 Omarchy 这样的东西时，我确实会看代码。我看形状，看比例。就像，我不需要再自己雕刻一切了。这又是，当我自夸的时候……一会儿我是迈凯伦的汽车工程师，一会儿我是达芬奇，和他的整个学生工作室一起，他们在精细的大理石上雕刻，对吧？然后他说：“啊，不，比例不太对。”这就是现在和智能体一起工作的感觉。我可以像编辑一样给出反馈。你知道吗？我不会自己写所有东西，但我可以告诉你什么时候论点不成立，什么时候形状与问题不成比例，或者什么时候感觉太复杂了。而且惊人地频繁，它就行得通。就像早期，笑话是“不要犯错”。我认为你不再需要告诉智能体那些了，因为现在工具架很好，它们运行自动化测试，得到反馈。但你确实需要告诉它们：“让它更简单。”

When I build something like Omarchy, I, I do look at the code. I look at the shape. And I look at the proportions. Like, I don't have to chisel everything myself anymore. This is, again, when I'm flattering myself for... Not one moment I'm a McLaren auto engineer, and the next moment I'm a da Vinci, working with his whole studio of students who are doing all the chiseling on the fine marble, right? And he goes like, "Ah, no, the proportions are not quite right." That's how it feels working with agents now. That I can give feedback like an editor. That, do you know what? I'm not gonna write everything myself, but I can tell you when the argument doesn't land. I can tell you when the shape isn't proportional to the problem, or when it just feels like it's too complicated. And shockingly often, it just works. It's like the early days, where the jokes was, "Don't make mistakes." I don't think you have to tell the agents that anymore, because now the harnesses are so good, they run automated testing. They get that feedback. But you actually do have to tell them, "Make it simpler."

语音交互 Voice interaction

Host

你在交互中会用语音吗？还是你……你打字多少？你……

Do you use voice at all for the interaction? Or are you... How much are you typing? How much are you...

DHH

Plaud 显然有 API。你可以获取录音，转录，清理转录文本，因为你希望它……因为我提到文件，提到技术概念，比如文件和函数……等等，你希望确保转录、语音转文字能正确识别。所以你需要一个词典。你希望它在处理文件时能感知代码库。然后你就有了一大段东西，通常是我花 10、15、20 分钟讨论完整设计，我觉得这真的非常强大。因为它没有过度具体化的问题，因为系统对你想象的内容有大量的意识流思考。这对设计的早期阶段非常有用。比如，如果我想从头构建 Omarchy，最初阶段我会口述。可能长达一个小时。我有过一个小时的提示词，我就一直跑着……说着。

I'm referring to technical concepts, like files and functions... and so on, that you wanna make sure the transcription, the speech-to-text gets correctly. So you wanna have a dictionary. You want it to be aware of the code base when you process the files. Because it doesn't have the problem of overspecification because of how much stream of consciousness thinking the system has about what you're imagining. And this is really good for the early steps of a design. Like, if I wanted to build Omarchy from scratch, the first stages, I talk through. It could be an hour. 别让完美成为优秀的敌人。我的意思是，如果你突然让人们能做到以前根本做不到的事，多花五秒也没关系。我觉得这是史蒂夫·乔布斯非常擅长的一个领域，他意识到，比如第一代 iPhone 在很多方面都很糟糕，对吧？数据连接慢得可怕，性能严重不足，等等。但产品太有吸引力了，所以这些都不重要。如果你等到一切都完美无缺再发布，那就太晚了。如果你解决的是人们目前没有解决方案的问题，他们愿意等五秒。如果你在一个竞争激烈的市场，那好吧，情况不同。但那意味着问题已经解决了，你不需要去解决它。

Don't let the perfect be the enemy of good. I mean, if you're suddenly enabling something people wouldn't be doing before at all, it's okay to take five seconds longer. I think this is one of the areas that Steve Jobs was so good, that he realized, like, the first iPhone was terrible in all sorts of ways, right? Absolutely horrendously slow data connection, very underpowered, all the things. But the product was so compelling that it just didn't matter. You'd be shipping too late if you waited until everything was just right, just perfect. If you're solving a problem that people currently don't have a solution for, they're willing to give it five seconds. If you're in a competitive market, yeah, okay, it's different. But that means the problem's already solved, and you don't need to solve it.

DHH

顺便说一句，我觉得这是另一个突破，会让 Linux 彻底称霸。智能体把诊断 Linux 系统的所有麻烦都解决了，还把 Linux 生成那些过于具体、完全晦涩难懂的报错信息这件事，变成了它最大的优势。当 Linux 出问题时，智能体可以拿那条对普通人来说毫无意义的非常具体的报错信息，去关联它预训练时学过的 4000 万行 Linux 代码，所以它确切知道该去哪里查，然后把它定位出来。

I think this, by the way, is one of the other breakthroughs that is going to lead to the total domination of Linux. The agents have taken all the hardship out of diagnosing Linux systems and turned the fact that Linux produces these overly specific, totally arcane error messages into its greatest advantage. When Linux has an issue, the agent can take that very specific error message that makes no sense to a normal human and correlate it with the fact that the agent was pre-trained on 40 million lines of Linux code, so it knows exactly where to look and dial it down.

我给你讲一个特别棒的小故事。我当时在和 JDX 这个人合作，他在做 mise。这是一个为快速迭代的开发工具设计的包管理器。我们就是用这个来管理所有智能体软件等等。普通的包管理器不是为了一天更新七次而设计的。而这些智能体框架大约一天更新七次，所以我们需要一个带外包管理器，而 mise 恰好非常适合这个。总之，它有个小 bug。有个竞态条件，智能体特别擅长发现这种问题，因为当你同时运行多个智能体时，它们会把你底层基础设施里那些人类手动操作永远不会触发的竞态条件都找出来。所以它发现了这个问题，对吧？我跟智能体说：“嘿，你能把这个作为 bug 报告发到 JDX 的 GitHub 上吗？”不幸的是，就在那之前，我不得不用八个不同的智能体对 Omarchy 本身做了一次 QA 运行。它发现了 28 个真实问题……

I'll give you one amazing anecdote. So I was working with this guy JDX, who's working on mise. This, this package manager for fast-moving development tools. This is how we manage all the agent software and so forth. Normal package managers are not built to be updated seven times a day. And all of these agent harnesses are updated about seven times a day, so we needed an out-of-band package manager, and mise just turns out to be perfect for this. Anyway, it had a small bug. There was some race condition which agents are very good at finding because when you start running multiple agents at the same time, they will suss out all these race conditions you have in your underlying infrastructure that was never triggered by a human trying to do manual thing. So it finds this issue, right? I tell I tell the agent, "Hey, can you post this as a bug report to JDX's GitHub?" 让我觉得神奇的是，你能一口气说出这么多不同的竞争者。这个市场如此开放，确实在来回变化，我们有真正的竞争，有这么多实验室能够达到前沿或者接近前沿。顺便说一句，这很了不起。我仍然不完全理解这一点。但回答你的问题，目前总体上最好的模型是 Fable。在我看来，第二好的模型是 Opus 5。但就在 Opus 5 下面一档，而且它们并不总是落后，有时候它们还会领先。我一会儿会说到这个。我会把 GPT Sol 评为非常好。Grok 4.6 我几天前刚开始测试。我偶然设置了一个很棒的测试，我把一个 Python 库翻译成了 Rust。呃，你刚才看到的那个有很酷动画的屏幕保护程序？它是由一个叫 Terminal Text Effects 的 Python 库驱动的。非常酷的库。我们从 Omarchy 的第一天起就在用它。问题在于它是用 Python 写的，所以当它启动时，尤其是在笔记本电脑上，它用 Python 运行，会占用你所有的 CPU 来做这些效果，因此，这意味着它大约消耗 30 瓦的能量，会让风扇转起来，还会耗尽电池。在台式机上没什么关系，但在笔记本电脑上就很重要了。

What's amazing to me is that you could rattle off so many different contenders. That this market is so wide open, that it does actually change back and forth, that we have real competition, that there are so many labs that are able to get either to the frontier or close to it. That, by the way, is remarkable. I still don't fully understand that. But to answer your question, the best model in general right now is Fable. The second-best model, in my opinion, is Opus 5. But the tier just below Opus 5, and it's not even that they're always below, sometimes they're ahead. I'll get to that in a second. I would rank GPT Sol very good. Grok 4.6 I just started testing a few days ago. I had this wonderful test that I've set up by accident where I translated this Python library into Rust. Uh, the screensaver you just saw with all the cool animation? That's powered by a Python library called Terminal Text Effects. Really cool library. We've been using it since the first day of Omarchy. The problem with that is it's written in Python, so when it starts up, especially on a laptop, and it runs in Python, it uses all of your CPU to do these effects, and therefore, it means it uses about 30 watts of energy, and it spins up your fans, and it drains your battery. Doesn't really matter on a local computer, but it does matter on a laptop.

用AI将Python库翻译成Rust Translating a Python library to Rust with AI

DHH

于是我想：“你知道吗？这听起来像是 Rust 该解决的问题。”所以我先给 Fable 出了这个挑战，我只告诉它：“这是 Python 库的源代码，”这个 TTE 库有一堆依赖等等。“我想要一个 Rust 版本，没有依赖，单个可执行文件。”就像，这就是 Rust 的用途。所以基本上，我要用 Rust 实现。我要它像素级完美，逐帧分析，不要停，直到完成。

So I thought, "Do you know what? This sounds like a problem for Rust." So first I gave Fable the challenge, and all I told it was, "Here's the source code for the Python library," this TTE library that had a bunch of dependencies and so forth. "I want a Rust version of this with no dependencies, a single executable." Like, that's what Rust does. So basically, I want it in Rust. I want it to be pixel perfect, frame by frame, do a full analysis, don't stop until you're finished.

Host

嗯。

Yep.

DHH

那就是提示词。

That was the prompt.

Host

嗯。

Yep.

DHH

我没开玩笑，不到 45 分钟，它就像说：“我都听到了吗？我完成了。我检查了所有东西。我把启动时间从 86 毫秒降到了 2 毫秒。我把执行速度提升了 9.6 倍，我记得是。可执行文件是 3 兆字节。你想运行它吗？”

I kid you not, in just under 45 minutes, it was like, "Am I heard all? I'm finished. I've checked everything. I have reduced the startup time from 86 milliseconds to two milliseconds. I have sped up the execution time by 9.6 times, I believe it was. The executable is three megabytes. Do you wanna run it?"

Host

太棒了。

That's awesome.

DHH

所以我运行它——

So I run it—

Host

天哪。

Oh, man.

DHH

……而且我不知道自己为什么惊讶，因为这种翻译工作我们早就知道 AI 很擅长，但对我来说仍然令人震惊的是，我可以一次性完成对一个我用了整整一年的 Python 库的完整翻译，其他人用得更久，然后把它变成一个 Rust 可执行文件，而我完全不懂 Rust，也根本没看过 Rust 代码，就生成了这个可执行文件，然后我立刻告诉智能体：“太棒了，发布吧。”它把它打包成一个新包。它问我：“你想叫它什么？”“呃，就叫 TTFX 吧。我们创建一个新的 Git 仓库。”它设置了 Git 仓库。“我们为构建系统创建一个新包，构建包。”它把它放上去。“我们把它推出去。我们向 Omarchy 本身打开拉取请求，这样我们就从 TTE（Python 实现）切换到 TTFX。”它做了所有这一切，而我只是坐在那里。而且，我已经在智能体加速中完全兴奋到极点，但我还是不得不往后一靠，心想：“这就是 AGI，不是吗？这就是 AGI 的样子。如果我们有……如果这些时刻就是常态，那这就是 AGI。”

... and I don't know why I'm surprised, because this translation job is something we've known for a while that AI is pretty good at, but it was still staggering to me that I could one-shot a full translation of a Python library I had been using for a year, that others had been using for much longer, and turn it into a Rust executable without knowing any Rust, without looking at the Rust code at all, and produce this executable that I then told the agent right after, "This is great. Ship it." It packaged it up as a new package. It told me, "What do you want it to call?" "Uh, let's call it TTFX. Let's create a new Git repo." It sets up the Git repo. "Let's create a new package, build package for our build system." It puts that up. "Let's push it out. Let's open the pull request to the Omarchy itself so we switch around from TTE, the Python implementation, to TTFX." It does all of it, and I'm just sitting there. And again, I'm already at this point fully delirious with agent acceleration, and I still had to lean back and I go like, "This is AGI, isn't it? This is what AGI looks like. If we have... If these moments are just what it is all the time, this is AGI."

And then we assign them tasks-

Host

太棒了。

That's awesome.

DHH

……在 Base Camp 里，所以它们会在 Base Camp 里处理待办事项，或者处理卡片上的工作。结果发现，一个为异步通信优化的协作工具其实是正确的形式，而这些套件更像聊天。聊天，我是说，是这些东西的原始形式，并不合适，因为你坐在那儿等着。它引诱你坐在那儿等，而当你用 Base Camp 这样的东西时，你可以给智能体一个待办事项，你不会期待立刻得到回复。所以我不知道最终形态会是什么样，但我一直在用所有套件。我在 Herdr 里用它们，Herdr 有面板。所以我经常在上面跑 Claude，下面跑 Codex，也许还开一个 OpenCode。但我要说，就在最近，我发现人在循环里是瓶颈。所以我开始搭建更自动化的系统。我在构建一个 Omarchy 机器人，能在 Omarchy 上做更自主的开发，就是按固定日程处理待办、PR 和 issue，然后用 Hey CLI 给我发邮件，然后我就收到这封邮件：这里有 12 个 PR，要么可以合并，要么我觉得你应该关掉。然后我在那里做最终决定。

... inside of Base Camp, so they will do work on a to-do in Base Camp, or they'll work, do work on a card. And it turns out that a collaboration tool that's optimized for asynchronous communication is actually the right format versus these harnesses are a little more like chat. And chat, which was, I mean, the original format for these things, is not the right thing because you're sitting around waiting. It entices you to sit around and wait, versus when you're dealing with something like Base Camp and you can just give an agent a to-do item, you don't expect to hear back immediately. So I don't know what the final form is gonna take, but I've been using all of the harnesses. So I use them inside of Herdr, and Herdr has panes. So oftentimes I'll run Claude up top, and then I'll run Codex down below, and then maybe I also have an OpenCode set up. But I will say just very recently, I found that the human in the loop is the limit here. So I've started setting up more automated systems. I've been building an Omarchy bot that can do more autonomous development on Omarchy that is just On a regular schedule process, all depending to do or PRs and issues, and then send me an email using the Hey CLI, and then I just get this email. Here's 12 PRs that are either ready to go or I think you should close. And then I just make the final determination there.
