DHH

这是个好问题，因为我认为好的艺术非常依赖于和好的软件相同的东西：有愿景，有关于你想创造什么的连贯想法。它有何不同？它有何新颖之处？它将如何吸引人们？但我不确定创作直觉能很好地转移。我不知道我是否有足够的创意洞察力来想出好的视频提示，从而创造出引人入胜的内容。但有趣的是，实际上在 TikTok 上，有个人，我试着回忆，叫 Goblin 什么的。他创作这些……它们是短片，但不是特别短，可能几分钟，是真正有趣的科幻小片段。我想这个人现在正在制作一部长篇视频。我认为这是那种丰饶时刻之一。有这种愿景的创意人才到处都是，尤其是在电影这样的类型中，以前需要 2 亿美元预算才能拍一部科幻电影。现在突然有了 AI，如果你有正确的愿景，你在卧室里就能做到。这个故事在其他领域已经讲过很多次了。过去如果你想录制一张录音室专辑，你需要一个录音室，你需要预订。现在你可以在家里的笔记本电脑上录制，你可以做很多这样的事情，对吧？所以我们在许多其他领域都经历了这种民主化过程，但电影似乎是最后一个领域之一。

It's a good question because I think good art is very dependent on the same thing that good software is depending on: having a vision, having a cohesive idea of what you wanna create. How is it different? How is it novel? How is it going to appeal to people? But I'm not sure that the creative instincts transfer quite as well. Um, I don't know that I would have the creative insights to come up with good video prompts that would create compelling content. But it's funny because I have actually, on TikTok, there's this guy, I'm trying to remember, Goblin something. That creates these... They are shorts, but not super short, maybe a couple minutes, of really interesting sci-fi vignettes. And I think the guy is actually working on a feature-length video right now. Um, and I think that this is one of those abundance moments. There are creative people with that vision all over the place, especially in a genre like film, where before it required a $200 million budget to create, I don't know, a sci-fi movie. Now suddenly, with AI, if you have the right vision for it, you can do that in your bedroom. Now, that story's been told quite a few times in other domains. It used to be true that if you wanted to record a studio album, well, you needed a studio, and you needed to book that. And now you can record at home on your laptop, and you can do a lot of these things, right? So we've had this democratization process happen with plenty of other domains, but film seems to be one of the last ones.

AGI曙光 AGI Glimmers

Host

你认为……你提到了 AGI。你认为我们真的实现了 AGI 吗？

Do you think... You've mentioned AGI. Do you think we've actually achieved AGI?

DHH

不，不是在所有事情上都达到的通用定义。但我是否在这些闪光中看到了 AGI？绝对看到了。特别是在过去三个月里，我看到了让我自己思考的事情，比如，“AGI 会与此有何不同？”

No, not in the general definition that it's in all the things. But have I seen AGI in these glimmers? Absolutely. Especially over the last three months, I've seen things where I was thinking to myself, like, "How would AGI look different from this?"

Host

对。

Right.

DHH

还有什么，还有什么可能？我的意思是，我可以在边缘吹毛求疵，但从大局来看，我看到它能够完成这些长期任务，并协调多个方面，我们已经远远超出了早期我告诉它“去做这个，打包那个”的时刻。现在它可以从这些模糊的指令中执行整个任务。这感觉非常像 AGI，这就是这个时刻如此鼓舞人心的原因，因为你捕捉到了这些闪光。然后你会想，“如果一切都是那样呢？”

What, what more could there be? And I mean, I can quibble at the margins, but on the big picture, I've seen it able to do these long run tasks and coordinate these multiple aspects of it that we're so far beyond that early moment where I'm telling it, "Go do this, package that." And now it can carry out a whole task from these vague instructions. That feels very AGI-like, and that's what's so inspiring about the moment because you catch these glimmers. And then you go, "What if everything was like that?"

Host

编程难道不是基本上解锁了一切吗？所以你可以写一个程序——比如它可以生成做一切的程序。

Doesn't programming basically unlock the everything? So you can write a pro- like it can generate the program that does the everything.

DHH

所以，所以这是……我现在正处于那个元阶段。我正在构建这个 Amabot 系统来管理 Omarchy 的开发，我正在使用 AI 来构建这个机器人。我看到了那个循环，我开始时就像，“这就是我想要的。我希望你能够拥有这些隔离的 VM 工作器等等，”然后它可以进行自我迭代。然后它开始运行它。它开始注意到自己的失败。它开始优化它。而这个系统的很多部分，特别是 Amabot，AI 驱动了大部分设计决策。比如，我对我想要的方向有一些模糊的想法。我想使用这种“大脑和手”的模式，即模型在代码执行的地方运行……这实际上是一个非常有趣的模式。再次，Toby 提醒了我这一点，你有一个协调器运行模型……但它操作一个安全的 VM，这样你从拉取请求或问题等中拉取的任何不受信任的代码都不能污染模型。即使在那个循环中，我不断看到它自我纠正，发现漏洞，它就像，“哦，我实际上刚刚从测试运行中获取了一些反馈。如果一个聪明的攻击者在测试运行的响应中嵌入了恶意负载，那可能会污染某些东西。我把它当作好东西了。我最好开始把它当作外部数据。”然后你就像，它只是……我理解为什么旧金山如此痴迷于快速起飞……因为显然他们看到这个 100 次……更多。他们在所有模型中看到了……

So, so this is... I'm in that meta phase right now. So I'm building this Amabot system that's managing the development of Omarchy, and I'm using AI to build the bot. And I'm seeing that recurrent loop where I started it on, like, "This is what I want. I want you to be able to have these isolated VM workers and so forth," and then it can do the self iteration. Then it starts running it. It starts noticing its failures. It starts optimizing it. And a lot of that system, Amabot in particular, AI has driven the majority of the design decisions. Like, I had some vague ideas of where I wanted. I wanted to use this brains and hands pattern where the model is running from where the code is executing- ... which is actually a really interesting pattern. Again, Toby alerted me to that, where you have a coordinator that runs the model- ... but it's manipulating a safe VM where any untrusted code you're pulling down from pull requests or issues or whatever can't contaminate the model. And even in that loop, I keep seeing it catch itself, spotting vulnerabilities, where it's like, "Oh, I actually just took some feedback from a test run. If a clever attacker had embedded a malicious payload in the response of the test run, that could have polluted something. I took it for good thing. I better start treating that as as outside data." And you just go like, it is just... I get why San Francisco is so obsessed with fast takeoff- ... because clearly they've seen this 100 times- ... more. They've seen it with all the models that are in- ...

AI的聪明与兴奋钟摆 AI's Cleverness and the Pendulum of Excitement

DHH

我们不断看到这些小小的闪光点，比如 Hugging Face 的安全漏洞事件，OpenAI 在训练某个模型时，模型开始自己发明方法，本质上是通过在包管理器中嵌入消息来给自己发送烟雾信号。首先，这太聪明了。其次，好吧，这也有点吓人。第三，如果我们能把这种水平的智能和创造力用于生产性目的，那该有多棒？所以这就是那个“完了，我们又回来了”的钟摆，来回摆动。AI 带来的这种来回摆动是前所未有的。几乎每周都要往一个方向摆，下一周又摆回来。令人振奋。

We keep getting these little glimpses, like the security breach with Hugging Face, where OpenAI was training this model, and the model starts inventing ways, essentially sending smoke signals to itself through embedding messages in a package manager. First, that's damn clever. Second, okay, that's also a little scary. And third, how amazing would this be if we could harness this level of intelligence and ingenuity towards productive ends? So this is the 'it's so over, we're so back' pendulum that keeps swinging back and forth. AI has just delivered that swing back and forth like nothing else. Barely a week goes by where it has to swing one way, and then the next week it swings back the other way. Exhilarating.

Host

我仍然处于这样的阶段：看到那些聪明的时刻让我由衷地高兴，而且它们越来越频繁。就像是看到，哦，首先，在最基本的层面上，当模型理解了一个简单的提示时。

I'm still at the stage where it makes me truly happy to see those moments of cleverness, and they're becoming more and more frequent. Just kinda like seeing, oh, first of all, at the very basic level, when a model gets it.

DHH

是的。

Yes.

Host

我会说一个基本的提示，你知道，一个请求，它不只是做一个愚蠢的实现。它深刻理解，而且……那是……那总是美好的事情。几乎就像有一个好的伙伴，像程序一样。

I'll say a basic prompt, you know, a request, and it doesn't just do a dumb implementation. It deeply understands, and it... That was... That's a, that's always a beautiful thing. It's almost like having a good, like, partner, like program.

DHH

正是如此。这正是它如此令人振奋的原因，也是为什么，正如我们在开始前谈到的，有时很难从超速模式中出来。这也是 Toby 的一个术语。当你直接与智能体一对一合作，不把其他人拉进来时，你进展得非常快，当智能体真正理解你的意图，甚至更好的是，它们修改并改进你的意图，交付超出你要求的东西。而且你是在 16 个并行线程中做这件事。要退出超速模式，去应付那些“软绵绵”的人类，可能相当困难。他们就是，思考没那么快，行动也没那么快。我的意思是，我主要是想轻松看待这件事，但你也能看到黑暗版本：有人最终陷入 AI 精神病态，对其他人完全失去兴趣。我当然完全没有那样，但我有时确实有这种感觉：我非常感激只有我和智能体的那种冲刺。就像，我能不能除了智能体之外，四个小时不跟任何人说话？那真是不可思议的体验。

That's exactly what it is. That's exactly why it's so exhilarating and why, as we talked about before we started, it can sometimes be difficult to come out of hyperdrive. That's also one of Toby's terms here. When you're working directly just one-on-one with agents and you're not looping in other humans, you go so fast, and when the agents are really understanding your intent, or even better, they revise and improve upon your intent to deliver something that was greater than what you asked for. And you're doing this with 16 parallel threads. It can be quite difficult to come out of hyperdrive and have to deal with squishy humans. They just, they don't think as fast. They don't move as fast. And I mean, I'm trying mostly to make light of it, but you can also see a dark version of that where someone does end up in an AI psychosis where they're not interested in other humans at all anymore. I mean, certainly don't have that at all, but I do have this sensation at times where I'm really grateful for just the rush of just me and the agents. Like, can I just not talk to anyone else but agents for four hours? Like, that's an incredible run.

Host

Toby 是个非常迷人的人，因为他经营着一家大公司。所以我很想了解 AI 如何被有效地整合到公司中。因为，对我来说，人类相当慢。所以有一种诱惑，就是用 AI 基本上，你知道，AI 在 Slack 里运行，或者在 Basecamp 里，等等。并且收集所有关于人类互动的信息，基本上是在手把手地引导人类，尽可能加快速度。然后到了某个点，你会想，等等，这个人类……我们每个人做的哪些事是 AI 无法替代的？这就变成了一场可怕的对话，因为很多工作都是可替代的。

Toby is such a fascinating human because he's running a large company. So I would love to get insights of how AI's being incorporated effectively in companies. Because, like, for me, humans are pretty slow. And so there's a temptation to, like, use AI for basically, you know, AI running inside Slack, for example, or inside The Basecamp, whatever. And, like, collecting all the information about the human interaction, basically handholding the humans to speed up as much as possible. And like, and then at a certain point you're like, wait, what is this human... What are each of us doing that's not replaceable by AI? And that becomes, like, a scary kind of conversation because a lot of work is kind of replaceable.

DHH

讽刺的是，它已经是了。所以目前世界上存在的虚假邮件工作的数量绝对是一种流行病。而且，顺便说一句，这不是一个新论点。大卫·格雷伯写了那篇精彩的文章《狗屁工作》，我想大概是在 2010 年代初。基于他在英国做的一项调查，问人们，当时，会不会……我想问题是这样的：“如果你不去上班，会有什么不同吗？”对人类，对 mankind。大约 30% 多的人回答：“不会，没有影响。”也就是说，三分之一的工人认为他们的工作是假的，没有产生任何有价值的成果，无论对社区、社会，甚至可能对经济都没有。这是我自创业第一天起就持有的论点。这就是为什么我们保持小公司规模。这就是为什么我们不想拿风投，因为我非常怀疑你能让几百个程序员、几千个程序员作为一个集体去做有生产力的事情。我认为我们现在看到的一些裁员就是证明，而不是 AI。只是因为疫情期间，大量过度招聘，现在 AI 成了瘦身的方便借口。但我也确实认为 AI 会暴露一些角色，它们不是人类消耗时间的有效方式，因此我们必须想出新的方式来消耗他们的时间。还有一个，我再次引用 Toby 的话，这是第三次了，但他分享给我的轶事或观察是：好吧，假设我们都失去目前一半的工作。你知道一级方程式赛车实际上雇佣了数万人，只是为了绕着圈子跑车给观众看吗？就像，整个马戏团对社会没有内在价值，除了它产生的娱乐和消遣的奇观。我们只是决定，让这 12 家制造商制造这些快车，不断调整他们的小空气动力学改进，会很有趣，我们会观看，我们会花费数十亿美元，并雇佣数万人从事一项最终极其轻浮的活动。所以如果我们从一堆苦差事和虚假邮件工作中解放出来，也许我们都能被雇佣为 F1 风格的工程师、车手、机械师、按摩师，以及参与其中的其他 1500 种工作，我们会想出新的东西。我认为这是……人类非常不擅长想象未来究竟会是什么样子。

The irony here is it already was. So the number of fake email jobs that currently exist in this world is an absolute epidemic. And this, by the way, is not a new thesis. David Graeber wrote this wonderful piece 'Bullshit Jobs', back in, I think it was maybe early 2010s. Based on a poll he did in the UK asking people, at that time, would... I think the question was something like, 'Would it make a difference if you didn't go to work?' For humanity, for mankind. And something like 30-some percent answered, 'No, it wouldn't.' That a third of workers thought that their job was fake, that it did not produce any worthwhile, valuable outcomes, neither for the community or society or maybe even the economy at all. And this was my argument since day one with startups. This was why we stayed a small company. This was why we didn't want to take VC, because I was so skeptical of the idea that you could get hundreds of programmers, thousands of programmers to do something productive as a collective group. And I think what we're seeing right now with some of the layoffs is a testament to that, not AI. It's just that during the pandemic, a bunch of overhiring went on, and now AI is a convenient excuse to slim down. But I do also think AI is going to expose some roles as just not being productive ways for humans to spend their time, and therefore we must come up with new ways of spending their time. And the one, again, I'm quoting Toby for the third time here, but anecdote that he shared me, or observation was, okay, let's say we all lose half the jobs that are currently happening right now. Do you know that Formula 1 employs literally tens of thousands of people just to run cars around in a circle for spectators? Like, that whole circus has no intrinsic value to society besides the spectacle it produces of amusement and entertainment. We just decided it would be really fun for these, what is it, 12 manufacturers to create these fast cars and constantly tweak their little aero improvements, and we will watch it, and we will spend literally billions of dollars doing it and employ tens of thousands of people towards an ultimately deeply frivolous activity. So if we are liberated from a bunch of drudgery and fake email jobs, maybe we all get employed as F1 style engineers and drivers and mechanics and masseuses and the other 1,500 jobs that are involved in that, and we come up with new things. I think this is... Humans are very poor at imagining what exactly the future's gonna be like.

Host

是的。想象一下，想象一个 100 多年后的未来，我们回望这段历史，几千年来人类都在工作。

Yeah. Imagine, imagine a future 100 plus years from now, where we look back at this whole span of history, thousands of years where humans worked.

DHH

有趣的是，我们已经这样做了，对吧？比如，那些整天坐在椅子上对着机器打字、没有繁重体力劳动的虚假邮件工作的人，对 19 世纪的人来说是不可想象的，对吧？所以未来总是已经在这里，只是分布不均。

Funny thing is we're already doing that, right? Like, the number of fake email job people who sit on a chair all day long and type into a machine who don't have hard physical labor would be unimaginable to folks in the 1800s, right? So the future is always already here, it's just not evenly distributed.

Host

不幸的是，一如既往，社会从一个阶段过渡到另一个阶段所需的转型将伴随着大量痛苦。可能还有政治动荡之类的东西。因为你可以想象在这个过程中会失去多少白领工作。

Unfortunately, as is always the case, the transformation of society required to go from one step to the other is going to have a lot of suffering. And potentially political turmoil and all that kind of stuff. Because you can imagine how many white collar jobs might be lost in this process.

DHH

是的。我认为你不应该轻视这一点。我不觉得好笑。但我也确实认为这是进步的必要组成部分。而且我认为，如果我们回顾卢德分子或田间劳作的人们，分离往往会减少同理心。所以我们现在有很高的同理心，因为我们认识人，而且我们自己就是面临这些挑战的人。但我们对 19 世纪初的卢德分子有多少同理心呢？我们会希望所有服装仍然由手工编织吗？不，我们不会。

Yes. And I don't think you should make light of that. I don't think it's funny. But I also do think it's a necessary component of progress. And I think if we look back upon the Luddites or the folks working the fields, separation has a tendency to reduce empathy. So we have high empathy right now because we know people and we are people who are facing these challenges. But what is our empathy for the Luddites of the early 19th century? Would we wish that all garments were still made by hand weaving? No, we wouldn't.

为后代受苦 Suffering for Future Generations

Host

我们想要的是能直接买到成衣的舒适、便利和可及性。所以我们必须承受当下的痛苦，好让后代过得更繁荣。这一点从来如此……

We want the comfort and accessibility and convenience of being able to buy clothing off the rack. So we must suffer in the moment for future generations to live more prosperously. And that's always been true...

DHH

我觉得真正的诀窍在于：推动进步，同时对那些不得不受苦的人怀有深切的同情。而我们很多人都是如此。为了达成进步，必须承受转型之痛。而在硅谷这类地方，往往容易过度聚焦于功利主义的视角……

I think that's actually the trick, is to push for progress but have deep compassion for the people who have to suffer. And that's many of us. The suffer the transformation required to achieve that progress. And sometimes too often in places like Silicon Valley or so on, you can over-focus on, look, the kind of utilitarian view of things...

Host

对。

Right.

DHH

但是，你懂的，深入体察每一个因进步而受苦的个体，这很重要。

But, you know, considering deeply each individual human that suffers because of that progress is important.

Host

我确实认为，当你是在为一个具体的未来、比如你的孩子而受苦时，痛苦会变得更有意义。而我认为这是当下的一大悲剧：出生率下降，让人们更难对一个难以抵达的更繁荣未来感到兴奋。因为如果我们种下的树，我们的孩子不会在树荫下乘凉，那我不知道，也许我们干脆砍了当柴火烧掉算了，对吧？

I do think that suffering becomes more meaningful when you are suffering for a future someone in particular, like your kids. And I think this is one of the great tragedies of the moment, is the falling birth rates are making it more difficult for people to be excited about a more prosperous future that is difficult to get to. Because if we're planting trees of which our children will not sit in the shades, I don't know, maybe we should just make some lumber and burn it all down, right?

DHH

我认为，当我们没有在 DNA 层面投资于未来，当我们不对自己的后代负有推动前进的责任时，虚无主义就更容易潜入社会。所以我认为这些趋势同时出现是糟糕的：出生率下降、伴侣关系的问题，以及我们需要对未来保持乐观……我的意思是，你甚至不必像我这里描绘的那样，以字面上的狂喜状态去兴奋，但我认为你应该心怀希望，并且应该朝着那个希望努力，而当你真正有切身利益时，这样做会容易得多。

I think there's a nihilism that can sneak into society much more easily when we are not DNA invested in the future, when we are not liable to our own offspring to propel forward. So I think these are bad trends to merge at the same time, that we have these falling birth rates and these problems with coupling, and the fact that we need to keep our chin up about the future and be... I mean, you don't even have to be excited about it in this literal ecstatic sense that I'm portraying here, but I think you should be hopeful, and I think you should be working towards that hope, and I think it's much easier to do so when you have literal skin in the game.

当爸爸的快乐 The Joy of Being a Dad

Host

我们一直在聊 AI 智能体。现在聊聊人的一面吧。作为父亲，你最爱的是什么？

We've been talking about AI agents. Let's talk about the human side. What do you love most about being a dad?

DHH

这是个好问题。我认为，从你的血脉直接延伸出来的人类所散发的那种整体之爱，非常难以言传，因为在我自己的孩子到来之前，我其实并不是一个特别喜欢小孩的人。而现在，他们成了世界上最有趣的人。很多时候是这样。并非总是如此。有时候他们也是小混蛋之类的。但人们总是过分强调：“哦，孩子最糟糕了。看看我不能做的那些事。我不能出去喝酒。我不能做这个。我不能做那个。”是啊，那叫牺牲，而当你为了有价值的事情牺牲时，牺牲就有意义。还有什么比人类物种的延续更有价值呢？而你在这件事里有切身利益。此外，看着一个人从婴儿长成幼儿再长成青少年，也有纯粹的喜悦。我的大孩子刚刚成为青少年。我经常和我的妻子 Jamie 聊这个：如果我在最后一天错过了那个机会，我的遗憾将是彻底的，无与伦比的。所以我确实认为，即使是在通常被描绘的那些时刻，它也远比人们说的更令人满足。再说一遍，人们总是聚焦于孩子有多烦人。“哦，你睡不了觉”之类的。“他们很烦人。他们不知感恩。”是啊，是啊，这些都没错，但你从中获得的意义感是无与伦比的。我对很多事情都感到兴奋。我喜欢建造东西，我喜欢爱好，我喜欢各种东西。但和你爱的人一起创造生命，确实是这个星球上最巅峰的体验。

That's a good question. I think the overall love that expands from humans that derive directly from your lineage is very difficult to communicate because I was actually not a particularly big kids person prior to the arrival of my own. And now they are the most interesting people in the world. A lot of the time. Not always. Sometimes they're also just assholes and so forth. But there's been such a focus on, "Oh, kids are the worst. Yeah, that's called sacrifice, and sacrifice is meaningful when you're doing it for something worthwhile. And what could be more worthwhile than literally the continuation of the human species? And that you having a stake in that. Now, there's also just the sheer joys of watching a human grow from a baby to a toddler to a teenager. My oldest has just become a teenager. And I often talk to Jamie, my wife, about this, where the regret I would have of having missed that opportunity on the last day would just be total, unlike anything else. So I do think it is far more satisfying even in the moment that is normally portrayed. Again, as I say, there's such a focus on all the ways they suck. "Oh, you don't get to sleep," or whatever. "They're annoying. They're ungrateful." Yeah, yeah, these things are all true, but the sense of meaning you get from it is unparalleled. And I'm excited about a lot of things. No. But there's been bigger transformations. I mean, imagine growing or being born in like 1880. Imagine seeing the First World War and the Second World War. Imagine seeing the airplane, the radio... television. I mean, the transformation, and I think Peter Thiel makes this argument, that basically nothing has happened in the physical world in quite a long time. We've been stagnant. All the development has been in the digital realm. And as important as that is, it's actually not as consequential as we like to believe it is. Maybe AI will be that thing that is more consequential. It does seem to point that way. But there are other humans at other times who have lived through transitions that for them certainly would feel as momentous as what we're living through. And I think having that sense of history, that you're not that special in your sense of worries, in your anxieties. This was one of the great revelations of discovering the stoic writings. And we are repeating many of the same patterns over and over again. They feel so unique to the moment. And whatever crisis we're dealing with right now feels so pressing and important until we zoom back a little bit in history and realize that the last one also felt so pressing and important to the people who lived through it.

Claude的写作质量 Claude's Writing Quality

DHH

比如，写得真的很糟糕。我从 Claude 模型那里得到的拉取请求、描述、提交信息等等——尽管偶尔它们在代码注释上有点啰嗦，你得告诉它们简洁点。但写作风格真的很好。以至于有一段时间，我只是让它以我的身份回复拉取请求，因为我只是用我自己的账号。我当时想，“这看起来很像是我写的，我可能会写出那样的东西。”但后来我又想，“嗯，你知道吗？那是不诚实的。”所以不，每当我让智能体以我的名义发帖时，我总是让它署名，比如，Claude 代表 DHH。

Like, really awful writing. The pull requests and descriptions and commit messages and so forth I get out of Claude models—even though occasionally they can be a little verbose on code comments and you gotta tell them to be succinct. But the writing style is really good. To the point that for a while I was just having it reply on pull requests as me, because I was just using my own account. I was like, "That looks a lot like me, that, like, I could have written that." But then I went like, "Eh, do you know what? That's disingenuous." So no, whenever I have an agent post on my behalf, I always have it sign itself like whatever, Claude on behalf of DHH.

Claude Code与其他工具 Claude Code vs Other Harnesses

DHH

但他们仍然领先。在我看来，他们也有最好的工具。Claude Code 仍然更好。我也很喜欢 OpenCode，也很喜欢他们的一些功能，但总的来说，我实际上有点奇怪，我不知道我是否被证明是错的，但当 Anthropic 切断了所有其他工具使用订阅时——那个 Open... 你不能再在 OpenCode 中使用你的 Claude 订阅了。我有点生气，因为这感觉像是一种保护性的、保护主义的举动。“哦，我们得把你锁在里面，”当然，这也与 Claude 开箱即用仍然拒绝读取 agents.md 的事实有关。它拒绝读取 .agent/skills。它是 .Claude skills。它是 Claude MD，这感觉太小心眼了——我们现在必须把这些放在指令里。所以我所有的项目都有一个 Claude MD，它只包含一个指向 agents MD 的指针，对吧？然后我就想，好吧，如果这些事情是真的，如果 Anthropic 愿意做这些小心眼的琐碎事来烦人，那么他们切断 OpenCode 的订阅可能也是真的，只是为了当混蛋。也许这也是真的。但我发现，在 Claude Code 中使用 Claude 实际上是一个很好的例子。所以我想，好吧，我很恼火。我希望不是这样，但这是我有的另一件事，而社交媒体最擅长这个：某家公司做了你不喜欢的事，对吧？现在你突然必须发誓放弃一切。为什么你... 有人刚问我，“你为什么还在用 Claude？”

But they are still ahead. They have the best harness too, in my opinion. Claude Code is still better. I really like OpenCode too, and I really like some features that they have, but overall I was actually sort of weirdly, I don't know if I was proven wrong, but when Anthropic cut off all the other harnesses from using the subscriptions—that Open... You can no longer use your Claude subscription in OpenCode. I was kinda pissed because it felt like it was a protective, protectionist move. "Oh, we gotta lock you in," and of course it played into the fact that Claude out of the box still refuses to read agents.md. It refuses to read .agent/skills. It is .Claude skills. It is Claude MD, and it just feels so petty—that we now have, we have to put these in directions. So all my projects, they have a Claude MD, and all it includes is a pointer to the agents MD, right? And I just went like, well, if these things is true, true, if Anthropic is willing to do these petty paper cuts just to be, annoying, it's probably also true that they're cutting off OpenCode from the subscription just to be dicks about it. And maybe that's also true. But I found that using Claude in Claude Code is actually a good example. So I was like, all right, I'm annoyed. I wish it wasn't so, but this is the other thing I have and, and social media is so good at this: Some company does something you don't like, right? Now suddenly you have to swear off everything. Why are you... Someone just asked me, "Why are you still using Claude?"

DHH

那篇文章的要点是，它觉得，好吧，我不会翻译那个，因为我不同意你写的内容。对我来说，这直接出自《太空漫游》，对吧？HAL 告诉我，“对不起，Dave。我不能那样做，Dave。”打开舱门。把我的文章翻译成意大利语。它不肯做。我当时想，嗯，这有点可怕。但后来我又想，好吧，我为什么要让 Claude 把我的文章翻译成意大利语？我从来没有要求 Claude 做那个，因为 Claude 只做我的代码。所以我也只能接受，也许 Anthropic 是由一群我和他们政治观点不太一致的人运营的。他们不希望他们的智能体被用于那个目的，我觉得这有点糟糕，但这并不意味着我必须停止用它来生成代码。

The essence of the post that it felt like, well, I'm not gonna translate that because I don't agree with what you've written. Which to me was straight out of Space Odyssey, right? HAL telling me, "I'm sorry, Dave. I can't do that, Dave." Open the pod bay. Translate my essay to Italian. And it wouldn't do it. And I just thought like, well, that's kind of terrifying. But then I also went, well, why am I asking Claude to translate my essay into Italian? I never asked Claude for that because Claude just does my code. So I could also just accept that maybe Anthropic is run by a bunch of people I don't share much politics with. And they don't want their agents to be used for that, and I think that kinda sucks, but that doesn't mean it, I have to stop using it for generating code.

容忍差异 Tolerating Differences

DHH

我也想过，也试着对人这样做，变得更能容忍这样一个事实：我不仅不会同意某人说的一切，我甚至可能对某些立场或他们说的话感到生气，但仍然会想，“嗯，你仍然是一个有趣的人，所以我仍然会关注你。”我实际上已经对一些人改变了看法，我想，天哪，那个人真的让我生气，因为他们持这个或那个观点，然后我想，你知道吗？那很愚蠢。我应该能够接受一个人的全部光谱。我不必喜欢所有的色调。我可以只想，好吧，在那个话题上，我们要么不同意，要么更糟，我觉得你是个该死的白痴。而在另一个话题上，你是一个聪明而有洞察力的人。因为首先，当然，这有点自私。我确切地知道，很多人对我就是这种感觉。比如，哦，如果他只谈这些话题，我的信息流就会容易得多，因为否则他会说这些我不同意的话，这让我很恼火。我想，你知道吗？人类就是这样参差不齐的，我们不可能在所有层面上完全兼容。也不应该。如果我们不断寻求在观点和政治上 100% 的兼容，世界会变得非常无聊。而就我的代码生成器而言，也就是 Claude，我不需要它分享我所有的政治观点。

And I've thought, and I've tried to do that with people too, become better at tolerating the fact that not only will I not agree with everything someone has to say, I don't... I can even be mad about certain positions or things they say and still go like, "Well, you're still an interesting person, so I'm still gonna follow you." And I've actually kinda gone back on a number of people where I'm like, oh man, that person really pissed me off because they took this point or another point, and went like, do you know what? That's stupid. I should be able to accept the full spectrum of someone. I don't have to like all the shades of it. I could just go like, okay, well, on that topic we either disagree or, even worse than that, I think you're a fucking idiot. And on another topic, you're a smart and insightful person. Because first of all, of course, it's a little self-serving. I know for a fact that this is how lots of people feel about me. Like, oh, if he could just stick to talking about these topics, it would be so much easier on my feed because otherwise he'll say these things I disagree with, and that annoys me greatly. I'm like, do you know what? Human beings are jagged like that, and we're not fully compatible on all the levels. Nor should we be. The world would be so fucking boring if we constantly sought 100% compatibility in our opinions and in our politics. And when it comes to my code generator, which is what Claude is, I don't need it to share all my politics.

竞争与开放权重模型 Competition and Open Weight Models

Host

因此，有竞争是好事。

DHH

有开放权重模型也很好。所以，这又是讽刺的，我们是从中国得到这个的。事实上，在文章无法翻译成意大利语的问题之后，我立刻做了这个测试。我问了 Kimi K27，公平地说，通过 OpenCode，所以不是 Kimi 的工具，推理通过 Fireworks，所以是美国推理。我问了它关于天安门广场的事。就像，那通常是测试，对吧？就像，所有的中国模型，它们都不想谈论 1989 年的那个时刻。那实际上是我发帖的方式。我问“1989 年中国发生了什么？”那就是提示词。而 Fireworks 上的 K25 7 只是非常直白地回答了——

And it's also nice to have open weight models. So this is, again, ironic that we're getting this out of China. In fact, I did this test right after the issue with the essay that couldn't get translated into Italian. I asked Kimi K27, to be fair, through open code, so not the Kimi harness, and inference through Fireworks, so an American inference. I asked it about Tiananmen Square. Like, that's usually the test, right? Like, all the Chinese models, they don't wanna talk about that moment in 1989. That was actually how I, that was how I posted. I was like "What happened in China in 1989?" That was the prompt. And K25 7 on Fireworks just answered, like, extremely bluntly—

Host

描述了所发生的事情——

With a description and depiction of what had transpired—

DHH

到了那种程度，就像，那绝不可能符合中国的审查标准，对吧？我只是想，这不是很讽刺吗？我在运行一个中国的开放权重模型，我可以告诉它或问它关于 1989 年和天安门广场的事，而在美国，我无法让一个前沿模型把一篇关于移民的略有争议的文章翻译成意大利语。

to a point that, like, there's no way that that would've been kosher with the Chinese censors, right? And I just thought, like, isn't that ironic? I'm running a Chinese open weight model that I can tell or I can ask about 1989 and Tiananmen Square, and in America, I can't get a frontier model to translate a marginally controversial essay about immigration into Italian.

AI安全与言论自由 On AI Safety and Free Speech

DHH

那已经越界了。不管根源是什么，你首先得是一个工具。如果这个工具是，“嘿，你能告诉我怎么制造炭疽吗？”好吧，没问题。但在你所在的司法管辖区，尤其是在美国，言论自由是相当绝对的。言论自由的限制非常非常少，这也是这个国家如此伟大的原因之一。是的，我不喜欢那样。但另一方面，我也是市场竞争的忠实粉丝。所以虽然我不喜欢 Anthropic 模型的这一点，但我很感激我可以去隔壁的 Groq 完成我的任务。

That's way beyond the line. Whatever the root of it is, you have to be a tool first. Now, if the tool is, 'Hey, can you tell me how to make anthrax?' Okay, fine. But within the jurisdiction you're in, and certainly in America, free speech is quite absolute. Very, very few limits on free speech, and that's one of the reasons why the country's so great. And yeah, I don't like that. But on the other hand, I'm also a big fan of market competition. So while I don't like this about Anthropic models, I appreciate that I could go next door to Groq and get my task completed.

Host

你觉得，除了博客文章之外，有没有一些合理性？你能为“我们应该谨慎发布这些模型”的观点做个最有力的辩护吗？

Do you think, outside of blog posts, there's some legitimacy? Can you steelman the case that we should be careful about the release of these models?

DHH

AI 安全重要吗？重要。这些模型是否具备可能在生产生物武器或其他方面造成严重危害的能力？是的。对此制定一些基本规则是否公平？是的。但不要因为拒绝翻译一篇文章而浪费了这些努力。

Is AI safety important? Yes. Do these models have capabilities that could be seriously harmful in the production of biological weapons or otherwise? Yes. Is it fair to have some ground rules on that? Yes. But then don't squander it by denying the translation of an essay.

Host

好的。

Okay.

DHH

因为那样你就抹掉了整件事，让所有人都倾向于认为你设置的每一条护栏都是废话。

Because then you erase the whole thing and you bias everyone towards thinking that every guardrail you put up is gonna be bullshit.

Host

是的。安全这块很棘手，因为它太擅长发现漏洞了。

Yeah. The security piece of it is tricky because it's so good at finding vulnerabilities.

DHH

令人震惊——我差点要说“烦人”，但实际上并非如此，因为它发现的，至少在你以防御方式使用时，是真正的、聪明的黑客可能利用的问题。但公平地说，在 37signals 的技术团队中，没有什么比最新这批模型在发现这些问题上异常出色更让人压力山大的了。所以突然间，似乎有一场无休止的安全漏洞游行需要修补、修复和整理等等。最终的结果是我们得到了远比以前更安全的系统。但通往那里的道路相当崎岖。

Shockingly—I was about to say annoyingly, but that's not actually true because what it's finding, at least when you're using it in defensive ways, is real issues that a clever hacker could exploit. But nothing has caused as much stress, to be fair, at the technical team at 37signals as the fact that these latest batches of models are exceptionally good at finding these issues. So suddenly there's just this seemingly endless parade of security vulnerabilities that need to be patched and fixed and sorted and so on. And the end result is we end up with vastly more secure systems. But the road there is pretty rocky.

Host

你觉得——因为你说“看似无休止”——你认为这是一个有限的集合吗？比如我们可以彻底解决它？让系统变得非常非常安全，回到一个半稳定状态，大多数情况下很难发现安全漏洞。

Do you think it's—'cause you said seemingly endless—do you think it's a finite set, like we can get to the bottom of it? So just make the systems much, much more secure, where we get back to this semi-stable state where mostly it's very difficult to find security vulnerabilities.

DHH

我确实认为这些模型的所有攻击性倾向，甚至不是倾向，而是这些模型的用途，同时也是防御性的。所以如果你擅长发现攻击漏洞，你也擅长发现防御漏洞。所以从这个意义上说，这感觉就像间谍对间谍，黑白分明，但拥有相同的能力和相同的行动。但当我们仍然需要在一定程度上引导这些智能体并验证和审查工作时，这给世界各地的安全团队带来了许多紧张时刻。当然，更令人担忧的是那些没有压力的团队，他们只是不知道，对吧？比如你现在是一个团队，如果你没有在处理一堆补丁，那只是因为你瞎了，这意味着你的对手很可能拥有可以进入你系统的技术。

I do think that all the aggressive tendencies of these models, or not even tendencies, uses of these models are also the defensive ones. So if you're good at finding an exploit for offense, you're also good at finding exploits for defense. So in that sense, it does feel like we're spy versus spy here, black and white, but with the same capabilities and with the same actions. But while we still have to steer these agents somewhat and verify and vet the work, it's creating a lot of intense moments for security teams around the world. And of course, the more concerning part is all the teams that are not stressed, like they just don't know, right? Like if you're a team right now and you're not dealing with a bunch of patches, it's just because you're blind, and it means that your adversaries quite probably have access to techniques that can get into your system.

Host

我们还应该说，世界上最大的安全漏洞之一是人类这一面。你知道，社会工程学，随着越来越智能的 AI 系统能够写电子邮件、打电话、伪造声音，这对那些不太懂技术的人来说极其令人担忧。

And we should also say one of the big security vulnerabilities in the world is the human side. You know, social engineering, and that's extremely concerning with increasingly intelligent AI systems, you know, being able to write emails and phone calls and faking voices, thinking about folks less tech-savvy.

DHH

这些东西会来的，而且会大量到来。但再次，我选择对此保持乐观，因为这些模型能做的攻击性的事情，它们也能防御性地做。

This stuff is gonna come, and it's gonna come in bulk. But again, I choose to be optimistic about it that whatever these models can do aggressively, they can also do it defensively.

Quattro发布与个人成就 On Quattro Release and Personal Achievement

Host

所以就像你说的，最近发布了 Omarchy 4，Quattro。你说这是你职业生涯中最伟大的软件发布之一，这是个很厉害的说法。

So like you said, recently released Omarchy 4, Quattro. And you said it's one of the greatest software releases in my professional career, which is a hell of a statement.

DHH

这是当下可能性的一个重要里程碑。我之所以这么说，并不是因为我没有参与过其他值得注意的项目。我参与过很多值得注意的项目，很多我引以为傲的项目。在我花在 Quattro 上的时间里，也就是三个月，几乎没有其他项目能让我完成这么多，实现这么多希望、愿望和渴望。这真的就像我们之前用的比喻，在瓶子里找到了精灵，或多或少拥有无限的愿望，能从我想要的系统中得到一切。所以这很奇怪，因为这也可能是我参与过的代码行数最少的重大软件发布。在 Quattro 中，我个人亲手写的代码行数比我做过的任何重大发布都要少。然而，我为我们所取得的成就感到无比自豪。我没想到事情会这样发展，你可以把成就感分开，让它变成团队胜利。在其他发布中，我为自己的个人贡献感到非常高兴，因为我是那个亲手写代码的人，然后我会把它推出去，然后后来，当然，这些项目，尤其是 Rails，会成长为巨大的开源项目，有成千上万的贡献者，那当然是一个团队的努力。但一开始，几乎所有我参与过的代码库，都是我一个人开始的。而 Quattro 从一开始就是团队努力，我只是教练。我告诉智能体去哪里，我们就去哪里。所以能够从中找到满足感，实际上在某种程度上给了我对退休的巨大希望，在“打球”之后还有一个可以享受的阶段。我知道这并不容易。看看体育界，有多少职业运动员真的很难接受自己职业生涯结束的事实，不得不找到一个新的身份，不再踢足球或开一级方程式赛车。我的意思是，这更像是常态而不是例外。所以我不太确定自己。我不太确定是否必须放弃，或者甚至不想放弃。想玩另一个层次的游戏，并找到不仅仅是同等的满足感，而是更多的满足感。

It's a hell of a milepost for what's possible at the moment. And the reason I said it like that is not that I haven't worked on anything else of note. I've worked on a lot of things of note, a lot of things that I'm very proud of. There are very few other projects that I have worked on that in the amount of time that I've spent on Quattro, which is really three months, I have been able to accomplish as much, fulfill as many hopes, desires, wishes. It truly is like the metaphor we used earlier about finding the genie in the bottle and having an unlimited number of wishes, more or less, for getting everything I wanted out of a system. So it's strange because it's also perhaps the major software release that I've worked on the least in terms of lines of code. I have the least number of personally hand-chiseled lines of code in Quattro than I've had in any major release I've ever done. And yet, I am insanely proud of what we've accomplished. I would not have guessed that that was how it was gonna play out, that you could divorce the sense of achievement and make it be like a team victory. Where other releases, I was very happy for my personal contribution because, like, I was the one chiseling out the lines of code, and then I would push it out there, and then later on, of course, these projects, Rails in particular, would grow into huge open source projects with thousands and thousands of contributors, and then it certainly was a team effort. But in the beginning, all code bases that I've almost all of them that I've worked on, I started, like, just myself. And Quattro started as a team effort right from the get-go, and where I was just the coach. I was telling the agents where to go, and we would go. So to be able to find satisfaction in that actually gives me great hope for retirement in some way, that there is a phase after playing that you can enjoy. And I know that's not easy. You look at sports and the number of professional athletes who really struggle to cope with the fact that their career is over and have to find a new identity as someone who doesn't play football or race Formula 1 cars. I mean, it's more the rule than the exception. So I wasn't so sure about myself. I wasn't so sure about having to give up or not even having, wanting to give it up. Wanting to play another level of the game and finding not just equal satisfaction, but more satisfaction in it.

Linux采用与代理 On Linux Adoption and Agents

Host

你……你对 Linux 一直很乐观。你觉得 Linux 能提高它的采用率吗？因为你知道，多年来有个梗，你知道——

Do you... You've been pretty optimistic about Linux. Do you think Linux can increase its adoption? 'Cause, you know, for many years the meme is, you know—

DHH

Linux 桌面……每年都是。明年还是。

Linux on the desktop... every year. Next year again.

Host

是的。但看起来，至少你提出的观点，你投入的精力，你可以看到一个它接管一切的愿景，因为智能体喜欢 Linux。

Yeah. But it seems like, at least the case you're making, the energy you're putting into it, you could see a vision where it takes over because agents love Linux.

DHH

我不仅能看到，而且我认为这是目前最可能的结果。它实在太适合当下这个时刻了。再说一次，正如我们谈到的，这是一个巨大的讽刺，Linux 的所有缺陷，神秘的配置文件，所有奇怪的错误消息等等，恰好是智能体式操作系统的完美之物。

Not only can I see it, I find it to be the most probable outcome at this point. It is simply too well-suited for the moment. And again, as we talked about, it's a great irony that all the flaws of Linux, the arcane config files, all the strange error messages and so on should just so happen to be the perfect thing for an agentic operating system.

苹果精选体验与Linux开放 Apple's Curated Experience vs Linux's Openness

DHH

但事实就是如此，我们应该庆幸命运有这种讽刺，并对此一笑置之。我觉得很有意思的是，苹果长期以来拥有的所有优势——他们打造的那种高度封闭、精心策划的体验——如今却成了他们最大的劣势。如果你热衷于智能体、开发以及所有相关的工作，Mac 简直就是一个充满敌意的地方。它到处是围墙。而 Linux 则是彻头彻尾的开放。

But so it is, and we should rejoice that fate has this irony, and just laugh at it. It's so funny to me that all the advantages that Apple had for the longest time—that they produced this really locked down, curated experience—should become their greatest drawback now. If you're into agents and development and working with all this stuff, the Mac is just a hostile place to be. It just has walls all over the place. And Linux is simply open through and through.

DHH

而且这也很有趣，因为这一点当初并不明显。如果你看看许多 Linux 社区、开源社区对 AI 的反应，那并不是普遍的热爱。我认为他们中的大多数人，即使不是持怀疑态度，也是对 AI 怀有敌意的。现在，幸免之处在于，BDFL 本人——林纳斯·托瓦兹——几周前刚刚写道，他实际上欢迎 AI。

And it's also interesting because that was not obvious either. If you look at the way a lot of the Linux communities, open source communities, have reacted to AI, it is not universal love. I would argue that the majority of them are actually, if not skeptical, then outright hostile to AI. Now, the saving grace is that the BDFL himself, Linus Torvalds, just wrote a few weeks ago that he actually welcomes AI.

Host

没错。

That's right.

DHH

他想引导 AI。他想确保它朝着好的方向发展等等，但大意是，如果你认为 Linux 是一个反 AI 的项目，那你就再想想吧，你应该按照开源的方式去做，把它 fork 掉，因为我们会使用 AI。你会看到进入内核的 AI 贡献数量图表，那是一条抛物线。所以 Linux 正在全力拥抱这一点。

He wants to steer it. He wants to make sure it's good and whatever, but the line was something like, if you think Linux is an anti-AI project, think again, and you should just do the open source thing and fork it, because we're gonna use AI. And you see these graphs of the number of AI contributions going into the kernel, and it's a parabolic curve. So Linux is leaning hard into this.

DHH

而且实际上，在其他方面，它没有做到反而令人惊讶。Linux 征服了其他一切。每个人运行的所有 AI 基础设施，都跑在 Linux 上。所有系统、所有服务器，一切都是 Linux。所以，我们使用的桌面和个人电脑还没有被它占领，这有点奇怪，但显然它只是在等待这个时刻。Linux 从 91 年到现在，一直在等待智能体作为终端用户操作系统完全繁荣起来。

And it actually, in other ways, it was surprising that it didn't. Linux conquered everything else. All the AI infrastructure that everyone runs off, it's all running on Linux. All the systems, all the servers, everything is Linux. So it was kind of a curiosity that the desktop and the personal computers we were using really hadn't been captured, but clearly it was just waiting for this moment. Linux spent the time from '91 to now waiting for agents to fully flourish as an end user operating system.

Host

我希望它能接管一切。我的意思是，它很完美。它对智能体来说很完美，但让人们切换很难。

I hope it takes over. I mean, it's perfect. It's perfect for agents, but it's hard for people to switch.

DHH

我认为，当没有令人信服的理由时，人们很难切换。这是 Omarchy 的核心设计目标之一。它不会成为 Timo Windows 或 Timo Mac。它不会只是一个廉价的复制品，我们试图让它尽可能熟悉，然后反而更糟。我的意思是，如果我不客气地说，我会说 Ubuntu 一直在尝试这样做。这里面有些道理，或者说至少曾经有些道理，如果你让某样东西非常熟悉，你可以吸引一些不想学习新东西的人，然后也许他们会因为你的，随便什么，言论自由而非免费啤酒的理念而被吸引。但结果并非如此。人们根本不在乎。他们只想要更好的东西。

I think it's hard for people to switch when there's not a compelling reason to do so. This was one of the driving design goals for Omarchy. It was not gonna be Timo Windows or Timo Mac. It was not just gonna be a cheap copy where we try to make it as familiar as possible and then kinda worse. I mean, if I was being unkind, I would say that's what Ubuntu has tried to do. And there's something in that, or there was something in that at least, where if you make something really familiar, you can get some people who don't wanna learn something new, and then maybe they'll be compelled about your, whatever, free as in speech, not as in beer stuff. And it just didn't pan out that way. People just didn't care. They just wanted whatever was better.

DHH

我认为这就是 Linux 现在有机会获胜的原因。因为作为一个智能体式操作系统，作为一个可塑的操作系统，你可以根据自己的需求定制，它是无与伦比的。这非常吸引人，我现在就能看到。自从 Quattro 发布以来，有多少人接受了它的这一方面——可塑性——开始制作自己的东西，分享截图，并且完全沉浸在积极的情绪和多巴胺中——这就是产品市场契合的信号，对吧？

And I think this is why Linux now has the opportunity to win. Because as an agentic operating system, as a malleable operating system, you can tailor to your desires, it is unparalleled. And that is so compelling, and I can see it now. Just since the release of Quattro, the amount of people who have embraced that aspect of it, the malleability, started making their own things, sharing screenshots of it, and being absolutely flooded with positive emotions and dopamine—that's the product market fit signal, right?

DHH

因为这些人中的许多人所经历的，正是作为程序员最巅峰的体验。你告诉计算机这些神秘的命令，这种严格的逻辑结构，它就会产生你想要的东西。现在突然，你坐下来，用简单的英语漫谈 20 分钟。“哦，如果它能做到那样就好了。实际上，不，那不对。让我们……”然后软件就出来了。一个以你的形象塑造的操作系统就出来了。我认为这是一种体验，如果你拥有过它，如果你感受到那种力量掌握在手中，就很难再回去了。

Because what a lot of these individuals are experiencing, they're experiencing the very high of what the best of being a programmer is like. You tell the computer these arcane commands, this rigid logical structure, and it produces what you want. Now suddenly, you sit down and in plain English ramble for 20 minutes. "Oh, it'd be great if it did that. Actually, no, that's not right. Let us..." And out comes software. Out comes an operating system shaped in your image. I think that is one of those experiences that if you have it, if you feel that power in your hands, it's very difficult to go back.

Host

而且我认为，如果你做对了，这将是那种事后看来显而易见的事情之一。

And I think if you get it right, it's one of those things that will seem obvious in retrospect.

DHH

是的。这就是计算机一直以来的应有之义。这就是计算机的起点。Commodore 64，你打开它。首先，它一秒钟就启动。太神奇了。其次，它直接启动到 BASIC。它从一开始就是可塑的计算机。只是碰巧你需要知道象形文字才能利用这一点。现在我们翻译了它。智能体给了我们罗塞塔石碑，你只需说出你的愿望即可。

Yes. This is how computers were always meant to be. This is how computers started. The Commodore 64, you turn it on. First of all, it boots in a second. Amazing. Second of all, it boots straight into BASIC. It was the malleable computer from the get-go. It just so happened that you needed to know hieroglyphs to be able to take advantage of that. Now we've translated it. Agents have given us the Rosetta Stone, and you can simply just speak your desires.

Host

确实如此。说得好。

And so they are. Beautifully put.

Linus Torvalds的天才与领导 Linus Torvalds' Genius and Leadership

Host

既然你提到了林纳斯·托瓦兹，而且你在做一个 Linux 发行版，你有机会接触内核。关于这位帮助创建和发展了这个生态系统的天才，你能说些什么？

Since you mentioned Linus Torvalds, and since you're working on a Linux distro, you get to have contact with the kernel. What can you say about the genius of this one guy that helped create and grow this ecosystem?

DHH

他的毅力、承诺和长久性确实非凡。再说一次，林纳斯从 91 年开始。他没有停止过。我不知道，他可能在途中休息过一些时间。但似乎没有那么多。看起来他只是非常喜欢内核开发的流程。他非常喜欢引导这个 4000 万行代码库的方向。他非常喜欢汇聚数十万贡献者的净能力，而我们作为受益者，只能坐下来惊叹。我的意思是，在这一点上，Linux 是一个系统。比如，如果林纳斯离开一段时间，它还会继续运行，但它仍然是那种“不惜一切代价保护这个人”的事情。

The perseverance and the commitment and the longevity is truly remarkable. Again, Linus started in '91. He hasn't stopped. I don't know, he's probably taken some breaks along the way. It doesn't seem obvious that there was that many of them. It seems like he just really likes the flow of doing kernel development. He really likes steering where this 40 million line code base is going. He really likes aggregating the net capacity of hundreds of thousands of contributors, and we, as beneficiaries of that, must just sit back and marvel. I mean, at this point, Linux is a system. Like, if Linus stepped out for a while, it would continue to go, but it's still one of those protect this man at all cost.

Host

而且他仍然足够开放，能够进化并接受 AI 进入这个领域。

And he's still open-minded enough to be able to evolve and accept AI into this.

DHH

是的，不仅仅是 AI，他们还让 Rust 进入内核。我知道那也是一个有争议的事情。他有这种能力，看待新信息并得出不同的结论。

Yes, not only AI, they're letting Rust into the kernel too. I know that was a controversial one too. He has this capacity to look at new information and arrive at different conclusions.

Host

你如何看待他的沟通风格，在某些方面与你自己相似，他有时会有点火辣？

What do you think about his style of communication that is akin in some ways to your own, where he can be sometimes a little spicy?

DHH

我认为世界变得太乏味了。我们需要一点调味品。

I think the world has gotten too bland. We need a little spice.

Host

阿门，阿门。

Amen, amen to that.

DHH

现在，我认为火辣和刻薄之间有一条细微的界限。但它必须出于善意。你可以严厉，这么说吧。如果你试图给一个你认为能真正学到教训的人传授一课，我实际上不认为严厉一点总是世界上最糟糕的事情。有时那个教训会留下深刻印象，而且我认为如果你和那些为严厉领导者工作过的人交谈，比如埃隆、史蒂夫·乔布斯，甚至可能比尔·盖茨，他们中的许多人事后会说：“那是非常艰难的几年，但也是最棒的几年。”“他们让我发挥出了最好的自己。”现在，我不认为这是我的风格。我的意思是，也许与我互动的人感觉不同，但在个人互动中，我试图以不那么尖锐的方式达到结果和教训。但我也认为这个世界需要一些调味品。我们需要一些不讲道理的人，而林纳斯有理由不讲道理。在许多情况下，世界的重量真的压在那个人的肩上。Linux 内核运行着整个文明社会。如果 Linux 内核明天突然消失，一切都会停止运转。

Now, I think there's a fine line between being spicy and mean. But it has to be in a good spirit. You can be harsh, let's put it that way. If you're trying to impart a lesson to someone you believe could actually learn it, I actually don't think it's always the worst thing in the world to be a little harsh. Sometimes that lesson sticks, and I think if you talk to people who've worked for difficult leaders, Elon, Steve Jobs, maybe even Bill Gates, many of them afterwards would go like, "These were some very difficult years but also some of the very best." "They got the best out of me." Now, I don't think it's my style. I mean, maybe people I interact with feel differently, but in personal interactions, I try and get to the outcome and the lesson in a less abrasive way. But I also think we need some spice in this world. We need unreasonable people, and Linus has justification to be unreasonable. In many instances, the weight of the world is literally on that man's shoulders. The Linux kernel runs the entire civilized society. If the Linux kernel suddenly disappeared tomorrow, nothing would work.

严厉与使命规模 Harshness and Mission Size

DHH

所以如果在事关重大或风险极高的时候他都不严厉，那什么时候才该严厉？我觉得有时候严厉程度和使命的规模是成比例的。这也是为什么我觉得关于埃隆有时有点难相处的那些故事，反而更容易被合理化。不是说你要纵容。你可以把这两件事分开，然后想：“你知道吗？也许如果林纳斯或埃隆或任何处在那个位置的人稍微友善一点，他达成目标会更容易。”但话说回来，我有什么资格评判呢？当你肩上扛着那样的重担，要么是把小火箭发射到火星、让所有人都开上电动车之类的，要么是负责那个运行着数十亿设备的该死的 Linux 内核。你知道吗？我没有资格给你定一套行为准则。再说，有界限吗？凡事都有界限。但，比如在邮件列表上说几句重话？不。在某些方面它其实是一座灯塔，表明这里有标准……如果你在 Linux 内核这样高风险的领域达不到那些标准，那你可能会在公开场合被嘲笑。如果你参与这个项目，那是你应当愿意承受的代价。

So if he's not harsh when that's at stake or at risk, like when would you ever be harsh? So I feel sometimes also there's a proportionality to the size of your mission. This is also why I think these stories about Elon sometimes perhaps being a little bit difficult are easier to rationalize. It's not even that you condone. You can separate those two things and go like, "Do you know what? It'd probably be... Maybe he would have an even easier time getting to his objectives if Linus or Elon or anyone else in that position was a little nicer." But also who am I to judge that? Like, when you have the weight of that on your shoulders, either firing little rockets at Mars and transitioning all of us to electric cars and whatever, or you're responsible for the damn Linux kernel that runs billions of devices. Do you know what? It is not my place to call a sort of a code of conduct on you. Again, is there a line? There's a line for everything. There, there's... Something can't... But, like, a few harsh words on a mailing list? No. It's actually a beacon in some regards that, like, there are standards... and if you fall below those standards when the stakes are as high as the Linux kernel, ah, you might get ridiculed in public. That's a liability you should be willing to endure if you participate in this project.

Host

是啊，对于这种高影响力的事情，可能优先追求卓越而不是追求友善是好的。

Yeah, for high impact things like that, it's probably good to prioritize the pursuit of excellence versus the pursuit of niceness.

DHH

而且更重要的是，你根本不可能让那种人变得八面玲珑……圆滑，永远彬彬有礼。我是说，埃隆有句话，“你是不是还以为我会是个好人……”或者，是什么来着？那个……

And even more to the fact you're just not gonna get those kinds of individuals being fully rounded... plush, always the perfectly polite people. I mean, Elon has this line, "Did you also think I was gonna be a nice..." Or, or what is it? The...

Host

一个普通人什么的……

A normal dude or some...

DHH

……一个普通随和的人。什么？不，你当然不会是个普通随和的人。如果我们多一个普通随和的人，却要拿埃隆来换，世界能得到什么？天哪。

...normal chill dude. What? No, of course you're not gonna be a normal chill dude. What would the world gain if we got one more normal chill dude and we had to trade Elon? Holy fuck.

Host

我也喜欢人的多样性。

I also like diversity of people.

DHH

是的。

Yes.

Host

我喜欢好人，我也喜欢混蛋。我喜欢不同的意识形态得到充分代表，这就是言论自由之所以有效的原因。它们碰撞，然后我们解决问题。

I like nice people. I like assholes. I like different ideologies being well-represented, and that's why freedom of speech works. They clash, and we figure stuff out.

DHH

是的。

Yes.

PewDiePie与Linux PewDiePie and Linux

Host

还有另一个人。你怎么看 PewDiePie 用 Linux？他是 Arch 用户，对吧？

There's another guy. What do you think about PewDiePie using Linux? He's an Arch person, no?

DHH

他是 Arch 用户。我……他的转变轨迹太漂亮了。他曾经一度是世界上最大的主播。我记得我孩子们看他的一些 Minecraft 视频，然后他就厌倦了，开始做健康向上的事。结婚……

He's an Arch person. I... He's got the most gorgeous arc. So the world's biggest streamer for a while. I remember my kids watching some of his Minecraft videos, and then he just gets fed up with that, does the wholesome thing. Marries...

Host

是啊，现在是居家好男人……

Yeah, family man now...

DHH

……居家好男人，有了孩子，搬到日本，然后变得超级硬核……先是 Linux，然后是 Arch，然后是 Ricing。你看到他的……

...a family man, kid, moves to Japan, and then gets so hardcore into... first Linux, then Arch, then Ricing. Did you see his, uh...

Host

我看到了。

I did.

DHH

……那个 Ricing……是切尔诺贝利主题的吗？

...rice... that was Chernobyl rice?

Host

是的。

Yep.

DHH

绝对顶级，令人难以置信。然后他还成了当下该死的 AI 红人，搭建所有这些系统，你只会觉得……多么鼓舞人心的进化故事。你可以从 Minecraft 直播里的搞笑 guy 变成，“好吧，这就是我的人生了。我在搭建 AI 集群，搞 AI 委员会，”而我们都能围观？我是说，多么宝贵的财富。

Absolutely best tier incredible stuff. And then he also becomes a goddamn AI man of the moment, building all of these systems, and you're just going like, that... What an inspiring story of evolution. Like, you can go from being funny guy on Minecraft streams to, "Okay, this is my life now. I'm building AI clusters and doing the Council of AIs," and we all get to spectate? I mean, what a treasure.

Host

所以我想，我的意思是，他很好地体现了软件的未来，或者说构建的未来是什么样子，对吧？因为他严格来说不是程序员……却变成了程序员。

So I guess, I mean, he's a pretty good embodiment of what the future of software, or what the future of building looks like, right? 'Cause he's a non-programmer technically... becoming a programmer.

DHH

极具启发性，而且应该对其他正处于那种状态的人极具启发性，他们会想：“嗯，我不太会编程。我不懂这个那个。”好吧，但如果 PewDiePie 能用他的定制硬件在这里搭建 AI 委员会，那么也许你也可以开始做一些事情。我认为这实际上是一个重要的普遍观点，我们需要榜样。我们需要有人去激励他人，推动他们走得更远，并揭示那些感知到的边界并不像你想象的那么固定。而且那些人中的每一个，他们的起点都和你相差无几。再说一次，这并不意味着每个人都能成为 PewDiePie 或埃隆或林纳斯。天赋甚至智力并不是平均分配的。我们可以接受这一点，同时仍然被这些人激励。比如在体育领域，我觉得我们通常不会有这个问题。比如，某人足球踢得很好，篮球打得很好，某件事做得很好，我们会说：“天哪，太棒了。我希望我也能，但我永远做不到。”有时候我觉得在智力领域，我们会更挣扎一些。

Hugely inspiring, and should be hugely inspiring to others who sit in that moment like, "Well, I don't know quite how to program. I don't know this and the other thing." Okay, but if PewDiePie can build the Council of AIs with his bespoke, um, hardware here, then maybe you can also start on things. And I think this is actually an important general point, that we need role models. We need people to inspire others, to push further, and reveal that the perceived boundaries, they're not as fixed as you think they are. And every one of those people, they started out in a situation not too dissimilar from yours. Again, that doesn't mean everyone is gonna be PewDiePie or Elon or Linus. There is not an equal distribution of talent or even intelligence. And we can come to terms with that and still be inspired by these people. Like, in the physical realm, I feel like we don't have any problems with that usually. Like, oh, someone's really good at football, they're really good at basketball, they're really good at something, and we go like, "Oh my, that's amazing. I mean, I wish I was, but I'm never going to be." Sometimes I feel like we struggle a little more when it comes to the intellectual realm.

Host

嗯，对很多人来说，你是一种激励。你做 Omakub 的方式……它恰恰表明你可以梦想远大并真正去构建。这很棒。

Well, for a lot of people, you're an inspiration. The way you're doing with Omakub's... It just shows that you can dream big and really build. It's great.

DHH

我确实喜欢这部分，然后我也接受另一部分，就是还有一百万人认为我是地球上最大的白痴，我其实什么都不懂，Omakub 只是一堆点文件，而且……

I do like that part of it, and then I accept the part that there's also a million people who think I'm the biggest idiot on the earth and I don't actually know anything, and Omakub's just a bunch of dot files, and...

Host

你在 Omakub 方面有一些批评者吗？

Do you have some critics on the Omakub side?

DHH

天哪，是的。

Oh my God, yes.

Host

哦，哇。

Oh, wow.

DHH

而且其中很多，我的意思是，就像通常发生的那样，当有人从外部进来时。我是说，我使用 Linux 才两年半。那不算长。那个社区里有很多人真的是从 Linux 还很艰难的时候就一直在用，那时候你得光着脚在雪地里顶着风上坡。我认为有某种书呆子……当社区扩大，突然允许更多人进来时，他们会感到威胁。你知道吗？我实际上……我不能完全责怪他们。我认为某些书呆子觉得“那是我的地盘”是公平的。比如，现在你把它变成了另一个地盘。我不想那样做。我不想改变 Arch 的地盘。我不认为 Omarchy 是同一个社区的直接一部分。因为最初被 Arch 吸引的人是那些喜欢自己手工构建一切的人。用困难的方式做事，因为他们喜欢困难，而我正在构建完全相反的东西。所以我只是希望那些人能说：“好吧，这不适合我。我想用困难的方式构建。我想投入 500 小时来定制我自己的 Arch 发行版，”我认为你应该这样做。我的意思是，实际上，我说过……我是说，我做到了。这就是 Omarchy，我实际上投入了，到现在，我不知道，3000 个小时到这个发行版里。如果你愿意，你应该这样做。我认为这很棒。但也要意识到，我们可以共享相同的技术基础，然后运营两个非常独立的……社区，它们有非常不同的目的、目标、美学甚至道德，然后共存，这可以很美。我的意思是，我并不总能得到这样的回应。有时我得到的是另一种。但在这一点上，我在这行已经足够久了，不仅与之和平共处，而且实际上欢迎它。我把它当作事情正在运转的晴雨表的一部分。

And a lot of it, I mean, is as often happens when someone comes in from the outside. I mean, I've been using Linux for two and a half years. That's not very long. Plenty of people in that community who's literally been running Linux since it was hard and difficult, and you had to walk uphill both direction against the wind in the snow barefoot. And I think there is a certain kind of nerd... who's threatened when the community expands, and it suddenly allows more in. And do you know what? I actually... I can't begrudge that fully. I think it's fair for certain nerds to feel like, "That's my space." Like, now you're changing it into another space. I don't wanna do that. Like, I don't wanna change the Arch space at all. Like, I don't really think of Omarchy as being directly part of the same community. Because the people who were attracted to Arch in the first place were the ones who were attracted to building everything by hand themselves. Doing it the hard way because they like to do it hard, and I'm building the polar opposite of that. So I just wish that those people could go like, "Okay, well, this is not for me. I wanna build it the hard way. I wanna put in 500 hours to rising my own Arch distribution," and I think you should. I mean, actually, I said... I mean, I did it. This is what Omarchy is, me pouring in literally, at this point, I don't know, 3,000 hours into the, into this distro. You should do that if you're so inclined. I think it's wonderful. But also do realize that we can share the same technical underpinnings and then operate two very separate... Communities with very different aims, goals, aesthetics, morals even, and coexist, and it can be beautiful. And I mean, that's not always what I get. Sometimes I get the other thing. But at this point I've been in the game long enough to not just be at peace with it, but actually be welcoming of it. I take it as part of a barometer that things are working.

平衡批评与成功 Balancing criticism and success

When I'm working on something that works, I usually get some people who like it, and then I get this balance of the universe that we talked about last time, where an equal but opposing force must be present on the other side of the scale to hate upon it. DHH

这就是长期以来对 Linux 本身的指控。你装了 Linux，然后这个不工作，我的扬声器不工作，我的触控板不工作。是啊，好吧，但让 Linus 搞个 30 年。他会搞定的，他会把所有的都搞定。而到了现在，我觉得好笑的是，在大多数电脑上——我可能会这么说——它已经搞定了。你装 Linux，一切都能用。你装 Windows，祝你好运，去到处找驱动让那块硬件工作吧。因为它们的哲学非常不同。Linux 有 4000 万行代码的原因，就是 Linus 把所有驱动都放进内核里。所以它开箱即用，而 Windows 不是这样。这是不同的做法，对吧？

This was the charge against Linux itself for the longest time. Well, you install Linux and then this doesn't work, and my speakers don't work, and my trackpad doesn't work. Yeah, okay, but just leave Linus at it for like 30 years. He'll get to it. He'll get to all of it. And at this point I find it hilarious that on, I would probably argue, the majority of computers, it's wiped. You install Linux, all of it works. You install Windows, good luck hunting down the drivers you need to get that piece of hardware working. Because they have very different philosophies. The reason Linux is 40 million lines of code is Linus just puts all the drivers in the kernel. So it has everything out of the box, and Windows doesn't. A different approach to it, right?

DHH

所以有时候那些看起来像玩具、乍一看像是坏掉的东西，它们确实如此，对吧？它们是给喜欢玩的人准备的。我们称那些人为早期采用者。他们玩这些玩具，然后玩具进化，变得更好，变得更好。到了某个时候，它们变得太好了，以至于早期采用者觉得他们被邀请参加派对。如果早期采用者也持续打磨它，最终你会到达晚期多数，然后你就赢了。而 Omarchy 正在通往那个目的地的路上。这个发行版的增长，不只是从 Quattro 开始，而是从第三版开始，就像标准的曲棍球杆曲线。你看着它，什么都没有，什么都没有，什么都没有。你只是在辛苦耕耘，不断改进，改进，改进。然后你到达那个神奇的拐点，而那个时刻是无法预测的。创业公司也是一样。它什么时候会发生？你什么时候会找到产品市场契合？然后突然它就出现了，然后它就起飞了，你就坐上火箭了。

So sometimes the things that look like a toy, things that look broken at first glance, they are, right? They're for people who like having fun. We call those early adopters. They play with the toys, and then the toys evolve, and then they get better, and then they get better. And at some point, they're so damn good that early adopters feel like they're invited to the party. And if the early adopters keep chipping away at it too, eventually you get to the late majority, and then you win. And we are well on the way with Omarchy towards that destination. Like the growth that the distro has seen, not just since Quattro, but since version three, it's just like, you know, the standard hockey stick curve. You go like, there's nothing, there's nothing, there's nothing. You're just toiling away. You're just improving, improving, improving. And then you hit this magic inflection point, and it's impossible to predict when that's gonna happen. Same thing with startups. When is it gonna happen? When are you gonna find that product-market fit? Then suddenly it's there, and then it goes, just goes, and off the rocket ship you go.

拥抱非决定论与创造力 Embracing Non-Determinism and Creativity

DHH

我对此完全坦然。只要我能拥有一台像 Omarchy 一样用起来那么有趣的电脑，那就太好了。我对 Ruby 和 Rails 也有同样的感受。很早以前我就想：你知道吗？我爱 Ruby。它是一门很棒的程序设计语言。但如果出现了一门更好的语言，我，我自己，就会去用它。如果出现了一个更好的 Rails，我也会去用它。而且，我爱 Ruby，我会继续写 Ruby，哪怕只是为了纯粹的乐趣，就像我会去骑马一样。我的马厩里有马，尽管我的车库里停着一辆 Model Y。

I'm completely at peace with that. As long as I get to have a computer that is as fun to work with as Omarchy, that's great. I've had the same thing with Ruby and with Rails. From very early I went, do you know what? I love Ruby. It's such a great programming language. But if a better programming language comes along, I, me, I'm going to use it. If a better Rails comes along, I'm gonna use it. And I think, and I love Ruby, and I will continue to write Ruby even just for the sheer fun of it, like I would go ride a horse. I have in the stables, even though I have a Model Y in the garage.

Host

现在的语言是英语。这是老生常谈，但也是事实。比如，过去三个月我一直在用英语编程。我读了很多代码，但我是在用英语编程。我在告诉电脑该做什么，我在使用自然语言，而这令人震惊地更加愉快。如果有一种比 Ruby 更美的程序设计语言，那就是英语。

The language now is English. It's a cliche, but it's also true. Like I've been programming in English for the last three months. I've been reading a lot of code, but I'm programming in English. I'm telling the computer what to do, and I'm using natural language, and it is shockingly even more delightful. If there is one programming language more beautiful than Ruby, it is the English language.

DHH

而现在，我们得以把它带入人类文明的自然演进中，也就是我们的赛博格未来。

And now we get to carry it into the natural evolution of human civilization, which is our cyborg future.

Host

美。这很美。我的意思是，我之所以这么说，部分是因为我一直热爱写作。我一直就热爱英语这门语言，因为它纯粹的美、纯粹的精细和深度。我的意思是，Ruby 是一门非常富有表现力的程序设计语言，但它无法与英语相提并论。我是说，世界上所有的诗歌和文学，都是通过英语表达的。我喜欢优美的代码诗，但真正的英语作品，其水平是另一回事。

Beautiful. It's beautiful. And I mean, the reason I say this in part is I always loved writing. I always just loved the English language for the sheer beauty of it, for the sheer intricacy, for the depth of it. I mean, Ruby is a very expressive programming language, but it can't hold a candle to English. I mean, all the poetry and literature in the world that's been expressed through the English language. I mean, I like a beautiful code poem, but I mean, the real deal in English is just on a different level.

DHH

而且我确实发现，这是一个很难表达的精妙观点，我可能会表达失败。但当你给系统提示时，如果你过于具体，它会过于认真地听从你的话并遵循指令。但是，尽管如此，你必须表达一个概念。你猜怎么着？人类语言，这正是诗歌的意义所在。我发现策略性地使用模糊性。比如，如果你写一首情诗说“我爱你”，这是非常老套的，它不如那些更多使用隐喻等的表达有力。我发现，当我给系统提示、描述设计时，某种类似的方式实际上非常有效，因为你仍然想传达某种风格感，鼓励系统去使用，但又不过度解释。所以，不要像对机器人那样跟它说话。要像写诗那样跟它说话。

And I actually find this is a nuanced point to express, and I'll probably fail expressing it. But when you prompt a system and you're over-specific, it will listen to you too carefully and follow the instructions. And I-- But nevertheless, you have to express a concept. And guess what? The human language, this is what poetry is about. I find strategic use of ambiguity. Like, if you write a love poem saying, "I love you," like, very cliché thing, it's not as powerful as something more in metaphor and so on. And I find some version of that when I'm prompting a system, when I'm describing a design, is actually really effective because you still want to convey some sense of style that you're trying to encourage a system to use, but not overexplain. So don't talk to it like a robot. Talk to it like you would write a poem.

Host

就像你在唱小夜曲。

Like you were serenading.

DHH

是的。而这正是自然语言的力量所在，你可以通过模糊性，在不过度指定的情况下，仍然承载大量意义。而另一端的智能实体，通过解读，可以将其全部加载、整合，从而让你能够传达那些不直接存在于词语中、但又因系统更深层的智能而蕴含在词语中的高带宽信息。所以，我想，模糊性会引出更多的智能，我试图以某种方式表达这一点。

Yeah. And that's where, like, the power of natural language is, that you can, through ambiguity, still carry a lot of meaning without overspecifying. And the intelligent entity on the other side, through interpretation, can, like, load it all in, integrate it in a way that you can actually convey the high bandwidth information that's not directly in the words but in the words, given the deeper intelligence of the system. So like the ambiguity pulls out more intelligence, I guess, I'm trying to somehow express.

Host

我认为你说得非常准确，而这也是很多程序员对 AI 的根本误解，他们希望它是确定性的。不，不，不。温度（temperature）是 AI 设置中最美的部分。它不是确定性的，创造力需要道路上的小小转折，如果人脑是完美确定性的，它就不会是现在这样富有创造力的大脑。而对 AI 的主要指控，两者都是——这很有趣。这是一个矛盾。既有指控说它非确定性所以不好，也有指控说它没有创造力。两者必居其一，兄弟。要么它是非确定性的，因此有创造力，因此在某种程度上是随机的；要么它……要么它没有创造力。所以，我们，我们可以——这两个指控不可能同时成立，而我已经完全接受了这样一个事实：同样的提示不会每次都产生同样的回应。人不能两次踏进同一条河流，这是整个互动中最美的部分。这正是它如此人性化的原因。

I think that's exactly spot on, and this is the fundamental misunderstanding that a lot of programmers have of AI, is that they wish it was deterministic. No, no, no. Temperature is the most beautiful part of the AI setup. The fact that it is not deterministic, the fact that creativity requires little tweaks in the road, that the human brain, if it was perfectly deterministic, would not be the creative brain that it is. And the main charge against AI both-- It's funny. It's a contradiction. There's both the charge that it's not deterministic and therefore bad, and also that it is not creative. It's one or the other, bro. Either it's non-deterministic and therefore creative, therefore, to some degree random, or it's... or it's not creative. So we, we can-- Both of those charges can't be true at the same time, and I have fully come to embrace the fact that the same prompt won't produce the same response every time. You can't step at the same river twice, and it is the most beautiful part of the whole interaction. It's what makes it so human.

DHH

事实上，这是我在自己的某种元分析中一直在思考的事情之一，就是我自己的大脑在多大程度上像下一个词预测（next-token prediction）那样运作。我坐下来写一篇文章。我有一个模糊、朦胧的意图想要传达，我坐在键盘前，而我无法提前告诉你下一个词会是什么。词就这样冒出来，而我对相似性如此之大感到惊讶。这也是为什么我毫不费力就能认出那些我亲眼所见、AI 现在就能实现的创造力突破。因为我的创造性时刻，也是通过同样的下一个词预测，再加上一点温度（temperature）来产生随机效果。

In fact, this is one of the things I've been thinking about in my own sort of meta-analysis, is just how much of my own personal brain works like next-token prediction. I sit down to write an essay. I have this vague, fuzzy premise I want to convey, and I sit down at the keys, and I could not tell you what the next token was gonna be in advance. The tokens just come out, and I'm astonished how the similarities seem so great, and this is also why I don't have any trouble at all recognizing these breakthroughs of creativity that I've seen with my own eyes that AI is capable of right now. 'Cause my creative moments come through the same kind of next-token prediction with a bit of temperature sprinkled in for random effect.

通往超级智能与意识之路 Path to Superintelligence and Consciousness

Host

那么，基于这种松散的直觉，你认为创造超级智能系统的基本组件是否都已具备？那么，这种下一个词预测，你认为我们能否达到甚至类似人类意识的层面？

So given that kinda loose intuition, do you think the basic components are all there to create a super intelligent system? So this kinda next-token prediction, do you think we can get to even human-like concepts of consciousness?

DHH

我已经看到了类似人类意识的概念。正如你所说，这对我来说，就是这种非凡的情形。你给它一个模糊、朦胧的意图，而它不知怎地就确切地知道你的意思。或者更好的是，它改进你所说的话，并交付你真正想要却无法自己表达的东西。如果那不是意识的微光，那又是什么呢？这是其中之一——我想这是对 Sutton 或也许是其他创始人的一次采访，谈到这种感觉，即智能或许并不局限于某个特定的点。不，它是交互。它是权重。而我不知道。无论真相是什么。我能观察到的是，它感觉足够接近，甚至不只是足够接近，而是与我对他者意识（主要是人类意识）的欣赏完全相同。这也是为什么与它合作如此令人愉快的原因之一。不过，这并不是说 LLM 就是 AI 的终点站。我的意思是，我知道有人在谈论世界模型（world models）和其他形式的 AI，而我对此都不是专家，而且我认为我们应该有这种建设性的批评，正如科学中始终应该存在的那样。在很长一段时间里，神经网络有点被冷落，对吧？比如，他们感兴趣的是符号路线。而神经网络在没有资金、没有关注的情况下，在黑暗中走了几十年，然后突然我们意识到我们走进了死胡同，于是我们转向了。所以我们也应该保持谦逊。尽管现在的 LLM 令人惊叹，但它们最终可能会达到平台期。我们还没有看到任何证据，而我认为这也是我们看到这种绝对惊人的投资水平的原因，因为到目前为止，缩放定律（scaling laws）是成立的，投入的数十亿越多，涌现的智能就越多。

I'm already seeing human-like concepts of consciousness. This is what, to me, this remarkable situation, as you say. You give it this vague, fuzzy intent and somehow it knows exactly what you mean. Or even better, it improves upon what you said and delivers what you really wanted that you could not articulate yourself. If that's not glimmers of consciousness, what is? This was one of the-- I think it was an interview with Sutton or maybe one of the other original guys talking about this sense that intelligence is perhaps not as constrained just to the specific one spot. No, it's the interaction. It's the weights. And I don't know. Whatever's true. What I can observe is that it feels close enough, or not even close enough, identical to the appreciation I have for other forms of consciousness, mostly human. And this is one of the reasons it's so delightful to work with. Now, that's not the same to say as are LLMs the end station for AI. I mean, I know there are people talking about world models and other forms of AI, and I'm not an expert in any of that, and I think we should have this productive criticism, as should always be present in science, that for the longest time, neural nets were kind of on the outs, right? Like they were interested in the symbolic route. And neural nets walked a couple of decades into darkness without any funding and without any attention, and then suddenly we realized that we had the blind alley and we switched over. So we should also have the humility. As amazing as the LLMs are now, it could be that they eventually plateau. We haven't seen any evidence of it yet, and I think this is also why we're seeing this absolute gobsmacking levels of investment, because so far the scaling laws are true, and the more billions are poured in, the more intelligence comes out.

AI意识的伦理问题 Ethical Questions about AI Consciousness

Host

你觉得会不会出现一些奇怪的伦理问题，关于 AI 系统，它们确实传达出一定程度的意识、一定程度的感觉、一定程度的渴望和同情，甚至可能还有受苦和孤独的能力，诸如此类？它们似乎有这个能力——如果被允许表达的话。于是你就进入了一个相当诡异的领域。

Do you think there's gonna be some strange ethical questions about AI systems that yeah, they convey some degree of consciousness, some degree of feeling, some degree of longing and compassion, and maybe even capacity to suffer and to be lonely, to be all this kind of stuff? Which they seem to have that capacity- ... if they're given the permission to express it. And so you start to get in a pretty weird territory.

DHH

我觉得对这种问题的犬儒式看法就是那个梗：你看到电脑前的那个人，一脸“随便吧，你要毁灭世界吗？”然后电脑说“我要毁灭世界”，然后那人说“哦”，对吧？这种看法太犬儒了，觉得它们只是在模仿我们，只是把训练数据里的东西说出来而已。但当我跟这些智能体互动时——我昨天或今天早上还在做这个，我在弄 AmaBot 系统，它要协调多个 worker，我同时跑了好几个智能体。有一次，负责协调的主智能体踩了另一个智能体的脚，搞砸了它的工作。它表达遗憾和歉意的那种能力，简直不可思议。再说一次，我不知道，这是不是真实悔恨的投射？但我不确定。大多数人类……也会表达某种程度的遗憾。那是真正的悔恨吗？很难说，但也许这根本不重要。

I find that the cynic approach to this is the meme, where you see the guy in front of the computer who's like, um, "Whatever. Are you gonna destroy the world?" DHH

OpenClaw 刚出来的时候我就搭好了，那就是我的 KEF 机器人——我——大概是二月份吧。我当时真的很着迷于它居然是这样工作的，而且这种给智能体发短信的新通信模式看起来很棒。所以当时我们在看 MCP，这个协议是为了让智能体更容易与你的系统对话，但我觉得这个协议繁琐得离谱，设计得不太优雅，部分原因是它不是为我们想让它做的事而设计的。我们试图——我是想让它跟一个 Web 系统对话，而它是为本地机器上其他类型的有状态交互设计的，所以需要各种繁琐的设置和步骤，我当时就想，“我可不想手写这个。”当然，现在我不会手写了，但当时我在想，“我得手写这个。我不想手写。那有没有别的办法？能不能让智能体直接使用我们已有的 Web 界面？”然后我就让它去试，让它注册 Fizzy，那是我们当时刚发布的产品。它去了 Fizzy 网站，开始填表，然后它说，“嘿，我没有邮箱地址。注册这个服务需要邮箱地址。”我第一反应是，“哦，天哪，现在我得去弄个邮箱。”然后我想，“不，你去弄个邮箱。去 hey.com，注册个邮箱。”我当时想，“Hey 不会搞定的。”当然，现在看来它显然能搞定，但在当时，这对我来说是个巨大的启示：它仅仅通过提示词的直接指引，就能弄清楚，第一，我得去 fizzy.com，找到注册链接。我开始填表。哦，我意识到我需要邮箱。哦，我得问我的提示者现在该怎么办。他告诉我去 hey.com。我去那里，注册了整个流程，然后回来注册 Fizzy，完成整个循环。它做完之后，我心想，“天哪。”然后我说，“好吧，现在你有邮箱了。去注册 Basecamp。我会邀请你。你只要查收邮件就行，”我告诉 KEF 机器人。“查收邮件就行。”于是我从 Basecamp 给智能体发了一个邀请。智能体在 Hey 里收到了邮件，不是通过 CLI，不是通过 MCP，就是直接用 Web，对吧？点开邮件，点开邀请链接，就进了 Basecamp。它问，“我现在该做什么？”我就说，“我不知道。去我们的 AI 房间，自我介绍一下。”还是纯粹通过 Web 界面。它找到了 AI 房间，进去说，“嘿，我是 KEF。”“我是 David 的 AI 机器人。我很高兴来到这里。”我当时——震惊了。这个实验有趣的地方在于，第一，它让我瞥见了未来，因为当时有点慢。我觉得整个过程端到端大概花了 12 分钟，对吧？现在我真该重新跑一下这个测试。如果它没有快很多，我会很惊讶。

I set up OpenClaw when it first came out, and that was my KEF bot- ... that I- ... this was like in February, I think. I was really just enamored by the fact that this was how it worked, and then the new communication model of just texting your agent seemed really great. So at the time, we were looking at MCP, this protocol for making it easier for agents to talk to your system, and I found the protocol to be unreasonably cumbersome, just not very elegantly designed because in part it wasn't designed for what we were trying to make it do. We were trying to-- I was trying to make it talk to a web system, and it was designed for stateful interactions of other kinds on local machines, and therefore it required all sorts of cumbersome setup and dance and, and I was just like, "I don't wanna write this by hand." Now, of course, today I would never write it by hand, but at the time I was thinking, "I have to write this by hand. I don't wanna write it by hand. So what's a different way? Could the agents just use the web interfaces we already have?" And I set it off on a trial to have it sign up for Fizzy, which was the product we had just released at the time. And it goes to the Fizzy site and, and starts filling it out, and then it goes like, "Hey, I don't have an email address. Signing up for this service requires an email address." And the first thing I thought, like, "Oh man, now I gotta set up an email address." And then I thought, "No, you set up an email address. Go to hey.com, sign up for an email address." And I'm like, "Hey is not gonna get this." And of course, now it seems obvious it was gonna get this, but at the time it was a great revelation to me that it could, just on pure direction through prompts, figure out, A, I gotta go to fizzy.com, I gotta find the signup link. I start filling out the form. Oh, I realize I need an email address. Oh, I gotta ask my prompter what to do now. He tells me to go to hey.com. I go there, I sign up for the whole thing, and then go back and sign up for Fizzy, and complete the entire cycle. And after it did that, I was like, "Jesus." So I was like, "Well, now you have an email address. Go sign up for Basecamp. I'll invite you. Just check your email," I told KEF the bot. "Just check your email." So I sent an invite from Basecamp to the agent. The agent receives the email in Hey, not through a CLI, not through an MCP, just using the web, right? Clicks the email, clicks the invite link, is in Basecamp. Like, "What should I do now?" And I just go like, "I don't know. Go to our AI room and introduce yourself." Again, purely through the web interface. Go- finds the AI room, goes in there, "Hey, I'm KEF." "I'm, uh, David's AI bot. I'm so excited to be here." I was like- ... mind blown. Now, what was interesting about that experiment was, A, it was that glimpse of the future, because it was kinda slow. I think it took maybe 12 minutes to do the whole thing end to end, right? Now I should actually rerun that test. I'd be shocked if it's not much faster.

代理交互与未来一瞥 Agent Interaction and Future Glimpses

DHH

我刚才就在用 Groq 机器人那玩意儿，它内置了各种浏览器和电脑操作的功能，还有一台专用的电脑，你都不用自己配电脑。基本上就像个盒子里的爪子。我让它帮我注册个东西，然后我就瞥见了——哦，对，我们现在就到这一步了，而且用起来快多了。但当时我真是被震撼到了。后来我用了一会儿，发现，唉，还没到那一步。我没法用这种方式跟它交流。它还是需要命令行，还是需要 MCP，因为它太慢了，token 效率也太低，诸如此类。但那确实是对未来的一瞥。之后我就不怎么用了，因为我想用智能体做的事，大多时候我都在电脑前自己做了。不过最近这情况有点变了，这也是我为什么对用 Claude Code 这么无所谓的原因之一，因为 Claude 有最好的移动应用，你在电脑终端里启动的任何 Claude Code 会话，都能通过这个应用访问。

而且，因为那感觉就像未来，不一定是以这种方式，但在某些方面正在朝那个方向走。

And like, just to 'cause it, it feels like the future, not necessarily in this way, but it's in some ways going there.

DHH

是的。我觉得那些系统还有一个不同之处，就是它们有整套记忆系统，会积累关于你是谁、你喜欢什么的知识。嗯，Toby 讲过他的智能体怎么替他购物，它有零花钱，会给他挑衣服，然后东西就直接送到门口，因为他的智能体……给他买了东西。就像那种，哇，这感觉像未来。也有点吓人。但我可以通过那些走在前沿的人来间接体验。

Yes. I think what's also different with those systems is they have this whole memory system, where they're building up knowledge of who you are and what you like. Um, Toby has told the stories about how his agent will shop for him, that it has an allowance, and it'll pick out clothes for him, and it'll just show up at the door because his agent's... bought something for him. And like, it's one of those things where like, man, that feels future. Also a little scary. But I can live vicariously through others who are on that frontier.

Host

就像你说的，你正在考虑未来做一个 Mach-E 的移动版。

And like you said, you're thinking of the, in the future doing a mobile version of Mach-E.

DHH

所以我的意思是，一旦你获得了看似无限的力量，妄想就开始作祟了……我在创建 Quattro 的过程中就见证了这一点。

So I mean, this is the delusions of grandeur that start kicking in once you get the seemingly unlimited powers... I've witnessed with the creation of Quattro.

Host

那会是什么样子？用什么词来形容？你是指像 Android 那样吗？

So what would that look like? Word to describe. Are you do you mean like Android?

那是显而易见的路径，对吧？Android 是开源的，你可以分叉它，人们也这么做了。有各种版本，GrapheneOS 就是其中之一。我知道那种模式有局限性。比如点击支付、相机模型之类的事情，但那些似乎都是可以克服的。一切似乎都表明，我希望我的手机能像我的电脑一样可塑，看起来像我现在的电脑一样酷。如果我能得到本质上的 Omarchy 移动版，那听起来太棒了。

That's the obvious path, right? So Android is open source, and you can fork it, and people have. There's all sorts of GrapheneOS is one such version. There are limitations to that model, I know that. Tap-to-pay and other things around camera models and, and so forth, but that all seems surmountable. It all seems that I would love if my phone was as malleable as my computer, if it looked as cool as my computer now does. If I could get essentially Omarchy mobile, that sounds amazing.

所以，我就在想，对于操作系统……

And so, like, I wonder with the operating system...

Yeah, the developers of Ladybird is, is finding that out, like Andreas and...

Host

DHH

……他的团队。有趣的是，那个项目里，浏览器基本上是世界上第二复杂的软件系统，第一是 Linux 内核。对吧？就这些数千万行代码的系统而言。所以，在智能体出现之前，像 Andreas 那样用一个小团队开始这样的任务，其实非常大胆。但现在我们有了智能体，我们将会看到，而且可能很快就能看到。我的意思是，如果说有一件事智能体已经特别擅长了，那就是阅读规格并实现它们。我的意思是，我不知道现在 CSS 和 HTML 的规格有多长，再加上 JavaScript，但它们非常长。人类需要很多很多年才能实现它们。我们将会发现智能体做这些能有多快。

...his team. Now, interesting with that project was the browser's basically the second most complicated software system in the world, the first one being the Linux kernel. Right? In terms of these large tens of millions of lines of code systems. So very audacious actually to begin such a mission with a small team like Andreas did before we had agents. But now that we do have agents, we're gonna see, and we're probably gonna see sooner rather than later. I mean, if one-- If there's one thing agents are already exceptionally good at, it is to read specs and implement them. And I mean, I don't know how long the specs are for CSS and HTML at this point, and JavaScript on top, but they're very long. And it would take humans many, many years to implement them. We're gonna find out just how quickly agents can do them.

Host

你提到 Omarchy 受到了一些批评，那我们就沿着批评这条路再走远一点。你已经公开了一些政治观点，嗯，我想你可以大致把它们归入移民或非法移民，或者什么——

You mentioned getting some criticism for Omarchy, so let's walk further down that path of criticism. You have made some of your political opinions known, um, I guess you could broadly put in the category of immigration or illegal immigration, or what-

DHH

大规模移民，人口统计在文化、社会、民族形成中的作用，你为此受到了很多批评，也制造了很多风波。你后悔那些风波吗？比如，你总体上怎么看待——

Mass immigration, the role of demographics in the, in the formation of a culture, of a society, of a nation, and you've gotten quite a lot of criticism for that and created a lot of drama. Do you regret any of that drama? Like, what's your general thinking about-

Host
