DHH

我这么说的原因是，奥弗顿之窗不会自己打开。它是一点一点被那些冒一点风险的人推开的。一点名声，一点反对，一点批评，或者有时是很多名声、很多批评、很多反对。我认为欧洲的大规模移民问题多年来一直是个完全的禁忌，而且在几个欧洲国家仍然如此。现在，也许只是出于偶然和巧合，在丹麦不是这样。

And the reason I say that is that the Overton window does not open itself. It opens one nudge at a time by people risking a little. A little reputation, a little pushback, a little criticism, or maybe sometimes a lot of reputation or a lot of criticism or a lot of pushback. And I think the question of mass immigration in Europe was for many years this total taboo, and it still is in several European countries. Now, for reasons of maybe just chance and happenstance, they weren't in Denmark.

丹麦早期移民辩论 Denmark's Early Immigration Debate

DHH

丹麦人早在 90 年代中期就开始讨论大规模移民及其后果了，非常早。其中一部分可以追溯到一个人，摩根斯·格利斯特鲁普，他是个非常古怪的人物。我记得他，80 年代在电视上看到他谈论大规模移民的危险。那是在——我 80 年代长大的，在我长大的那个社区，布伦斯霍伊，那是哪一年？84 年，99% 是丹麦裔，那些人的血统可以追溯到在这片土地上生活了 1000 年的丹麦人。丹麦拥有世界上最古老的君主制之一，一直追溯到哈拉尔德·蓝牙，蓝牙标准就是以他命名的。所以有超过 1000 年的历史。那里有很长的一段血统，人们住在同一个地方。而那个社区 99% 的人都是这样。然后到 90 年代初，我想这个比例降到了 5% 左右，而到现在，布伦斯霍伊已经降到大约 60% 多或 70% 左右了。这是一个巨大的变化。丹麦人大概在 90 年代开始注意到或谈论这件事，意识到这并非纯粹的福祉，大规模移民也有弊端。这场辩论在 2000 年代真正激烈起来，而邻国并没有发生同样的情况。尤其是瑞典，根本没有这场辩论，完全是禁忌。挪威也一样，欧洲其他国家也一样。而突然之间，在 2015 年之后几年，我们看到了 100 万叙利亚人沿着欧洲的高速公路徒步迁徙，德国选择接收其中很多人，丹麦也来了 3 万人等等，这件事得到了更多关注，对吧？因为这种变化已经发生了。

The Danes started having the discussion around mass immigration and the consequences thereof in the mid-'90s. Really early. And some of it can be traced back to one man, Mogens Glistrup, who was a very quirky character. I remember him, seeing him on the TV in the '80s talking about the dangers of mass immigration. And this was at a time where-- I grew up in the '80s, and in the neighborhood I grew up in, in Brønshøj, in, what was it? '84, 99% ethnic Danes, people who could trace their lineage back to Danes who'd lived in that country for 1,000 years. The Danes have one of the oldest monarchies in the world, all the way back to Harald Bluetooth, who's literally who has named the standard Bluetooth. So well over 1,000 years. Like, there's a long lineage there of people who lived in the same place. And 99% of the people lived in that neighborhood. And then early '90s, I think it goes to 5% or something like that, and then at this point, it's down to around 60-something or 70%- ... in Brønshøj. That's a big change. And the Danes started noticing that or talking about that, that this was not an undivided blessing, that there were downsides to mass immigration in about the '90s. And the debate really roared through the 2000s in a way that didn't happen in the neighboring countries. So Sweden in particular just didn't have that debate at all. Total taboo. Same thing with Norway, same thing with other countries in Europe. And suddenly now, after some years after '15, where we had the big migration of a million Syrians walking up through the highways in Europe, and Germany choosing to take a lot of them and 30,000 arriving in Denmark and so forth, It's gotten more of a focus, right? Because this change has happened.

注意文化变迁 Noticing Cultural Change

DHH

我发现有一种方法可以察觉文化中哪里不对劲，那就是：什么话题是允许谈论的？实际上，应该说，什么是允许被注意到的？我在一篇文章里注意到，那是什么时候？我记得是一年半前，关于伦敦。我 90 年代末开始去伦敦，那时候大概是 59%、60% 是英国白人。而 20 年后，这个比例降到了 34% 左右。这是显而易见的。就像走在大街上，你会意识到，这和我 20 年前来的时候已经是一个不同的城市了。如果你再往前推 20 年，你会看到和丹麦类似的比例。如果再往前，这个国家 85% 到 90% 以上都是英国白人。所以一代人的时间，国家就变了。注意到这一点是可以的。你也可以想：“你知道吗？我不同意这样。”“我希望不是这样。”

And one of the ways I find that you can spot where sort of there's something that isn't right in the culture is, like, what are you allowed to talk about? What are you allowed to notice, actually, is how I should put it. And what I noticed in an essay, when was that? Only a year and a half ago as I remember London, was that I started coming to London in the late '90s or something like that. At the time, whatever, it's 59%, 60% ethnic, uh, Brits. And then 20 years later, we're down to 34% or something like that. Like, that's noticeable. Like, literally just walking on the street, you realize, well, this is a different city from what it was when I visited 20 years ago. And if you went 20 years before that, you get back to similar rates as as what the Danes have. This was like a country of ethnic Brits in the 85s, 90s percentiles- ... if you get further back. So that's a different country in a generation. It's okay to notice that. It's also okay to think, "Do you know what? I don't agree with that." "I wish it wasn't that way."

自决权 Right to Self-Determination

DHH

我的意思是，我上次去中国是 19 年，2019 年。我在上海。嗯，那里全是中国人。如果我今年 10 月回去，突然发现只有 30% 的中国人，70% 是其他人，我会觉得有点奇怪。我可能会说：“呃，发生了什么？”如果那时中国人说：“啊，我们不喜欢这样。”我也不会觉得被冒犯。所以，认为欧洲国家没有权利，第一，注意到正在发生的事情，第二，反对正在发生的事情，我觉得这是错误的，对吧？就像——我认为存在一种关于自决的道德论点，我们在世界其他地区一直在援引这个论点，比如，哦，生活在那片地区的人民，他们有权为自己的国家自决。我认为，比如说，丹麦人、瑞典人或挪威人说：“嗯，我们在这里作为王国已经有一千年了，有着某种特定的民族构成。”这是完全合理的。

I mean, if The last time I was in China was '19, 2019. I was in Shanghai. Um, it was all Chinese. If I, when I go back here this October, suddenly find that there's only 30% Chinese and then there's 70% other people, I'd be a little weird. I'd probably be like, "Uh, what happened?" And I would not take offense if the Chinese in that instant would go like, "Ah, we don't like that." So this idea that countries of Europe don't have a right to, A, notice what's happening and, B, oppose what's happening, I find just to be, uh, wrong, right? Like there's just-- I think there's a moral argument for self-determination, and we invoke that argument all the time in other regions around the world that like, oh, the people who live in that region, they have a right to self-determination, um for their country. And I think it's completely fair for, say, the Danes or the Swedes or the Norwegians to go like, "Well, we've been kingdoms here for a thousand years with a certain ethnic mix-up."

基于功绩的移民 Merit-Based Immigration

DHH

这是一个——我的意思是，我非常支持移民。我是移民到美国的。嗯，择优挑选的移民，即各国竞相吸引世界上最聪明、最有才华的人，这真的很好。

It's a-- I mean, I'm a big fan of immigration. I'm an immigrant to the US. Um, cherry-picked immigration where countries compete to attract the smartest, most talented people around the world is really good.

Host

基于才能的合法移民。

Legal merit-based immigration.

DHH

基于才能是关键，因为实际上欧洲大部分地区几乎没有非法移民。南欧有一些非法移民，但欧洲大部分地区并没有严重的非法移民问题。问题在于其他类型的大规模移民，最终导致人口结构与短短几十年前完全不同。我认为欧洲国家会从移民中受益。事实上，丹麦人有趣的一点是，他们对移民有非常详尽的统计。我不太确定为什么这么详细。也许可以追溯到摩根斯·格利斯特鲁普和他 90 年代的讨论，但他们确实有。从这些统计中可以清楚地看到，当丹麦有来自英国、法国、美国的人时，平均而言，这些移民对丹麦国家非常有益。他们贡献的远远超过他们向国家索取的。所以丹麦人应该接受那些贡献远大于索取的移民。

Merit-based is key here because there's actually virtually no illegal immigration in most of Europe. Southern Europe has some illegal immigration, but most of Europe does not have a big problem with illegal immigration. And the problem is with mass immigration of other kinds, and that you end up with demographics that are just totally different from what they were even a few short decades ago. And I think the countries of Europe would benefit from immigration. In fact, one of the interesting things about the Danes is they carry meticulous statistics on immigration. I'm not entirely sure why they're so detailed. Maybe it's all the way back to Mogens Glistrup and his discussion in the '90s, but they do. And what's clear from those statistics is that when the Danes have folks from the UK, France, the US show up, on average, those immigrants are really beneficial to the Danish state. They contribute far more than what they ask of the state. So the Danes should accept immigrants who do that, who contribute far more than they take.

移民群体成本效益 Cost-Benefit of Immigrant Groups

DHH

然后你再看看其他类型的移民群体。上周刚有一份统计，是一位丹麦政治家要求财政部用最新数据做的。积极的移民给丹麦国家带来多少成本？就像我提到的这个案例。我想，在那个名单上，法国、英国、美国，他们都差不多，每年给丹麦国家带来约 2.5 万美元的净收益。哇，这非常积极。这又是平均值，对吧？而在光谱的另一端，最昂贵的移民是索马里人。他们平均每年让丹麦国家花费 2.8 万美元。我认为丹麦人这样说很公平：“我们更希望得到更多的法国人、英国人和美国人，而不是那么多索马里人。”

Then you look at other forms of immigrant groups. There was just a tally that came out last week from a Danish politician that had asked the, I think finance ministry to do this tally with the latest numbers. What does it cost the Danish state to have positive immigration? This case as I mentioned. I think for the, On that list whatever, France, UK, US, they were all clustered around the same thing, about $25,000 net benefit to the Danish state every year. It's like, wow, that's very positive. That's average again, right? And then at the other end of the spectrum on the most costly immigrants was Somalis. They ended up costing the Danish state on average $28,000 a year. I think it's fair for the Danes to go like, "We'd prefer to get more French, Brits, and Americans and not so many Somalis."

Host

你觉得为什么那个帖子让你招来这么多仇恨？

Why do you think you got so much hate on that post?

DHH

在很多圈子里，这是一个非常禁忌的话题。

That's a very taboo topic in a lot of circles.

Host

部分原因与奥弗顿之窗的扩展有关？

Some of it has to do with Overton window expansion?

DHH

我确定，但我也认为——我的意思是，我想在这里采用最有力的论点。我确实认为，这可能会变得丑陋，对吧？这些事情有没有可能直接转向明显的种族主义，而这种种族主义除了对他人的仇恨之外没有任何基础？是的，这可能会发生。但这种风险并不能否定讨论国家构成和移民政策应该是什么样的必要性。而且我——我还觉得很有趣的是，尤其是在欧洲，这种关于欧洲人是否有权决定自己国家面貌的自决讨论，似乎只适用于那里。没有人试图告诉日本人：“嘿，你太日本了。”不管怎样，我想东京 98% 都是日本人，嗯——……

I'm sure, but I also think-- I mean, I wanna take the steel man argument here. I do think that it, this can turn ugly, right? Is it possible for some of these things to just veer into overt racism that isn't founded in anything else but a hatred of other people? Yeah, that could happen. But that risk does not negate the need to have a discussion about the makeup of your country and what your immigration policy should be like. And I-- what I also find so funny is, especially in Europe, it's so kind of myopic that this discussion about whether Europeans should have a right to self-determination about what their countries look like, that standard seems to only apply there. No one is trying to tell the Japanese, "Hey, you, you're too Japanese." Like whatever, it's 98%, I think, in Tokyo that's, um-- ...

移民与同化 Immigration and Assimilation

DHH

日本裔，对吧？比如，有没有人试图告诉日本人，如果东京的日本裔比例降到伦敦那种水平，比如 37%，东京会成为一个更好的国家？我觉得没有。所以，在我看来，有一种奇怪的近乎自我厌恶的情绪，Gad 称之为“自杀式共情”，这种情绪渗透在这些事情里，我至今还没完全想明白。比如，这到底从哪来的？为什么会这样？但不管怎样，我只是注意到，嘿，伦敦看起来不一样了。我更喜欢以前的伦敦，就像我会说我更喜欢我在哥本哈根长大的那个街区在 80 年代的样子。我完全理解你怎么能一下子跳五步，然后说：“好吧，那是种族主义。”而且我也不在乎了。这是一个公平的讨论。国家和人民有权制定移民政策，而这种认为我们不应该有任何边界，或者更糟的是，认为一切都是一张白纸，所有民族都一样，我们可以把人们从地球的一个地方带到另一个地方，然后一切都会顺顺利利地运转——这在经验上是不成立的。欧洲从 80 年代起就在做这个实验，结果并不好，现在欧洲有一股力量想要推翻这个实验，想要结束迄今为止的大规模移民。而我碰巧相信，大多数丹麦人和其他一些民族也相信：“你知道吗？是的，我们一直以来的做法行不通，我们必须做出一些改变。”而且我不认为这意味着零移民。再说一次，我自己就是移民。我认为美国总体来说从移民中获益巨大，但这就是那种障眼法。移民的效果很大程度上取决于移民的是谁。当一个国家接纳的移民是社会的净贡献者，并且在大多数情况下，他们同化了当地文化，那么这个国家就会受益。如果你来到丹麦，想成为一个丹麦人，你付出所有努力，为社会做贡献，我想大多数丹麦人会相当乐意以合理的数量接纳你。如果你来到丹麦，不想同化，对社会是净负担，生活在平行社会里，那么丹麦人说“呃，我们别这样了”也是公平的。

Ethnic Japanese, right? Like, is anyone trying to tell the Japanese that their Tokyo would be a much better country if it were down to London levels of ethnic Japanese, say 37%? I don't think so. So there's this weird almost self-loathing, in my opinion, I mean, Gad calls it suicidal empathy, that permeates this stuff to a degree that I still haven't fully unpacked. Like, where is this coming from? Why is it that way? But regardless, I just noticed, hey, London looks different. I preferred the old London, as I would say I preferred the neighborhood that I grew up in in Copenhagen, how it looked in the '80s. Like, I totally get how you can then jump five leaps and go like, 'Okay, well, that's racist.' And I also, I don't care anymore. Like, this is a fair discussion to have. Countries and peoples have a right to set an immigration policy, and this notion that we shouldn't have any borders or, even worse than that, it's all just a blank slate, that all peoples are just the same, and we can take people from one place of the earth and place them in another place on the earth and everything's just gonna work out hunky-dory. Empirically not true. Europe has been running that experiment since the '80s. It hasn't panned out well, and now there are forces in Europe who wanna undo that experiment, who want to end mass immigration as it's been run so far. And I happen to believe, and so do a majority of Danes and a bunch of other peoples, that, 'Do you know what? Yeah, it hasn't been working how we've been doing it. We gotta make some changes.' And I don't think that means, again, zero immigration. Again, I'm an immigrant. I think the US, generally speaking, has benefited tremendously from immigration, but that's the kind of sleight of hand. Immigration depends greatly on who immigrates. And a country benefits when the people who immigrate are net contributors to the society, and in most cases, that they assimilate with the local culture. If you come to Denmark and you wanna become a Dane, and you put in all the effort, and you contribute to society, I think most Danes would be quite happy to allow that in a reasonable number. If you come to Denmark and you don't wanna assimilate, and you are a net drain on society, and you live in a parallel society, it's also fair for Danes to say like, 'Uh, let's stop doing that.'

Host

有趣的是，我对美国的移民制度不太熟悉，但据我所知，基于 merit 的制度在这里并没有通过。

It's interesting that I'm not well-versed on the immigration system in the United States, but as far as I understand, merit-based systems have not passed here.

DHH

而且，美国文化非常不同。这一点是我在两个地方都长期生活过之后才充分体会到的。美国有一种“可选同化”的文化，对于从丹麦、乌克兰或其他地方来的人来说，更容易也更被宽容，他们来到美国，接受美国的价值观、规范和文化，然后就能被其他美国人视为美国人。这让我觉得好笑的是，当有人说“美国是个种族主义国家”时，我会想：“你去过别的地方吗？你知道其他国家是什么样吗？”如果你认为美国是种族主义最严重的国家，那你就是被误导了。我的意思是，我们在丹麦生活时就遇到了这个问题。我妻子是美国人，有斯堪的纳维亚血统，但已经在美国好几代了。非常难。非常难，作为一个金发蓝眼的女人，她付出了各种努力学语言、做所有事情，但融入丹麦文化仍然非常非常难。如果反过来，她是丹麦人搬到美国，就不会那么难。这是另一件事。我的意思是，在丹麦这样的国家生活，有一个有斯堪的纳维亚血统的美国妻子，她付出了所有努力，却仍然意识到，天哪，同化极其困难，即使你已经 97% 到位了。天哪，如果你来自一个完全陌生的文化和规范，那岂不是几乎不可能。当然，这正是我刚才引用的统计数据所证明的，文化差异越大、距离越远，成功的可能性就越低。

And then also, American culture is very different. This is a point I did not fully appreciate until living both places extensively. America has a culture of optional assimilation, where it is much easier and then much more condoned for someone coming from Denmark or Ukraine or wherever, come to America, buy into the American set of values and norms and culture, and then be able to be respected as Americans by other Americans. This is what's so funny to me when there's discussion like, 'America is such a racist country.' I'm like, 'Have you ever been anywhere? Do you know what other countries are like?' Like, if you think America is the most racist country, you are simply misinformed. I mean, we're dealing with that, with time we've spent in Denmark. My wife is American of Scandinavian heritage, but multiple generations in the US. Very difficult. Very difficult, as a blonde, blue-eyed woman who's put in all sorts of efforts of learning the language and doing everything, still very, very difficult to assimilate to Danish culture. In a way where if that had been the other way around and she had been Danish and moving to the US, it would not have been that difficult at all. And this was the other thing. I mean, living in a country like Denmark with an American wife who is of Scandinavian heritage and has put in all the effort, and still realizing, man, assimilation is incredibly difficult, even when you're 97% there all the way. Holy smokes, must it be almost impossible if you come from a completely foreign culture and foreign norms and the rest of it. And of course, that's what the stats I just quoted bear out, that the more dissimilar the culture is and the further it is, the less likely to be successful.

Host

表达这种立场是否让你失去了友谊或关系？

Has articulating that stance cost you friendships, relationships?

DHH

是的，但我想说，不仅仅是这个立场。更多的是 2020 年前后发生的巨大分歧，我认为很多政治断层线在那时真正裂开了。我的意思是，在美国，简而言之就是“你醒了吗？”显然，我有一群我以为关系很好多年的人，一旦政治断层线裂开，他们显然就不再是了。我觉得这很遗憾。而且我一直在努力理解这从何而来，部分原因是我自己多年来在几个话题上改变了看法，就像，有时候你只是随着时间改变主意。有时候你因为获得新信息而改变主意。你改变主意的原因和时机有很多种，我们不会都在同一时间改变，或者根本不改变。所以，让自己周围有，或者至少与那些在每个话题上都和你想法不同的人互动，我觉得这很健康。我的意思是，我偶尔和我哥哥讨论政治。我们在所有话题上并不完全一致，有时讨论会有点激烈，但之后也会冷静下来，我们意识到，好吧，我们在这个问题上不同意，但我们在很多其他事情上意见一致。我们欣赏很多相同的东西。实际上，当我们在 Basecamp 大吵一架时，我以一种全新的方式体会到了这一点——早先有一种规范，就是和陌生人甚至熟人，当然还有同事，谈论政治、宗教或金钱不是个好主意。如果你不总是把政治分歧互相摩擦，你更有可能与某人保持良好的工作关系。所以我实际上认为自由派和保守派应该一起工作。我认为如果部落之间完全分离，没有任何重叠或互动，国家会糟糕得多。而且我认为，如果你不总是专注于分歧，融合就容易得多。专注于你们共同喜欢的东西。我的意思是，这是我在科技界不幸疏远的一些人。我们有那么多共同点，比如对 Ruby 或其他东西的共同热爱，这难道不遗憾吗？

Yes, but not just that alone, I would say. It's more of the great divide that happened around 2020, where I think a lot of these political fault lines really opened up. And I mean, the shorthand in the US, I think, is were you woke or not? And clearly, I had a bunch of people that I thought I had good relations with for many years, who suddenly once that political fault line opened up, they were clearly just not anymore. And I think that's regrettable. And I think I've tried to put an effort into sort of both understanding where that comes from, in part because I've changed my mind on several of these topics over the years, and it was like, well, sometimes you just change your mind after time. Sometimes you change your mind after you get new information. There's all sorts of reasons and moments why you may change your mind, and we don't all change our mind on the same schedule or at all. So surrounding yourselves with, or at least interacting with, people that don't think like you on every topic, I think it's quite healthy. I mean, I occasionally discuss politics with my brother. I mean, we don't quite see eye to eye on all these topics, and sometimes the discussions can get a little heated, but then they'll also calm down and we realize, all right, okay, so we don't agree on this, and then we agree on a lot of other things. We appreciate a lot of the same things. Now, this was actually one of the things when we had our big blowup at Basecamp, I came to appreciate in a whole new way this earlier norm that talking politics or religion or money with strangers or even acquaintances, and certainly with colleagues, was just not a good idea. That you had a much higher likelihood of being able to carry a good working relationship with someone if you're not rubbing your political differences up against each other all the time. So I actually think liberals and conservatives should work together. I think the country would be much worse off if there's a complete separation between the tribes, and then there can't be any overlap or interaction. And I think that mingling is so much easier to do if you just don't focus on your differences all the time. Focus on what you like together. I mean, this is some of the people I've unfortunately drifted from in tech. We had so much in common, so much shared love for, say, Ruby or other things and, like, isn't that unfortunate?

Host

我希望生活在一个差异也很有趣、值得谈论的世界。

I would like to live in a world where the differences are very interesting to also talk about.

DHH

Yes.

DHH

Correct.

拥抱差异与文明对话 Embracing Differences and Civil Discourse

DHH

因为在差异中，首先，那才是有趣的东西所在。

Because in the differences, first of all, that's where interesting stuff is.

Host

是的。

Yes.

DHH

而且，这也是你成长的方式。如果一个社会拥抱差异，并将它们汇聚成一个可以讨论的社区，那正是社会健康的标志。

And, but also, it's how you grow. It's a sign of health in a society if the differences are embraced and they are brought together into community where you can talk about it.

Host

对我来说，一个很好的例证是，我看过一段威廉·F·巴克利节目的片段。我记得节目叫《火力线》之类的。他请了一位黑豹党成员上节目。很明显，这两位先生的政治立场大相径庭。但他们却能把对话进行下去，我记得我看了 25 分钟。巴克利就是不停地问：“那你怎么看这个？你怎么看那个？”然后黑豹党成员就像你在 70 年代预期的那样回答。语气里没什么和解的味道。我当时就想，哇，这太罕见了。如果今天你看到那个节目，那肯定是一场争吵，互相扔指责。巴克利能和他强烈反对的人进行这样的对话，还能探索他们立场中所有的智识层面，并让他们对这些层面发表看法，这标志着一个我十分想回去的失落时代。

One of the great illustrations of that for me was when I watched a clip from William F. Buckley's show. I think it was called Firing Line or something like that. And he had a member of the Black Panthers on his panel. It was quite obvious that those two gentlemen did not share a lot of politics. They were able to carry a conversation for, I think I watched it, 25 minutes. And Buckley's just asking, like, "So what do you think about this? What do you think about this?" And like, the Black Panther would reply as you would expect the Black Panther to reply in the '70s. Like, there was not a lot of sort of conciliatory tones. And I just thought, like, wow, that is so rare. If you had seen that show today, it would've been a shouting match. It would've been just accusations being hurled back and forth. The fact that Buckley was able to just have these conversations with people he vehemently disagreed with and still explore all the intellectual facets of their standpoints and letting them speak to those facets, talks of a lost era that I'd very much like to get back to.

DHH

我的意思是，我一直很喜欢和信任的朋友谈论政治，他们能以不涉及生死存亡的方式来讨论这些话题。

I mean, I always actually loved talking politics with friends that I trusted could talk about these topics in a way where it didn't have to be existential.

Host

但在当今时代，这种对话越来越难得了。所以你刚才说的这些很重要，需要经常说出来，提醒人们一个我们都应努力追求的理想：能够有分歧，并且去谈论它们。因为政治很有趣。我已经习惯说我现在讨厌政治，但我真正的意思是，不管现在这个体系如何，总有一些话题，我们都知道你能说的那些话，会把你归入蓝营或红营，这制造的是戏剧冲突，而不是对话。

But there's something about the current era where that's harder and harder to come by. And so what you just articulated is important to say regularly to remind people of an ideal we should all strive for, to be able to have differences and talk, and talk about them. 'Cause like, politics is fun. Like I've gotten used to saying I hate politics at this point, but what I really mean is whatever the system that's currently happening, where there's certain topics, and I just we know all the phrases you can say, that somehow put you in a bin of blue or red, and that creates drama versus a conversation.

DHH

比如我可以，如果我不关注互联网，我能想出很多无害的、分析世界局势的言论，但这些言论会引发所有人说：“哦，那家伙是个左派混蛋，”或者你知道的，一个右翼法西斯。

Like I could, I could think of a lot of harmless, if I wasn't paying attention to the internet, I could think of a bunch of harmless statements I could make analyzing the situation in the world that would trigger everybody to say, "Oh, that guy's a leftist piece of shit," or a you know, a right-wing fascist.

Yes.

DHH

然后客观地说，如果我不关注互联网，我会感到震惊，而且我确实震惊过。因为我没有深入关注互联网。但是，比如为了乌克兰战争，我做了大量阅读和准备，我震惊于我的某些言论会被解读成这样或那样，然后我因此遭到恶毒攻击。但那种攻击其实是在说：“嘿，你这个自以为可以自由行动和思考的混蛋，选个阵营，然后乖乖待在那个该死的阵营里。”

And then objectively, if I wasn't paying attention to the internet, I would be, and I have been shocked. 'Cause I haven't been paying attention to the internet deeply. Uh but reading and preparing a lot, for example, for, for the war in Ukraine, and I have been shocked how certain statements from me can come off as one way or the other, and I get viciously attacked for it. But what that attack is doing is it's saying, "Hey, you, asshole who thinks you could just move about and think freely, pick a bin and sit in that fucking bin."

Host

是的。

Yes.

穿上那件蓝衬衫或红衬衫。然后我意识到，事情反而会平静下来。如果你只是说“我是蓝营的人”或“我是红营的人”，系统就不会找你麻烦。

Put on that blue shirt or red shirt. And then actually what I've realized is things calm down. They don't, the system doesn't pick on you if you just say, "I'm a blue person," or, "I'm a red person."

你刚才说的只是观点。但从务实的角度看，我意识到除非我真的在乎某个问题，否则不要提政治。因为我意识到那是有代价的。他们会把你归入蓝营或红营。所以你必须更慎重地选择。

And you were just saying opinions. But then looking pragmatically at the situation, I have realized not to mention politics unless I really care about an issue. 'Cause I realize there's a cost. They'll, they'll put you in the blue bin or red bin. So you have to be more deliberate about choosing.

Host

是的。我也试着更注意这一点。当我确实选择发言时，通常是因为我深思熟虑过，我觉得“你知道吗？这值得。”我这么说，但我也经常发现自己有相反的想法，我关注某个人是因为他们的技术工作，然后我了解到了他们的政治立场。我经常想：“我真希望我不知道这个。”所以，如果我对别人有这种想法，然后又想“好吧，我偶尔会参与一些有争议的辩论”，那你必须接受那是有代价的。我确实认为你应该，而且更多人应该对此有所意识。再说一次，我们俩都在自相矛盾，但就像在说：“如果我们能在一个更自由、更开放的论坛上谈论政治，纯粹在智识上把想法翻来覆去，那该多好？”是的，那会很好。但在当前社交媒体等环境下，这可能吗？相当困难。

Yes. And I've tried to be more conscious of that, too. And then when I do choose to speak about it, it's usually because I have deliberated, and I think "You know what? This is worth it." Now, I say that, and then I also catch myself thinking the opposite all the time, that I follow some person or another for whatever their technical work, and then I learned or political standpoint. And I often do think, "I wish I didn't know that." So, it's hypocritical then to think that of other people and then go like, "Well, occasionally I will chime in on a debate that's controversial," and then you have to accept that there is a price to that. And I do think you should, and more people should, be somewhat conscious about that. Yes, it would. Is that currently possible in the climate of social media and so forth? It's quite difficult.

Now, some of it could simply also be a moment, and we could get past that moment.

DHH

也许有办法回到巴克利和黑豹党成员的那种对话，即使在社交媒体上，也能让那些讨论不演变成我们经历过的那些恶毒的群体围攻和取消文化闹剧，对吧？我实际上认为我们现在比五年前更接近那个状态了。比如，2020 年那种部落主义和部落对异见者施加的制裁，比 2025 年要严重得多。至少在科技领域是这样。我知道，如果你在学术界，我是说，试着去哈佛的社会学系，提一些右翼观点。我觉得那不会有好结果，而且你不会很快恢复过来。但在科技界和许多其他商业领域，至少现在有可能持有与 2020 年共识不符的观点。这是肯定的。

Maybe there is a way to get back to Buckley's conversation with the Black Panthers, even on social media, in a way where those discussions don't lead to the sort of just vicious mob cancellation nonsense drives that we've had, right? I actually think we're closer there now than we were five years ago. Like, the tribalism and the sanctions those tribes were able to exact on heretics in 2020 was way greater than it was in 2025. Certainly within tech. Now, I know if you're in academia, I mean, try going into the sociology department at I don't know, Harvard and mention some right-wing ideas. I don't think it's gonna go over very well, and I don't think you're gonna recover from it quickly. But in tech and in many other business domains at least, it is now possible to have an opinion that does not square with what the consensus was in 2020. That's for sure.

Host

嗯，Rails 社区有些风波。其他地方也有些风波。这一切……结果如何？都还好吗？

Well, there's some drama in the Rails community. There's some drama elsewhere. Is it, is it all... How did that turn out? Is that all okay?

DHH

从某种意义上说，每一轮风波都在缩小。所以就像我们，在 2020 年前后，和其他社区一样，经历了一场超级风波的高峰感染。然后你会看到，每次新的爆发，规模都更小。那些在意识形态上执着于那种斗争的人，只会越来越少。在这一点上，我认为 X 发生的最好的事情之一，就是 Bluesky 和 Mastodon 出现了。因为它们就像是一个蜜罐，吸引了当时 X 和 Twitter 上最恶毒的那些人，对吧？他们聚集在一个不断缩小的回音室里，经历了一轮又一轮的纯洁性循环，每次都缩小，直到剩下的人不多了。现在留在那里的人，已经眼不见心不烦了。这并不是说 X 是什么完美之地，或者那里没有偶尔的“行刑队”，或者没有……但确实少了。

In the sense that every single round of drama shrinks. So it's like we've, we had this peak infection of mega drama around the same time every other community had a peak infection of drama around 2020. And then you just see, like, every time there's a new flare up, it's less. And the kind of people who are ideologically wedded to sort of that struggle just shrinks and shrinks and shrinks. And at this point, one of the best things that happened for X, in my opinion, was that Bluesky and Mastodon came around. Because it kind of was like this honeypot for just the most vicious individuals on X and Twitter at the time, right? Who just congregated here in an ever-shrinking echo chamber that just went through purity cycle after purity cycle and shrank every single time to the point that there were just not a lot left. And the people who are left now there, they're just out of sight, out of mind. And that's not to say that X is some sort of perfect place, or there aren't firing squads occasionally there, or there aren't... But there's less of it.

Twitter与X体验 Twitter vs. X Experience

DHH

比如，每次我被拉进 Mastodon，或者没被拉进去，只是在远处围观，看个帖子，Bluesky 或者别的什么，我都会想：“哦，天哪，这就是以前的 Twitter 啊。天啊。”而现在你真的可以上 X，就像我这样，我 90% 的互动都是关于酷炫的 Linux 东西、酷炫的技术东西。“看，我发现了一台新电脑。你能看到它有多快吗？”我可以和其他同样对电脑兴奋的人进行这些对话。然后剩下 10% 的内容，好吧，我会对一些有点争议的事情插几句，我们可以稍微讨论一下。然后我们就回去继续对电脑兴奋。算法其实非常擅长找到你会觉得有趣的东西，或者——我不该这么说——让你投入的东西。有时候它让人愤怒，但它确实让人投入，因此，显示性偏好是大多数人都想要一个“为你推荐”页面。他们不想要只有关注流的页面。

Like, every single time I've been pulled into a Mastodon, or not been pulled in, just spectated from afar, thread, a Bluesky or whatever, it was like, "Oh, shit yeah, this is what Twitter used to be like. Jesus." And now you can actually be on X, as I am, and, like, 90% of my engagement is about cool Linux stuff, cool technology stuff. "Look, I discovered a new computer. Can you see how fast it is?" And I can have those conversations with other people who are just also excited about computers. And then there's, like, 10% of it left that's like, all right, I'll chime in on something that's a little controversial, and we can have a little discussion there. And then we can just go back to being excited about computers. The algorithm is actually very good at finding stuff that you will find interesting, or s- I shouldn't say that, engaging. Sometimes it's enraging, but it is engaging, and therefore, revealed preference is that most people want a For You page. They don't want just a following feed.

Host

不，但他们就是沉迷于“为你推荐”页面，对吧？

No, but they're addicted to the For You page, right?

DHH

他们……是的，是的。确实如此。

They... Yes, yes. That's true.

Host

我觉得一个 LLM，比如，如果我们有一个强大的 LLM 来做个性化的信息流，它会做得好得多。问题是如何大规模地提供这种服务是极其困难的。

I think an LLM, like, if we had a strong LLM doing the feed that's personalized, it would do much better. The problem is how to deliver that at scale is extremely difficult.

DHH

嗯，问题是奖励函数是投入度。一旦奖励函数是投入度……投入度就会追随原始本能，然后就这样了。但我也不是想这么消极。对我来说，我知道我们都有自己的个性化算法，有时候会有点过头。但我想说，平均来看，在过去 10 年里，这是 X 最好的状态。我这么说是因为我关心很多技术讨论、很多 Linux 讨论，以及很多这类东西。现在能够以这种方式发生，而不会不断被那些想把它拖入政治讨论的人闯入……我觉得这真的很棒。我发现 X 确实是一个找到这些东西的好地方。当然，它也是一个找到其他东西的地方，而且我们都有自己的算法，所以你不能对 X 是什么或不是什么做出任何断言。你的信息流可能和我的完全不同，也和别人的完全不同，对吧？

Well, the problem is the reward function is engagement. And as soon as the reward function is engagement- ... engagement's gonna follow base instincts, and here we go. But I also don't wanna be so negative. To me, and I get we all have our own personalized algorithms, and sometimes it gets a little much. But I would say, on average, over the last 10 years, this is the best X has ever been. And I say that as someone who cares about a lot of technology talk, a lot of Linux talk, and a lot of that. 拥抱有限性与Bryan的使命 Embracing finitude and Bryan's mission

Omarchy中的死亡提醒 Memento Mori in Omarchy

DHH

然后我们谈到的更大的点是生命的有限性。

And then the bigger point we're talking about is the finiteness of life.

Host

是的。

Yes.

DHH

在这一点上我同意你。

And I agree with you on this.

Host

这其实是一个功能，我把它做进了 Omarchy。所以“记住你会死”。如果你下拉 Omarchy 里的日历，你点击时钟就能打开它。它打开后，第一件有趣的事，我从网上拿来的。有个叫“年度进度”的账号，它每天更新，只显示“2026 年已完成 63%”。不知道你见过没有。它就这样填满。所以我把它做进了日历。所以现在它显示“2026 年已完成 63%”。但如果你双击它，它会问你：“你什么时候出生？你预期活多久？”默认是 90。所以我填了 1979。然后在“今年已完成多少”那行下面——

This was actually a feature, I built it into Omarchy. So memento mori. Remember death. If you pull down the calendar in Omarchy, you open it by clicking the clock. It opens up, and the first funny thing, I took it off the internet. There's this account called Year's Progress, and it, every day it updates, and it just shows 63% done with 2026. I don't know if you've seen that one. So it just fills up. So I built that into the calendar. So right now it says, "We're 63% done with 2026." But then if you double-click that, it'll ask you, "When were you born? How long do you expect to live?" And the default is 90. So I put in 1979. And then right below the line of, like, how long are we done with the year-

Host

哦，那太美了。

Oh, that's beautiful.

DHH

……有一行新的，显示你的人生已完成多少。我想我现在是 62%。如果你悬停在那里，它会显示“记住你会死”。

... is a new line that shows how long are you done with your life. I think I'm at 62%. I'm like, if you hover over it, it says, "Memento mori."

Host

那太美了。

That's beautiful.

DHH

相当多的人觉得这很病态，但这就是为什么它是个彩蛋。它不是暴露在外的。你得点几下才能到那里。我喜欢这个提醒。我喜欢提醒自己时间是有限的，我应该充分利用它。这也是有孩子的好处之一。你真的意识到那些陈词滥调都是真的。天哪，时间过得太快了。我记得我现在 13 岁的孩子一岁、两岁的样子。我当时想，“这就像昨天。”而它成为陈词滥调的原因是因为它是一种共同的体验。陈词滥调是好的。它们提醒你，你是人类体验的一部分，在你之前别人也经历过。

Fair number of people found it morbid, but this was why it is an Easter egg. It's not, like, exposed. You gotta click a few things to get there. I like the reminder. I like the reminder that time is finite, and I should make the most of it. And this is also one of the privileges of having children. You really realize that the cliches are all true. Oh my God, it goes so fast. And I remember my now 13-year-old being one, being two. I was like, "This was just yesterday." And the reason it's a cliche is because it's a shared experience. Cliches are good. They remind you that you are part of the human experience, and others have been here before you.

羊角面包之谜 The Croissant Mystery

Host

我得问一下，你提到了那个了不起的女人和好厨师。

I have to ask, you mentioned the amazing woman and great cook.

DHH

了不起。记住这一点很重要。不是“很棒”，是“了不起”。

Amazing. Very important to remember. Not wonderful, amazing.

Host

了不起。那么，有件事……你在推特上说了些关于牛角包的坏话。我看到了。所以丹麦的牛角包是不是更好？

Amazing. So there's this... You talked some shit about croissants or something. I saw this, this in a tweet. So is the Danish croissant somehow better?

DHH

实际上，丹麦的牛角包并不是特别好。在我看来，唯一能找到还不错的牛角包的地方，竟然是 7-Eleven，这对我来说很搞笑，因为在美国的 7-Eleven，至少芝加哥，是你去买思乐冰并且尽量避免被谋杀的地方。但在丹麦，7-Eleven 是一家非常高档、超级好的便利店，他们碰巧做出了不错的牛角包。而且我一直很困惑，我在美国住了整整 20 年，却从来没有吃过一个牛角包能比得上哥本哈根 7-Eleven 的水平。这看起来不太可能。

Actually, Danish croissants are not particularly good. In my opinion, the only place you can find a half-decent croissant, of all places, is 7-Eleven, which is hilarious to me because 7-Eleven in the US is the place where, at least in Chicago, you'd go to get a slushie and try to avoid getting murdered. But in Denmark, 7-Eleven is this very upscale, super nice convenience store, and they just happen to make decent croissants. And it always baffled me that I lived in the United States for 20 freaking years. I have yet to have a single croissant that's even at the level of a 7-Eleven croissant in Copenhagen. That seems improbable.

Host

你明白为什么了吗？

Have you understood why?

DHH

没有，我真的考虑过雇一个调查记者来查个水落石出，因为我听到的理论五花八门。是水的问题。是黄油的问题。是技巧的问题。是这个，那个，还有其他。我说，这些对我来说都说不通。如果是黄油，你不能直接进口吗？我好像在美国的一些超市看到过丹麦黄油，比如 Lurpak。所以那不可能是原因。是技巧吗？不，不可能是技巧。有很多法国人和比利时人，世界上最会做牛角包的人，住在美国。那到底为什么？然后我听到第二种说法——

No, I've legitimately considered hiring an investigative journalist to get to the bottom of this, because the number of theories I've heard have been all over the place. It's the water. It's the butter. It's the skill. It's the this, it's the that, it's the other thing. I'm like, none of it adds up to me. If it's the butter, can't you just import it? I thought I saw Danish butter in some supermarkets over here like Lurpak. So that can't be it. Can it be the skill? No, it can't be the skill. There are plenty of French people and Belgian people, the best croissant makers in the world, who live in the US. Like, what is the reason? And then I get the second thing, which is-

Host

……“不，不，你只是没去对地方。”

... "No, no, you just haven't gone to the right place."

DHH

我真的去过 15 家所谓的——

I've literally gone to 15 of quote unquote-

Host

……“对的地方”，结果所有牛角包都很烂。

... "the right place," and all the croissants were shit.

DHH

所以在我离开这个星球之前，我必须弄清楚为什么美国做不出一个像样的牛角包。而且我甚至不是在要求法国顶级牛角包、比利时顶级牛角包——

So before I leave the planet, I have to get to the bottom of why the United States cannot make a proper croissant. And I don't, I'm not even asking about peak French croissants, peak Belgian croissants-

Host

……那是世界上最好的牛角包。我只是在说哥本哈根 7-Eleven 的牛角包。这个要求过分吗？

... which are the best croissants in the world. I'm just asking about the 7-Eleven croissant from Copenhagen. Is that too much to ask for?

DHH

那真是个迷人的谜团。那么，放大来看，你最喜欢的……这让我想知道，你最喜欢的一餐是什么？比如，如果我给你一顿最后的晚餐，然后杀了你。

That's a fascinating mystery. What, zooming out, what's your, what's your favorite... This makes me want to know, what's your favorite meal? Like, if you, if I gave you a last meal and were to murder you after.

Host

那要看是午餐还是晚餐。如果是午餐，它——

It all depends whether it's lunch or it's dinner. If it's lunch, it-

DHH

就一顿饭。什么，什么……我觉得如果你要被谋杀，是午餐还是晚餐其实无所谓。

It's one meal. What's, what's... I feel like if you're getting murdered, it doesn't really matter if it's lunch or dinner.

Host

嗯，那取决于你什么时候被谋杀。

Well, depends on what time of day you're getting murdered.

DHH

哦，我明白了。

Oh, I see.

Host

如果是下午处决，我只能吃午餐。

If it's an afternoon execution, I only get lunch.

DHH

好吧。

Okay.

Host

如果我吃午餐，那就在路易斯安那，哥本哈根北部的博物馆。两天前我刚和杰米去过，我们经常去。博物馆很可爱。我们不是为了博物馆去的。我们是为了那个自助餐厅。这本身就听起来很疯狂。比如——

And if I get lunch, it's at Louisiana, the museum in north of Copenhagen. I was just there with Jamie two days ago, and we go there all the time. The museum is lovely. We don't go for the museum. We go for the cafeteria. That in itself sounds crazy. Like-

DHH

是的，确实。

Yeah, it does

Host

……你要开车半小时去一个自助餐厅。嗯，碰巧的是，那可能是我这辈子吃过的最好的午餐。那里的厨师好得令人难以置信，不仅食物很棒，更让我这个系统思考的大脑惊讶的是，我们在那里的时候，任何时候大概都有 200 人在等他们的食物。而我们的食物五分钟就送到了。他们的菜单很小，显然他们是大批量制作的，而且味道好得惊人。

... you're gonna, you're gonna drive half an hour to go to a cafeteria. Well, it just so happens to be, it is the best lunch I've probably ever had in my life. The chef there is just unbelievably good, and not only is the food amazing, what's even more amazing to my system thinking brain is that at any one time when we were up there, I think there were probably 200 people waiting for their food. And our food was delivered in five minutes. They have a small menu, and clearly they make it in bulk, and it's unbelievably delicious.

DHH

就只是博物馆里的一个自助餐厅？

Just a cafeteria at a museum?

Host

那是一个博物馆里的自助餐厅，碰巧有我一生中在任何地方吃过的最好的午餐。

It's a cafeteria at a museum that happens to have the best lunch I've ever had anywhere in my life.

DHH

我们说的是什么样的食物？什么样的午餐？

What kind of food are we talking about? What kind of lunch food?

Host

就是，北欧菜，所以它，它……有趣的是菜单，当我看到它时，我想，“你知道吗？如果我小时候看到这个菜单，我会说，‘我不在这里吃。’”就像，我不知道——

It's, like, Nordic cuisine, so it's, it's... A funny thing is the menu, when I look at it, I was like, "Do you know what? If I saw this menu- As a kid, I'd go like, 'I'm not eating here.'" It's like, I don't know-

DHH

这挺高级的。

This is pretty fancy

Host

……根茎类水果和——

... root free fruit and-

DHH

……和洋葱——

... and onions-

Host

……和各种健康的东西。听起来很恶心。然后你坐下来吃，天使都在歌唱。但更特别的是黄油。他们那里的打发的黄油，是世界上最好的黄油。

... and all sorts of healthy stuff. That sounds disgusting. And then you sit down and eat it, and the angels sing. But it's even more particular than that. It's the butter. The whipped butter that they have there, the best butter anywhere in the world.

DHH

我觉得那一定和牛角包之谜有关。

I think that has to be connected to the croissant mystery too.

Host

一定有某种联系。但真的——

There must be some connection. But literally-

路易斯安那的黄油 Butter at Louisiana

DHH

即使在巴黎，我也从没吃过比哥本哈根路易斯安那的黄油更美味的打发黄油。真的，我愿意开车 30 分钟去路易斯安那的食堂吃面包和黄油。

Even in Paris, I've never had whipped butter that tastes as delicious as the butter does at Louisiana in Copenhagen. Literally, I would drive 30 minutes to have bread and butter at the cafeteria in Louisiana.

Host

那你是怎么发现的？就是有一天你去了博物馆，然后……

Well, how'd you discover that? Just one day you went to the museum and...

DHH

我想我们就是去了博物馆……

I think we just went to the museum...

Host

然后你尝了一整道菜。

And you tried a whole dish.

DHH

……然后我们吃了午餐，我们就觉得……

...and we had the lunch, and we were like...

Host

这太奇怪了。

This is weird.

DHH

这简直可以和我这辈子去过的最好吃的餐厅相媲美。我去过 Noma 几次，尝过那里该尝的东西，而这里绝对不相上下。

This is rivaling the best tasting restaurants I've ever been to in my life, and I've gone to Noma a couple of times and tried what there is to try, and this is right up there.

最佳晚餐地点 Best dinner spot

Host

好吧。既然你提到了，那晚餐呢？是不是得想点别的？

All right. What about dinner, since you mentioned? Is it something different that you have to think...

DHH

嗯，我觉得哥本哈根有很多好选择，但我会选西班牙的一个地方。在马贝拉有个叫 Puente Romano 的地方，那是一家酒店，在海滩边有个海鲜餐厅，新鲜海鲜，好吃得难以置信。我记不清它叫什么名字了，但如果你查一下，Puente Romano 的海滩海鲜餐厅绝对棒极了。

Well, I think there's a lot of good options in Copenhagen, but actually I'll pick a place in Spain. So there is this place in Marbella called Puente Romano. It's a hotel, and down by the beach, they have this beach restaurant with fresh seafood. Unbelievably delicious. I'm trying to remember what it's called, but yeah, if you look it up, the beach seafood place at Puente Romano is incredible.

氛围在食物中的作用 Role of ambiance in food

Host

美食有多少取决于地点、和你在一起的人，对吧？或者你第一次去那里的体验？

How much of great food is, like, the place and the person you're with, right? Or the experience when you first are there?

DHH

我是说，氛围当然有帮助，但我觉得我的味蕾也……

I mean, the ambiance certainly helps, but I think my taste buds are also just...

Host

好吧，那……

Okay, that...

DHH

……对味道很敏感……

...in tune for...

Host

所以是货真价实的。

So it's legit.

DHH

……就是好吃。

...flavors... it is just good food.

Host

就是真的很好吃。

It's just really good food.

DHH

因为比如那个食堂，人很多。我不会说，天哪，这是个我想坐两小时的地方。但食物太好吃了。

Because for example, that cafeteria, there's a lot of people. I wouldn't rate it as, oh my God, this is just a place I want to sit for two hours. But the food is so delicious.

无偏采样 Sampling without bias

Host

这是我在周游全国时意识到的一件事，不带偏见地去品尝，就是去尝试。因为你可能会，我想你可能会感到惊喜。也许我会找到你的羊角面包。

That's one of the things I'm realizing in traveling across the country, to sample without bias, just try things. 'Cause you might, I guess you might just be surprised. Maybe I'll find your croissant.

人类未来 Future of humanity

Host

好了，我们谈过了死亡，谈过了食物。

All right, we've talked about mortality. Talked about food.

DHH

临终问题解决了。

Settled end of life.

Host

你觉得一千年后，人类的未来、人类文明的未来会怎样？你觉得我们能挺过去吗？

What do you think, a thousand years from now, what do you think is the future of humans, human civilization? You think we're gonna make it?

DHH

一千年后？天哪，我连 12 个月后都预测不了。

A thousand years from now? Man, I'm having trouble predicting 12 months from now.

Host

我知道。

I know.

DHH

一千年？

A thousand?

Host

你觉得我们有机会吗？

You think we got a shot?

DHH

哦，我要押注乐观。我要押注多行星物种。我要押注埃隆能把火箭送上火星，然后我们想办法弯曲时空，发现一些虫洞之类的。

Oh, I'm gonna bet on optimism. I'm gonna bet on a multi-planetary species. I'm gonna bet that Elon gets that rocket to Mars, and we figure out how to bend space-time and discover some wormholes or something.

人口再增长与黑镜 Repopulation and Black Mirror

Host

一旦我们做到了，你觉得地球上的人类会不会死一两次，然后在备份做好后重新繁衍？

Once we do, do you think all humans on Earth will die once or twice and be repopulated after we get a good backup going?

DHH

我是说，那其实是我最喜欢的《黑镜》剧集之一，就是他们被上传到 1980 年代某个海边小镇的那集。我当时就想，哇，这既是一个如此黑暗的时刻，却又在共同认识到 80 年代其实挺美好这一点上如此美丽。我对很多时刻没有太多怀旧，但我确实对 80 年代有怀旧。

I mean, that was actually one of my favorite episodes of Black Mirror, the one where they get uploaded to the 1980s sea town somewhere. And I just thought like, wow, this is both such a dark moment, yet also so beautiful in the shared recognition that, like, the '80s were actually kind of amazing. Like, I don't have a lot of nostalgia for many moments, but I do have nostalgia for the '80s.

Host

但是……

But...

DHH

而且他们说，无论你身处何时何地，你 12 岁或 14、15 岁时听的音乐，那会伴随你一生。比如，我在 80 年代才 10 岁。所以那是我童年早期，我对 80 年代仍然有一种难以置信的亲切感。所以如果我要被上传到云端……

And they say something about, like, whatever place and time you were in, the music you were listening to when you were 12 or 14, 15, something like that, that's what's gonna stick with you. Like, I got to be 10 in the '80s. So this was my early childhood, and I still have an unbelievably fond affiliation with the '80s. So if I'm gonna be uploaded to the sky...

Host

你想选 80 年代？

You want it to be the '80s?

DHH

……我选 80 年代。

...I'm doing the '80s.

Host

哇。我觉得我从来没听人这么说过。通常都是 70 年代或 90 年代。

Wow. I don't think I've actually ever heard anyone say that. Usually it's '70s or '90s.

DHH

哦，而且……

Oh, and the...

Host

因为 80 年代是个……

Because the '80s is a...

DHH

我真的很不喜欢 90 年代。我们能聊聊这个吗？

I really don't like the '90s. Can we talk about this?

Host

不，我说完了。没说完。

No, I'm done. Not done.

DHH

对我来说，那是虚无主义的重大转折点。音乐、流派、时尚……一切都从那种魅力、流行、乐观，甚至雅皮士什么的……转向了垃圾摇滚和涅槃乐队。尽管，我是说，我喜欢那些音乐，但我不喜欢那种伦理。我不喜欢那种道德。我不喜欢它的任何基础。它有一种失败主义……回想起来，如果我要重新插入矩阵，我会选 80 年代。

To me, this was the great turning point of nihilism. That both the music, the genre, the fashion... everything turned from, like, this glamour, the pop, the optimism, even the yuppies and everything... to grunge and Nirvana. And even though, I mean, I like the music, I don't like the ethics. I don't like the morals. I don't like any of the underpinnings to it. There was just such a defeatism to it... that in retrospect, I look back upon it, if I'm gonna get reinserted into the matrix, it's gonna be the '80s.

Host

那个时代充满了乐观和乐趣……

That was full of optimism and fun...

DHH

是的……

Yes...

Host

……和色彩。

...and colors.

DHH

是的。我记得我有那种橙色的裤子……上面有白点。那是 80 年代孩子能穿的普通东西。现在我再也没见过了。或者我是说，即使在 90 年代，一切都变成了西雅图灰。大倒退。

Yes. I remember having these orange pants... with, like, white dots on them. That was just a normal thing kids could wear in the '80s. I've never fucking seen that today. Or I mean, even in the '90s, it all turned just, like, Seattle gray. Great regression.

永恒轮回 Eternal recurrence

Host

所以尼采式的，你知道，永恒轮回的观念。如果你像《土拨鼠之日》那样永远活着，你想穿着橙色白点裤在 80 年代。

So the Nietzschean, you know, this idea of eternal recurrence. If you do a Groundhog Day and you live forever, you want it to be in the orange pants with the white dots in the '80s.

DHH

也许我会跳过橙色裤子，但是……

Maybe I'm gonna skip the orange pants, but...

Host

不……

No...

DHH

……我绝对选 80 年代。

...I'm definitely picking the '80s.

Host

You said it. All right, DHH. Thank you so much for everything you do. Thank you for being an inspiration to all of us, for building cool shit in the world, and thank you for talking today.

DHH

随时。再次感谢你，Lex，邀请我来。

Anytime. Thank you, Lex, again for having me.
